21	### Core Principles
22

24	- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only the current step file is in memory
25	- **Sequential Enforcement**: Sequence within the step files must be completed in order
26	- **State Tracking**: Document progress in output file frontmatter
27	- **Append-Only Building**: Build documents by appending content as directed

29	### Step Processing Rules

31	1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Always read the entire step file before taking any action

34	4. **CHECK CONTINUATION**: If the step has a menu with Continue, only proceed when user selects 'C'

38	### Critical Rules
39	
40	- 🛑 **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously

The skill we generate is not organized by version. If I generate a skill for cognee v0.5.0, when I detect drift in version 0.5.1 or 0.6.0, people using the skill for cognee 0.5.0 got a serious problem.
I propose an organization like:
|---- cognee
|---- 0.5.0 // version aware, user could specifiy what skill to install depending on the one he is using inside his project. He is compatible with skill.sh / https://www.npmjs.com/package/skills ( section Install a Skill -> Source Formats)
  |------ cognee
  --- 0.6.0
  |------- cognee

Go to party mode for different perspectives. Review the changes for any breaking changes, or missing impacts/bugs/regressions and ect... DO NOT HALLUCINATE

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of version information flow. Let me create a summary document.

## Summary: Version Information Flow in Skill Forge

Based on my analysis of the Skill Forge module, here's how version information flows through the system:

---

### **1. Entry Points: Where Version Information Enters**

#### **Source Detection (analyze-source & brief-skill workflows)**
Version enters the system early in `step-02-analyze-target.md` during the brief-skill workflow through **auto-detection**:

- **JavaScript/TypeScript:** Reads `package.json` `"version"` field
- **Python:** Checks `pyproject.toml` `[project] version` → `__init__.py` `__version__` → `_version.py` → `setup.py` `version=` → git tags
- **Rust:** Reads `Cargo.toml` `[package] version` (or workspace root if delegated) → git tags
- **Go:** Extracts from `go.mod` or git tags

**Location:** `/src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md` (lines 162-176)
**Location:** `/src/workflows/analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md` (lines 37-48)

---

### **2. Capture: skill-brief.yaml**

Version is captured as a **required field** in the skill brief YAML:

```yaml
name: {skill-name}
version: {detected-version or "1.0.0"}  # Semantic version (X.Y.Z or X.Y.Z-prerelease)
source_repo: {github-url-or-local-path}
language: {detected-language}
scope: {...}
```

**Key behaviors:**
- Pre-release tags are **preserved as-is** (e.g., `1.0.0-beta.0`, `2.0.0-rc.1`)
- Default fallback is `"1.0.0"` if detection fails or returns non-semver
- Version is **semver-aware** — comparisons respect pre-release ordering

**Locations:**
- `/src/workflows/brief-skill/data/skill-brief-schema.md` (lines 8, 33-48)
- `/src/workflows/analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md` (lines 8, 33-48)

---

### **3. Validation: create-skill step-01 (Load Brief)**

When create-skill begins, it validates the skill-brief.yaml including the version field:

**Location:** `/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-brief.md` (lines 88-94)

The version field is **required** in the brief validation. If missing, the workflow halts with an error.

---

### **4. Reconciliation: create-skill step-03 (Extract)**

During extraction, **version reconciliation** occurs to detect if source version has changed since brief creation:

**Location:** `/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` (line 75)

References: `/src/workflows/create-skill/data/source-resolution-protocols.md` — **Version Reconciliation protocol**

**Behavior:**
- If source version has changed since brief: warns user and uses current source version
- Ensures extraction is performed against the correct source version
- Critical for maintaining provenance accuracy

---

### **5. Tracking: Provenance System**

Version information is embedded in multiple provenance artifacts:

#### **provenance-map.json**
```json
{
  "skill_name": "{name}",
  "source_repo": "{url}",
  "source_commit": "{hash}",        // Commit hash pinned to version
  "generated_at": "{ISO-8601}",
  "entries": [
    {
      "export_name": "createServer",
      "source_file": "src/server.ts",
      "source_line": 23,
      "confidence": "T1",
      "extraction_method": "ast-grep"
    }
  ]
}
```

**Location:** `/src/knowledge/provenance-tracking.md` (lines 31-67)

Each extraction entry is version-bound — the provenance map tracks which version of the source code a claim was verified against.

#### **evidence-report.md**
Records:
- Source commit hash
- Generation date
- Tool versions used for extraction
- Extraction confidence breakdown

**Location:** `/src/knowledge/provenance-tracking.md` (lines 78-102)

---

### **6. Output: Final Skill Package**

Version information appears in three places in the final output:

#### **SKILL.md (Section 1: Overview)**
```markdown
## Overview

{name} v{version} — What the library does...
**Source:** {source_repo} @ {branch}
**Extracted:** {date}
**Tier:** {forge_tier}
**Exports:** {count}
```

**Location:** `/src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` (lines 29-40)

#### **context-snippet.md (Token-efficient discovery)**
```markdown
[{name} v{version}]|root: skills/{name}/
|IMPORTANT: {name} v{version} — read SKILL.md before writing {name} code. Do NOT rely on training data.
|quick-start:{SKILL.md#quick-start}
|api: {top exports}
|key-types:{SKILL.md#key-types}
|gotchas: {2-3 critical pitfalls}
```

**Key point:** Version appears in line 1 to signal training data staleness to agents

**Location:** `/src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` (lines 161-185)

#### **metadata.json (Machine-readable export)**
```json
{
  "name": "{skill-name}",
  "version": "{source-version}",
  "source_repo": "{github-url}",
  "source_commit": "{commit-hash}",
  "generation_date": "{ISO-8601}",
  "tool_versions": {
    "ast_grep": "{version-or-null}",
    "qmd": "{version-or-null}",
    "skf": "{skf_version}"
  },
  "confidence_distribution": {...},
  "stats": {
    "exports_documented": 0,
    "exports_public_api": 0,
    "total_coverage": 0.0
  }
}
```

**Location:** `/src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` (lines 188-235)

---

### **7. Update Cycle: update-skill Workflow**

During updates, version is **re-reconciled**:

**Location:** `/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md`

The update workflow:
1. Loads existing provenance-map.json (which has version info)
2. Re-detects current source version
3. If version changed: marks affected exports for re-extraction
4. Preserves unchanged provenance entries with original timestamps
5. Updates evidence-report.md with new extraction details

**Critical:** Version changes trigger selective re-extraction only for affected claims, not the entire skill.

---

### **8. Audit & Drift Detection: audit-skill Workflow**

Version is used to validate that drift detection is performed against correct source:

**Location:** `/src/knowledge/provenance-tracking.md` (lines 110-129)

The audit process:
1. Loads provenance-map.json with version info
2. Re-indexes current source to same version
3. Compares current state against recorded provenance
4. Produces drift-report.md showing severity-classified changes

---

### **9. Configuration: No Global Version Pin**

Neither `forge-tier.yaml` nor `preferences.yaml` contain version pinning:

- **forge-tier.yaml:** Tracks tool availability and CCC index state only
- **preferences.yaml:** Tracks tier override and passive context injection preference

**Design choice:** Version is source-bound (detected per-brief), not globally configured. This allows different skills to compile against different source versions simultaneously.

---

### **10. Summary: The Complete Pipeline**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ENTRY: analyze-source or brief-skill workflow                   │
│ → Auto-detect source version from package manifest or git tag   │
└────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                     │
                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ CAPTURE: skill-brief.yaml                                       │
│ ├─ name: {skill-name}                                           │
│ ├─ version: {detected-version or 1.0.0}  [REQUIRED]            │
│ └─ source_repo: {github-url-or-path}                            │
└────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                     │
                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ VALIDATION: create-skill step-01                                │
│ → Ensure version field is present and valid semver             │
└────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                     │
                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ RECONCILIATION: create-skill step-03                            │
│ → Re-detect source version                                      │
│ → Warn if changed since brief creation                          │
│ → Use current version for extraction                            │
└────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                     │
                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ EXTRACTION & PROVENANCE                                         │
│ ├─ Every claim citation includes source commit hash            │
│ ├─ provenance-map.json stores source_commit (version-bound)    │
│ ├─ evidence-report.md records extraction date & tool versions  │
│ └─ Confidence tier (T1/T1-low) per claim                       │
└────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                     │
                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ OUTPUT: Final skill package                                     │
│ ├─ SKILL.md § Overview: v{version}                              │
│ ├─ context-snippet.md line 1: [{name} v{version}]              │
│ ├─ metadata.json: version, source_commit, tool_versions        │
│ └─ provenance-map.json: version-bound export claims            │
└────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                     │
                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ UPDATE CYCLE: update-skill or audit-skill                       │
│ → Re-detect source version                                      │
│ → Selectively re-extract changed exports                        │
│ → Preserve unchanged provenance timestamps                      │
│ → Generate drift-report.md with version-aware diffing          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### **Key Design Principles**

1. **Version is source-bound, not global** — Each skill compiles against detected source version
2. **Pre-release support** — Pre-release tags preserved; semver-aware comparison used
3. **Provenance is version-pinned** — Every claim traces to specific source version via commit hash
4. **Reconciliation on extraction** — If source version changes, extraction warns and uses current version
5. **Selective updates** — Version changes trigger only affected exports for re-extraction, preserving provenance timestamps
6. **Agent-visible version** — Version appears in context-snippet.md line 1 to signal training data staleness
7. **No global version pin** — forge-tier.yaml and preferences.yaml do NOT pin versions; version is per-brief

---

This design ensures that skills remain accurate across source updates, versions are transparent to agents, and provenance claims are always version-bound and auditable.

## Summary: Skill Forge Compatibility Requirements and External Platform Support

Based on a complete review of the Skill Forge module documentation, here's what I found:

### **1. External Skill Distribution Formats and Platforms**

Skill Forge supports three primary IDE/platform targets for skill distribution:

| Platform | Config IDE Value | Target File | Root Path Prefix | Default | 
|----------|---|---|---|---|
| **Claude Code** | `claude-code` | `CLAUDE.md` | `.claude/skills/` | — |
| **Cursor** | `cursor` | `.cursorrules` | `.cursor/skills/` | — |
| **GitHub Copilot** | `github-copilot` | `AGENTS.md` | `.agents/skills/` | ✓ (fallback) |

**Key Details:**
- Platforms are configured via an `ides` list in `config.yaml` (referenced by step-01 of export-skill)
- If no valid platforms are configured, the workflow defaults to `copilot` (publishing to `.agents/skills/`)
- Users can override with `--platform` flag to export to a single target
- When multiple platforms are configured, the workflow processes all target files sequentially with root path rewriting per platform

### **2. Skill Distribution Format: agentskills.io Specification**

Skill Forge produces skills that comply with **agentskills.io**, which is not NPM-based or a separate registry like npmjs. Instead:

**Core Principles:**
- Progressive disclosure model with three loading phases: Discovery (~50-100 tokens) → Activation (<500 lines) → Execution (on-demand resources)
- Deterministic procedural instructions optimized for agent consumption
- Just-in-time resource loading to keep context lean

**Required Skill Structure:**

```
{skill-name}/
├── SKILL.md              # Main skill document with YAML frontmatter
├── metadata.json         # Machine-readable metadata
├── context-snippet.md    # Compressed reference (~80-120 tokens)
├── references/           # Optional: Progressive disclosure files (one level deep)
├── scripts/              # Optional: Executable automation (one level deep)
└── assets/               # Optional: Templates, schemas, configs (one level deep)
```

**Frontmatter Requirements in SKILL.md:**
- `name`: 1-64 chars, lowercase + hyphens, must match directory name (prefer gerund form: `processing-pdfs`)
- `description`: 1-1024 chars, third-person voice, trigger-optimized for agent matching, include negative triggers ("NOT for...")

### **3. Managed Section Injection Mechanism**

Skills are injected into agent context via a managed HTML comment block following **four-case logic (ADR-J)**:

**Case 1: Create** — File doesn't exist
```markdown
<!-- SKF:BEGIN updated:{date} -->
[SKF Skills]|{n} skills|{m} stack
|IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data.
|{skill-snippet-1}
<!-- SKF:END -->
```

**Case 2: Append** — File exists, no markers (preserves all existing content)

**Case 3: Regenerate** — File contains markers (replaces content between markers only)

**Case 4: Halt** — Malformed markers (`<!-- SKF:BEGIN` without `<!-- SKF:END -->`) → error

**Critical Guard:** Only skills that have been explicitly exported via `export-skill` (tracked in `.export-manifest.json`) appear in the managed section — draft skills created by `create-skill` or `update-skill` are excluded (ADR-K publishing gate).

### **4. Context Snippet Format (Vercel-Aligned Indexed Format)**

Generated for passive context injection with platform-aware root paths:

**Single Skill Template:**
```
[{skill-name} v{version}]|root: {platform_root}{skill-name}/
|IMPORTANT: {skill-name} v{version} — read SKILL.md before writing code. Do NOT rely on training data.
|quick-start:{SKILL.md#quick-start}
|api: {export-1}(), {export-2}(), ...
|key-types:{SKILL.md#key-types} — {inline summary ~10 words}
|gotchas: {pitfall-1}, {pitfall-2}
```

**Stack Skill Template:**
```
[{project}-stack v{version}]|root: {platform_root}{project}-stack/
|IMPORTANT: {project}-stack — read SKILL.md before writing integration code.
|stack: {dep-1}@{v1}, {dep-2}@{v2}
|integrations: {pattern-1}, {pattern-2}
|gotchas: {pitfall-1}, {pitfall-2}
```

**Key Features:**
- Token budget target: ~80-120 tokens per skill
- Section anchors verified against actual SKILL.md headings
- Root paths automatically rewritten per target platform (step-04)
- Gotchas derived from breaking changes, async requirements, version-specific behavior
- T1-now content only (no temporal annotations)

### **5. Export Workflow (EX) Pipeline**

Six sequential steps with deterministic file operations:

| Step | Purpose | Auto-Proceed |
|------|---------|---|
| **Step-01: Load Skill** | Validate agentskills.io compliance, parse export flags | User confirms ('C') |
| **Step-02: Package** | Validate package structure and metadata | Yes |
| **Step-03: Generate Snippet** | Create context-snippet.md in Vercel format | Yes |
| **Step-04: Update Context** | Inject into CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md/.cursorrules | User confirms ('C') or dry-run |
| **Step-05: Token Report** | Calculate token costs for all artifacts | Yes |
| **Step-06: Summary** | Final report with distribution instructions | — (terminal step) |

**Critical Rules:**
- Passive context can be disabled (`passive_context: false` in preferences.yaml) to skip snippet/context updates
- Dry-run mode (`--dry-run`) previews changes without writing
- All writes include verification: markers present, external content preserved

### **6. Directory Structure Conventions**

**Required Structure (agentskills.io):**
- Maximum one level deep for subdirectories (strictly enforced)
- No deeply nested hierarchies or parallel structures
- All referenced files resolved via relative paths (one level only)

**Skills Output Directory Layout:**
```
{skills_output_folder}/
├── skill-1/
│   ├── SKILL.md
│   ├── metadata.json
│   ├── context-snippet.md
│   ├── references/
│   ├── scripts/
│   └── assets/
├── skill-2/
├── .export-manifest.json      ← Tracks exported skills per ADR-K
└── ...
```

**Project Context Files (platform-dependent):**
- `.claude/skills/` (Claude Code)
- `.cursor/skills/` (Cursor)
- `.agents/skills/` (GitHub Copilot)

### **7. Source Authority Classifications**

Skills are labeled with one of three authority types affecting distribution instructions:

- **`official`**: Library maintainer-produced → can publish to agentskills.io via `npx skills publish`
- **`internal`**: Project-generated → shipped with source, auto-available to team
- **`community`**: Generated from third-party code → local use only, not published

### **Key Compatibility Takeaways**

1. **No external registry dependency**: Skills are distributed as markdown bundles injected into project context files — not published to NPM or a centralized server
2. **Platform-aware distribution**: Root paths automatically adapt to target IDE (`.claude/`, `.cursor/`, `.agents/`)
3. **Three platforms supported**: Claude Code, Cursor, GitHub Copilot (with Copilot as fallback)
4. **Strict structure enforcement**: Maximum one-level directory nesting, no exceptions
5. **Progressive disclosure**: Skills optimize for low-token discovery with on-demand resource loading
6. **Publishing gate**: Only explicitly exported skills (via `export-skill` workflow) appear in managed sections — drafts are excluded by design

## COMPREHENSIVE ANALYSIS: Skill Output Organization in Skill Forge

Based on my thorough examination of the Skill Forge module at `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/`, here are the answers to your five questions:

### 1. WHERE `skills_output_folder` IS CONFIGURED AND HOW IT'S RESOLVED

**Configuration Location:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/module.yaml`

```yaml
skills_output_folder:
  prompt: "Where should generated skills be saved?"
  default: "skills"
  result: "{project-root}/{value}"
```

**Resolution:**
- **Default:** `skills/` directory (relative to project root)
- **Resolution pattern:** `{project-root}/{user-configured-value}`
- **Example:** If user sets `skills_output_folder: "my-skills"`, the resolved path is `{project-root}/my-skills/`
- **Static key:** The same `skills_output_folder` is read by ALL workflows (create-skill, quick-skill, create-stack-skill, export-skill, update-skill, audit-skill)
- **Related:** `forge_data_folder` is configured similarly (default: `forge-data/`, resolved as `{project-root}/{value}`)

---

### 2. HOW EXPORT-SKILL, CREATE-SKILL, CREATE-STACK-SKILL, QUICK-SKILL WRITE OUTPUT

All workflows follow the same output directory structure but with slightly different completeness levels:

#### **CREATE-SKILL OUTPUT (Most Complete)**
**Location:** `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/` + `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/`

**Step 7 (Generate Artifacts) writes:**

**Deliverables to `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/`:**
1. `SKILL.md` — Complete agentskills.io-compliant skill document with all sections
2. `context-snippet.md` — Vercel-aligned indexed 2-line format
3. `metadata.json` — Machine-readable metadata
4. `references/` directory with:
   - One `.md` file per function group/type (progressive disclosure)
   - `integrations/` subdirectory (for stack skills)
5. `scripts/` directory (conditional — only if scripts detected in source)
6. `assets/` directory (conditional — only if assets detected in source)

**Workspace artifacts to `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/`:**
1. `provenance-map.json` — Per-claim source mapping with AST bindings
2. `evidence-report.md` — Extraction summary and validation results
3. `extraction-rules.yaml` — Language and ast-grep schema used (for reproducibility)

#### **QUICK-SKILL OUTPUT (Minimal)**
**Location:** `{skills_output_folder}/{repo-name}/`

**Step 6 (Write) writes only 3 files:**
1. `SKILL.md` — Best-effort skill from rapid extraction
2. `context-snippet.md` — Generated snippet
3. `metadata.json` — Minimal metadata

**Note:** Quick-skill does NOT generate `references/`, `scripts/`, `assets/`, or `forge_data_folder` artifacts.

#### **CREATE-STACK-SKILL OUTPUT**
**Location:** `{skills_output_folder}/{project-name}-stack/` + `{forge_data_folder}/{project-name}-stack/`

**Step 7 (Generate Output) writes:**

**Deliverables:**
1. `SKILL.md` — Stack skill document
2. `context-snippet.md` — Stack-specific snippet format
3. `metadata.json` — Stack-specific metadata
4. `references/{library-name}.md` — One file per detected library
5. `references/integrations/{libA}-{libB}.md` — One file per detected integration pair

**Workspace artifacts:**
1. `provenance-map.json` — Libraries and integration provenance
2. `evidence-report.md` — Extraction summary

#### **EXPORT-SKILL OUTPUT**
Export-skill does NOT create new skills. It:
- **Reads** existing skills from `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/`
- **Generates/updates** `context-snippet.md` (step 3)
- **Optionally updates** platform-specific context files:
  - `CLAUDE.md` (for Claude platform)
  - `.cursorrules` (for Cursor platform)
  - `AGENTS.md` (for GitHub Copilot platform)
- **Updates** `.export-manifest.json` in `{skills_output_folder}/` to track which skills have been exported to which platforms

---

### 3. IS THERE ANY EXISTING VERSION-AWARENESS IN OUTPUT PATHS?

**NO direct version-awareness in directory paths.** However, version is tracked within artifacts:

**Where Version Appears:**
1. **In `metadata.json`:** 
   - `"version"` field (string, semver format preferred)
   - `"generation_date"` timestamp
   - For stack skills: version determined by highest constituent library version, or 1.0.0 if none detected

2. **In `context-snippet.md`:**
   - Line 1 includes version: `[{skill-name} v{version}]|root: ...`
   - Line 2 (IMPORTANT): includes version: `|IMPORTANT: {skill-name} v{version} — read SKILL.md...`

3. **In `provenance-map.json`:**
   - `"last_update"` timestamp
   - Per-export `source_line` (file:line references enable fine-grained change detection)
   - Conditional `file_entries` for scripts/assets with `content_hash` for drift detection

**Version Resolution (create-skill only):**
- **Step 3 (Extract)** detects source version from:
  - `package.json` (`"version"` field)
  - `pyproject.toml` or `setup.py` or `__init__.py` (Python)
  - `Cargo.toml` (Rust)
  - `go.mod` (Go)
- **Monorepo handling:** For JavaScript workspaces, priority is: targeted package with `bin` field → root workspace → fall back to brief default
- **Conflict handling:** If source version differs from brief version, warns user and uses source version

**No version folder nesting:** Skills are stored flat within their skill-name directory, NOT in version-specific subdirectories (e.g., NOT `skills/express-skill/v4.18.0/SKILL.md`).

---

### 4. WHAT DOES THE FINAL OUTPUT DIRECTORY STRUCTURE LOOK LIKE?

#### **Complete Single-Skill Output**
```
{skills_output_folder}/
└── {skill-name}/
    ├── SKILL.md                          [Required, agentskills.io-compliant]
    ├── context-snippet.md                [Required, 2-line indexed format]
    ├── metadata.json                     [Required, machine-readable metadata]
    ├── references/                       [Optional, progressive disclosure]
    │   ├── {function-group-1}.md
    │   ├── {function-group-2}.md
    │   └── {integration-type}.md
    ├── scripts/                          [Optional, only if source scripts detected]
    │   ├── {script-1}.sh
    │   ├── {script-2}.py
    │   └── [MANUAL]/                     [User-authored scripts preserved]
    └── assets/                           [Optional, only if source assets detected]
        ├── {template}.json
        ├── {schema}.yaml
        └── [MANUAL]/                     [User-authored assets preserved]

{forge_data_folder}/
└── {skill-name}/
    ├── provenance-map.json               [AST bindings and confidence tiers]
    ├── evidence-report.md                [Extraction summary and audit trail]
    └── extraction-rules.yaml             [ast-grep schema for reproducibility]
```

#### **Stack Skill Output**

{skills_output_folder}/

    ├── SKILL.md
    ├── context-snippet.md
    ├── metadata.json
    └── references/
        ├── {library-1}.md
        ├── {library-2}.md
        ├── {library-N}.md
        └── integrations/
            ├── {libA}-{libB}.md
            └── {libA}-{libC}.md

{forge_data_folder}/
└── {project-name}-stack/

    └── evidence-report.md
```

#### **Export Output (Platform-Specific Context)**
```
{project-root}/
├── CLAUDE.md                             [If claude platform selected]
├── .cursorrules                          [If cursor platform selected]
├── AGENTS.md                             [If copilot platform selected]
└── {skills_output_folder}/
    ├── .export-manifest.json             [Tracks exported skills per platform]
    └── {skill-name}/
        └── context-snippet.md            [Updated with platform-specific root path]
```

#### **Platform-Specific Root Paths in Snippets**
- **Claude:** `.claude/skills/{skill-name}/`
- **Cursor:** `.cursor/skills/{skill-name}/`
- **Copilot (GitHub):** `.agents/skills/{skill-name}/`

---

### 5. HOW UPDATE-SKILL DETECTS DRIFT AND REFERENCES EXISTING SKILLS

#### **Drift Detection Mechanism (Steps 1-2)**

**Step 1 (Initialize Update):**
1. User provides skill name or path
2. Workflow loads:
   - `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/SKILL.md` (required)
   - `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/metadata.json` (required) — extracts `name`, `skill_type`, `version`, `generation_date`, `source_root`
   - `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/provenance-map.json` (optional but preferred) — contains per-export source mappings, confidence tiers, extraction timestamps
   - `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml` — determines analysis capability (Quick/Forge/Forge+/Deep)
   - Optionally: test report (for gap-driven mode via `--from-test-report`)

3. **Degrades gracefully if provenance unavailable:**
   - If no provenance-map.json found, enters "degraded mode" → performs full re-extraction instead of targeted diffing
   - Triggers warning: "Without a provenance map, update-skill cannot perform targeted change detection"

**Step 2 (Detect Changes):**

**Normal mode (provenance available):**
1. **Scans current source state** — builds inventory of files, sizes, timestamps
2. **Compares against provenance map** across 4 categories:

   **Category A — File-level changes:**
   - DELETED: Files in provenance but missing from source
   - ADDED: Files in source but not in provenance
   - MODIFIED: Files with different timestamps/sizes
   - MOVED: Same content at different paths

   **Category B — Export-level changes (per modified file only):**
   - DELETED_EXPORT: In provenance, not in source
   - NEW_EXPORT: In source, not in provenance
   - MODIFIED_EXPORT: Signatures changed
   - MOVED_EXPORT: Different line numbers, same content

   **Category C — Rename detection:**
   - Content similarity > 80% → classified as RENAMED (not deleted+added)
   - By tier: Quick uses file size ratio (±20%) + export name overlap (>70%); Forge+ uses ast-grep signature comparison; Deep uses CCC semantic similarity

   **Category D — Script/asset file changes:**
   - Computes SHA-256 content hashes from `provenance-map.json` file_entries
   - Detects MODIFIED, DELETED, NEW files
   - Preserves files in `scripts/[MANUAL]/` and `assets/[MANUAL]/`

3. **Builds unified change manifest** with structured entries including file:line references

**No-change shortcut (Section 4):**
- If zero changes detected → skips steps 3-6, jumps directly to final report
- Message: "No changes detected. Source code matches provenance map exactly."

#### **Gap-Driven Mode (`--from-test-report`)**

Instead of source drift detection:
1. Loads test report at `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/test-report-{skill-name}.md`
2. Extracts findings from:
   - **Gap Report section:** Severity, category, description
   - **Coverage Analysis section:** Per-export documented/missing/mismatch status
3. Translates gaps into change manifest:
   - Critical missing export → NEW_EXPORT
   - High signature mismatch → MODIFIED_EXPORT
   - Medium stale documentation → MODIFIED_EXPORT
4. Proceeds with re-extraction targeted at those specific gaps

#### **Update Workflow Output (Steps 3-6)**

**Step 3 (Re-extract):** Re-extracts only affected files/exports using current tier tools

**Step 4 (Merge):**
- Merges fresh extractions with existing SKILL.md
- Preserves `[MANUAL]` sections (user-authored content marked with `<!-- [MANUAL:*] -->` comments)
- Handles merge conflicts per merge-conflict-rules.md

**Step 5 (Validate):** Validates agentskills.io spec compliance

**Step 6 (Write):**
- Writes updated `SKILL.md` to `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/SKILL.md`
- Writes updated `metadata.json` with:
  - Version: increments patch if source version unchanged, uses source version if changed
  - `generation_date`: current timestamp
  - `exports`: updated array
  - `stats`: updated coverage percentages
  - `confidence_distribution`: updated tier counts
- Writes updated `provenance-map.json` to `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/provenance-map.json` with:
  - Per-export updates: changed entries replaced, unchanged entries preserve timestamps
  - Deleted exports: removed from map
  - New exports: added with fresh provenance and current tier
  - `last_update`: current date
  - `update_type`: "incremental" (normal) or "full" (degraded mode)
- Appends to `evidence-report.md` with update operation record
- For stack skills: updates `references/{library}.md` and `references/integrations/{pair}.md`

**Warning for single skills if exports changed:**
- ⚠️ "context-snippet.md was NOT updated — exports have changed. Run **[EX] Export Skill** to regenerate the context snippet and update CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md."

---

## SUMMARY TABLE: Output Paths by Workflow

| Workflow | Output Location | Files Written | Version Handling |
|----------|-----------------|---------------|------------------|
| **create-skill** | `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/` + `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/` | SKILL.md, metadata.json, context-snippet.md, references/*, scripts/*, assets/*, + 3 workspace artifacts | Detects from source (pkg.json, pyproject.toml, etc.); updates metadata.json |
| **quick-skill** | `{skills_output_folder}/{repo-name}/` | SKILL.md, metadata.json, context-snippet.md | No source version detection; defaults to 1.0.0 or brief version |
| **create-stack-skill** | `{skills_output_folder}/{project}-stack/` + `{forge_data_folder}/{project}-stack}/` | SKILL.md, metadata.json, context-snippet.md, references/*.md, references/integrations/*.md, + 2 workspace artifacts | Highest version from constituent skills, or 1.0.0 |
| **export-skill** | `CLAUDE.md` / `.cursorrules` / `AGENTS.md` + `.export-manifest.json` | Managed section with `<!-- SKF:BEGIN/END -->` markers | Reads version from existing metadata.json; includes v{version} in context-snippet.md |
| **update-skill** | `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/` + `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/` | Updated SKILL.md, metadata.json, provenance-map.json, evidence-report.md, references/* | Preserves or increments based on source version detection; `generation_date` updated |

---

## KEY ARCHITECTURAL INSIGHTS

1. **No version-based subdirectories:** All skills are stored flat by name, not nested by version. Versioning is metadata-driven within artifacts.

2. **Platform-aware paths:** Export-skill detects target platforms (Claude/Cursor/Copilot) from config.yaml and rewrites root paths in context snippets accordingly.

3. **Provenance-driven updates:** Update-skill relies on provenance-map.json for surgical change detection. Without it, falls back to full re-extraction (degraded mode).

4. **Two-tier artifact system:** 
   - **Deliverables** in `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/` are agent-facing (SKILL.md, snippets, references)
   - **Workspace artifacts** in `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/` are developer-facing (provenance, evidence, rules)

5. **Manual section preservation:** Update-skill preserves user-authored `[MANUAL]` sections through edits, ensuring user annotations survive automated updates.

6. **Platform-specific context injection:** Export-skill rebuilds the complete skill index from an export manifest (ADR-K) to prevent publishing un-exported skills, and rewrites root paths per platform during context file generation.
```

Now I have a thorough understanding of the codebase. Let me compile the complete structural design plan.

Based on my extensive exploration of the codebase, here is the complete version-aware directory structure design.

---

## Version-Aware Directory Structure Design for Skill Forge

### 1. Current State Summary

**skills_output_folder (flat):**
```
{skills_output_folder}/
├── .export-manifest.json
├── cognee/
│   ├── SKILL.md
│   ├── metadata.json
│   ├── context-snippet.md
│   ├── references/
│   ├── scripts/
│   └── assets/
└── cognee-stack/
    ├── SKILL.md
    ���── metadata.json
    ├── context-snippet.md
    └── references/
        └── integrations/
```

**forge_data_folder (flat):**
```
{forge_data_folder}/
├── cognee/
│   ├── provenance-map.json
│   ├── evidence-report.md
│   ├── extraction-rules.yaml
│   ├── skill-brief.yaml
│   └── test-report-cognee.md
└── cognee-stack/
    ├── provenance-map.json
    └── evidence-report.md
```

Path references currently appearing in the system:
- **Snippet root:** `{platform_root}{skill-name}/` (e.g., `.claude/skills/cognee/`)
- **Export manifest:** `{skills_output_folder}/.export-manifest.json`
- **Skill discovery glob:** `{skills_output_folder}/*/SKILL.md`
- **Snippet scan glob:** `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md`
- **Forge data lookup:** `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/provenance-map.json`

---

### 2. Design Decisions for Each Question

#### Question 1: Canonical Path Pattern for skills_output_folder

**Proposed pattern:** `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`

**Rationale for the inner skill-name repetition:** The agentskills.io spec (line 38 of `agentskills-spec.md`) requires `name` to match parent directory name. The `skill.sh` / `npx skills` toolchain expects the skill package root to be a directory named after the skill. The version directory is an organizational wrapper, not part of the skill package itself. The inner `{skill-name}/` directory IS the skill package root.

**Full versioned structure:**
```
{skills_output_folder}/
├── .export-manifest.json          <- version-aware (see Q3)
├── cognee/
│   ├── 0.5.0/
│   │   └── cognee/                <- skill package root
│   │       ├── SKILL.md
│   │       ├── metadata.json
│   │       ├── context-snippet.md
│   │       ├── references/
│   │       ├── scripts/
│   │       └── assets/
│   ├── 0.6.0/
│   │   └── cognee/
│   │       ���── SKILL.md
│   │       ├── metadata.json
│   │       ├── context-snippet.md
│   │       ├── references/
│   │       ├── scripts/
│   │       └── assets/
│   └── active -> 0.6.0            <- symlink (see Q6)
```

**Path variables for workflows:**
- `{skill_package_root}` = `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`
- `{skill_version_root}` = `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/`
- `{skill_group_root}` = `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/`

#### Question 2: forge_data_folder -- Mirror the Version Structure

**Decision: Yes, mirror the version structure, but without the inner name repetition.**

Forge data is not a distributable skill package -- it does not need `npx skills` compatibility. The inner name repetition serves no purpose here.

**Pattern:** `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/`

```
{forge_data_folder}/
├── cognee/
│   ├── 0.5.0/
│   │   ├── provenance-map.json
│   │   ├── evidence-report.md
│   │   ├── extraction-rules.yaml
│   │   └── test-report-cognee.md
│   ├── 0.6.0/
│   │   ├── provenance-map.json
│   │   ├── evidence-report.md
│   │   ├── extraction-rules.yaml
│   │   └── test-report-cognee.md
│   └── skill-brief.yaml           <- version-independent (see below)
```

**Key distinction:** `skill-brief.yaml` stays at `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml` (not version-nested), because the brief defines the extraction scope, not a versioned output. The brief's `version` field records the detected source version at brief creation time, but the brief itself is a workflow input, not a versioned artifact. When re-briefing for a new version, the brief is overwritten in place -- its history is not significant.

**Why mirror:** The provenance-map.json, evidence-report.md, and extraction-rules.yaml are all version-bound (as stated in `provenance-tracking.md` line 176: "provenance is version-bound"). Keeping old provenance alongside new provenance enables audit-skill to compare drift across version boundaries, not just within a single version.

#### Question 3: Version-Aware .export-manifest.json

**Current format:**
```json
{
  "exports": {
    "skill-name": {
      "platforms": ["claude"],
      "last_exported": "YYYY-MM-DD"
    }
  }
}
```

**Proposed format:**
```json
{
  "schema_version": "2",
  "exports": {
    "cognee": {
      "active_version": "0.6.0",
      "versions": {
        "0.5.0": {
          "platforms": ["claude"],
          "last_exported": "2026-03-15",
          "status": "archived"
        },
        "0.6.0": {
          "platforms": ["claude", "copilot"],
          "last_exported": "2026-04-04",
          "status": "active"
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
```

**Design rationale:**
- `schema_version: "2"` enables graceful migration detection. When step-04-update-context reads the manifest, it checks `schema_version`. If absent, it is v1 (flat) and can be migrated in-place.
- `active_version` is the authoritative pointer for which version's context-snippet.md gets assembled into the managed section. This replaces the symlink as the machine-readable source of truth (the symlink is a filesystem convenience; the manifest is the canonical record).
- `status` values: `"active"` (currently exported, snippet in managed section), `"archived"` (previously exported, retained for rollback), `"draft"` (created but never exported).
- Only one version per skill can have `status: "active"` at any time. The `active_version` field must match the version with `status: "active"`.

**Impact on step-04-update-context snippet scanning:**

Currently the glob is `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md`. This must change to:
```
{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{active_version}/{skill-name}/context-snippet.md
```

The step reads the manifest first, resolves each skill's `active_version`, then reads the snippet from the resolved path. This is more precise than globbing and eliminates the risk of picking up snippets from non-active versions.

#### Question 4: Platform Root Paths

**Current:** `.claude/skills/{skill-name}/`

**Decision: Do NOT version the platform root path.**

The platform root path (`.claude/skills/`, `.cursor/skills/`, `.agents/skills/`) is the **consumption** path, not the **production** path. These are the paths that agents use to find and load skills at runtime. The agent loads SKILL.md based on the `root:` directive in the managed section snippet. Agents have no concept of version directories -- they just need a stable path to the current skill package.

**The platform install path stays flat:**
```
.claude/skills/cognee/
├── SKILL.md
├── metadata.json
├── references/
├── scripts/
└── assets/
```

The export-skill workflow copies (or symlinks) the active version's skill package to the platform path. The `root:` in context-snippet.md continues to use `{platform_root}{skill-name}/`.

**This means export-skill step-02 (package) gains a new responsibility:** copying the skill package from `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/` to `{platform_root}{skill-name}/`. This is the "publish to platform" step. Currently it only validates; with versioning it also deploys.

**Snippet root path remains unchanged:**
```
[cognee v0.6.0]|root: .claude/skills/cognee/
```

The version is already encoded in the snippet's display name (`v0.6.0`), and the `root:` path points to the flat platform install. No path depth increase.

#### Question 5: Stack Skills

**Current:** `{skills_output_folder}/{project}-stack/`

**Versioned:** `{skills_output_folder}/{project}-stack/{version}/{project}-stack/`

Stack skills follow the same versioning pattern as single skills. The version for a stack skill comes from `metadata.json` (currently set to the primary library version or `1.0.0` in code-mode, or the highest constituent version in compose-mode -- see `step-07-generate-output.md` line 105).

```
{skills_output_folder}/
├── myapp-stack/
│   ├── 1.0.0/
│   │   └── myapp-stack/
│   │       ├── SKILL.md
│   │       ├── metadata.json
│   │       ├── context-snippet.md
│   │       └── references/
│   │           └── integrations/
│   └── active -> 1.0.0
```

**Forge data:**
```
{forge_data_folder}/
├── myapp-stack/
│   └── 1.0.0/
│       ├── provenance-map.json
│       └── evidence-report.md
```

**Compose-mode consideration:** When create-stack-skill runs in compose-mode (step-01-init.md line 110), it scans `{skills_output_folder}` for subdirectories containing SKILL.md and metadata.json. This scan pattern changes from `{skills_output_folder}/*/SKILL.md` to reading the manifest's active versions and resolving to `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{active_version}/{skill-name}/SKILL.md`.

#### Question 6: Active Version Pointer

**Decision: Use BOTH a filesystem symlink AND the manifest field.**

**Symlink:** `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/active -> {version}`

This is a relative symlink pointing to the version directory (not the inner skill-name directory). The full active package path resolves as: `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/active/{skill-name}/`.

**Purpose division:**
- **Manifest `active_version` field:** The authoritative source of truth. All workflow logic (export-skill, update-skill, audit-skill) reads this field. Machine-readable, atomic, cross-platform.
- **Symlink:** Filesystem convenience for human navigation and shell scripts. If the symlink is missing or broken, workflows fall back to the manifest. Symlinks are not available on all filesystems (e.g., some Windows configurations), so the manifest is always primary.

**Symlink creation:** export-skill step-02 (package) creates/updates the symlink after a successful export. create-skill step-07 (generate-artifacts) creates the symlink pointing to the newly created version.

**Rollback scenario:** To roll back to a previous version, the user (or a future rollback workflow) updates `active_version` in the manifest and re-runs export-skill. The old version's complete package is already on disk.

#### Question 7: agentskills.io Spec Compliance -- Subdirectory Depth

**Confirmed interpretation: The "one level deep" rule applies to skill package internals, not to the path TO the package.**

Evidence from `agentskills-spec.md` lines 47-56:
```
skill-name/
├── SKILL.md
├── scripts/
├── references/
└── assets/

All subdirectories are exactly one level deep.
```

And from the Anti-Patterns section (line 169):
```
Deeply nested directory structures -- one level maximum from skill root
```

The "skill root" is the directory containing SKILL.md. In the versioned layout, the skill root is `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`. The subdirectories `references/`, `scripts/`, and `assets/` are one level below THAT root. The version wrapper directories above the skill root are an organizational concern of the forge, not part of the skill package structure.

The inner `{skill-name}/` directory IS the skill package boundary. Everything above it is forge infrastructure; everything below it is spec-governed.

---

### 3. Complete Proposed Structure

**skills_output_folder:**
```
{skills_output_folder}/
├── .export-manifest.json                              <- v2 schema
├── {skill-name}/
│   ├── active -> {version}                            <- symlink (convenience)
│   ├── {version}/
│   │   └── {skill-name}/                              <- skill package root
│   │       ├── SKILL.md
│   │       ├── metadata.json
│   │       ├── context-snippet.md
│   │       ├── references/
│   │       ├── scripts/
│   │       └── assets/
��   └── {older-version}/
│       └── {skill-name}/
│           └── ...
├── {project}-stack/
│   ├── active -> {version}
│   └── {version}/
│       └── {project}-stack/
│           ├── SKILL.md
│           ├── metadata.json
│           ├── context-snippet.md
│           └── references/
│               └── integrations/
```

**forge_data_folder:**
```
{forge_data_folder}/
├── {skill-name}/
│   ├── skill-brief.yaml                               <- version-independent
│   ├── {version}/
│   │   ├── provenance-map.json
│   │   ├── evidence-report.md
│   │   ├── extraction-rules.yaml
│   │   └── test-report-{skill-name}.md
│   └── {older-version}/
│       └── ...
├── {project}-stack/
│   └── {version}/
│       ├── provenance-map.json
│       └── evidence-report.md
├── analyze-source-report-{project}.md                 <- version-independent
├── feasibility-report-{project}.md                    <- version-independent
```

**Platform install (unchanged -- flat):**
```
.claude/skills/{skill-name}/
├── SKILL.md
├── metadata.json
├── references/
���── scripts/
└── assets/
```

---

### 4. Files Requiring Modification and Impact Analysis

The path pattern change from `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/` to `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/{version}/{name}/` affects every workflow that reads or writes to these directories. Here is the complete impact map:

**High impact (path patterns change in core logic):**

| File | Current Pattern | New Pattern | Change Type |
|------|----------------|-------------|-------------|
| `create-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-artifacts.md` | `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/` | `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/{version}/{name}/` | Write paths + directory creation |
| `export-skill/steps-c/step-02-package.md` | `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/` | `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/` | Validation paths + new deploy-to-platform step |
| `export-skill/steps-c/step-03-generate-snippet.md` | `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/context-snippet.md` | Resolved via manifest active_version | Snippet read/write path |
| `export-skill/steps-c/step-04-update-context.md` | Glob `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md` | Manifest-driven resolution per skill | Snippet scanning logic |
| `export-skill/data/managed-section-format.md` | `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md` | Manifest-driven | Regeneration logic |
| `quick-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` | `{skills_output_folder}/{repo_name}/` | `{skills_output_folder}/{repo_name}/{version}/{repo_name}/` | Write paths |
| `create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md` | `{skills_output_folder}/{project}-stack/` and `{forge_data_folder}/{project}-stack/` | Both versioned | Write paths |
| `update-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` | `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/` | Resolved via manifest or active symlink | Read paths |
| `audit-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` | `{skills_output_folder}/{skill_name}/` | Resolved via manifest | Read paths |

**Medium impact (forge_data paths change):**

| File | Change |
|------|--------|
| `create-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-artifacts.md` | `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/` becomes `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/{version}/` |
| `create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md` | `{forge_data_folder}/{project}-stack/` becomes `{forge_data_folder}/{project}-stack/{version}/` |
| `update-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` | `{forge_data_folder}/{skill_name}/provenance-map.json` path changes |
| `audit-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` | `{forge_data_folder}/{skill_name}/provenance-map.json` path changes |
| `test-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` | `outputFile` path changes |
| `test-skill/steps-c/step-02-detect-mode.md` | `outputFile` path changes |
| `test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md` | evidence-report.md path changes |
| `test-skill/steps-c/step-04b-external-validators.md` | evidence-report.md path changes |

2. Add a "version resolution" section to export-skill step-01-load-skill.md that resolves the active version for a skill name
3. Add v1-to-v2 manifest migration logic in step-04-update-context.md

**Phase 2: Write-path updates (create-skill, quick-skill, create-stack-skill)**
4. Update `create-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-artifacts.md` -- new directory creation pattern, symlink creation
5. Update `quick-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` -- same pattern
6. Update `create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md` -- same pattern for both skills and forge-data

**Phase 3: Read-path updates (export-skill, update-skill, audit-skill, test-skill)**
7. Update `export-skill/steps-c/step-02-package.md` -- versioned path validation + deploy-to-platform logic
8. Update `export-skill/steps-c/step-03-generate-snippet.md` -- versioned snippet path
9. Update `export-skill/steps-c/step-04-update-context.md` -- manifest-driven snippet resolution

**Phase 4: Discovery/scan updates**
13. Update compose-mode scan patterns in create-stack-skill

**Phase 5: Documentation**

16. Update snippet-format.md and managed-section-format.md

---

### 6. Migration Strategy for Existing Flat Skills

When a workflow encounters a flat (v1) skill directory (`{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/SKILL.md` exists directly), it should:

1. Read `metadata.json` to get the `version` field
2. Create the versioned directory structure: `{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`
3. Move all package files into the versioned location
4. Create the `active` symlink pointing to `{version}`
5. If `.export-manifest.json` exists and is v1, migrate to v2 format
6. Do the same for `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/` (except `skill-brief.yaml`, which stays at the group level)

This migration can be performed lazily (on first access by any workflow) or eagerly (via a dedicated migration command in setup-forge). The lazy approach is recommended -- each workflow checks for the v1 layout at its resolution step and migrates before proceeding.

# Exhaustive Impact Analysis: Version-Aware Directory Structure Change

## Change Summary

**Current path pattern:** `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/`
**Proposed path pattern:** `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`

This adds a `{version}/{skill-name}/` nesting layer between the top-level skill name directory and the actual skill artifact files (SKILL.md, metadata.json, context-snippet.md, references/, scripts/, assets/).

## CRITICAL DESIGN DECISIONS REQUIRED

### Decision 1: Version Resolution Strategy

When update-skill, audit-skill, test-skill, export-skill, or any reading workflow resolves `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/`, it must now:
1. Enumerate version directories under `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/`
2. Determine which version to operate on (latest? user-specified?)
3. Navigate to `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`

This is the **single most impactful change** and touches every init step that resolves a skill path.

### Decision 2: Version Source

Where does `{version}` come from?
- For create-skill: from `brief.version` or source detection
- For quick-skill: from registry/manifest detection
- For update-skill: from current metadata.json `version` field (or bumped version)
- This must be consistent across all writing workflows

### Decision 3: Context Snippet Root Path

The `root:` path in context-snippet.md is consumed by agents to locate skill files. It must reflect the version-nested path. Every snippet template across all workflows must be updated.

### Decision 4: Export Manifest and Glob Patterns

`{skills_output_folder}/.export-manifest.json` stays at top level (no change), but the snippet scanning glob `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md` must become `{skills_output_folder}/*/*/*/context-snippet.md` or similar version-aware pattern. This is fragile -- consider a helper pattern or explicit manifest-driven lookup instead.

---

2. quick-skill step-06 (write paths)

4. update-skill step-06 (write paths)

**Phase 3 -- Context snippet templates:**
13. All snippet template files (see Category 4 table)

# Comprehensive Breaking Change and Regression Analysis

## Proposed Directory Structure Change

**Current (flat):**
- Skills: `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/`
- Forge data: `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/`

**Proposed (version-nested):**
- Skills: `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`
- Forge data: `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`

---

## RISK ASSESSMENT

---

### 1. agentskills.io Spec Violation: `name` Must Match Parent Directory

**Severity: CRITICAL**

The agentskills.io specification, codified in `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/agentskills-spec.md` line 38, explicitly states:

> `name`: 1-64 characters, lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens, **must match parent directory name**

The proposed structure `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/` satisfies this rule because the innermost directory is `{skill-name}`, which matches the frontmatter `name` field. However, the intermediate `{version}` directory creates an extra nesting level. The spec also says (lines 45-53):

> All subdirectories are **exactly one level deep**. Files are loaded on demand when SKILL.md directs -- never automatically.

The spec shows the canonical layout as:
```
skill-name/
  SKILL.md
  scripts/
  references/
  assets/
```

The version-nested structure means the skill package itself is nested two levels deep within `skills_output_folder`, not one. While the internal structure of the skill package is unchanged (still one level deep), the path from the skills root to the package has changed. Tools like `npx skills add` and `npx skill-check check` that expect `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/SKILL.md` will not find the file.

**Impact:** `npx skills add`, `npx skills publish`, `npx skill-check check`, `npx skill-check diff`, and `npx skill-check split-body` will all break. These are called throughout create-skill, update-skill, and export-skill validation steps.

---

### 3. Existing Users: Skills at Flat Path Become Unreachable

**Severity: CRITICAL**

Any user who has already generated skills using the current flat structure will find that all subsequent workflows (export-skill, update-skill, audit-skill, test-skill) fail immediately. The init steps of update-skill and audit-skill resolve skill names to `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/` (update-skill step-01 line 63, audit-skill step-01 line 67). After the change, they would resolve to `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/{version}/{name}/` -- but the existing skill is at the old flat path.

**Immediate failures:**
- update-skill step-01 section 2: "No SKILL.md found at {resolved_path}. Run create-skill first." (line 78)
- audit-skill step-01: "Skill not found at {resolved_path}." (line 71)
- export-skill step-01: skill discovery glob `{skills_output_folder}/*/SKILL.md` finds nothing at new depth (line 65)

**Migration path needed:** There is currently zero migration logic. A migration step or dual-path resolution strategy is mandatory.

---

### 4. `.export-manifest.json` Path References Break

**Severity: HIGH**

The export manifest at `{skills_output_folder}/.export-manifest.json` stores skill names as keys (not full paths), and the snippet scanning uses `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md` (export-skill step-04 line 119). Under the new structure, snippets would be at `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/{version}/{name}/context-snippet.md` -- three levels deep, not one. The glob pattern `*/context-snippet.md` will not match.

The manifest itself only stores skill names and platform arrays (step-04 lines 97-104), not full paths. So the manifest data itself survives, but the path resolution logic that reads snippets alongside the manifest is broken.

---

### 5. Context Snippet Root Paths Become Deeper

**Severity: HIGH**

Currently the snippet `root:` paths follow this pattern (from snippet-format.md):
```
[skill-name v1.0.0]|root: .claude/skills/skill-name/
```

Under the new structure, this becomes:
```
[skill-name v1.0.0]|root: .claude/skills/skill-name/1.0.0/skill-name/
```

This has two consequences:
1. **More tokens per snippet:** The root path is longer, eating into the 80-120 token budget per skill. With 20+ skills, this adds up.
2. **Platform root path rewriting logic** in export-skill step-04 section 4d relies on detecting known prefixes (`.claude/skills/`, `.cursor/skills/`, `.agents/skills/`, legacy `skills/`). The new structure inserts `{version}/{skill-name}` after the skill name, which changes the path pattern but does not break prefix detection (the prefix is at the start). However, agents reading the `root:` path to locate SKILL.md will navigate to a deeper path.

---

### 6. Provenance Map Location Change Breaks Drift Detection

**Severity: HIGH**

The provenance map is stored at `{forge_data_folder}/{skill_name}/provenance-map.json`. Update-skill step-01 (line 105) and audit-skill step-01 (line 106) load it from this path. If the forge_data_folder structure also changes to `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`, then:

- Existing provenance maps at `{forge_data_folder}/{skill_name}/provenance-map.json` become unfindable
- The provenance data (per-export source mappings, extraction timestamps, confidence tiers) is lost without migration
- Both workflows degrade to "no provenance map found" mode, offering degraded or abort options
- In degraded mode, update-skill performs full re-extraction (equivalent to create-skill) -- losing all provenance history and timestamps

This also affects: evidence-report.md, extraction-rules.yaml, test-report files, drift-report files, and skill-brief.yaml files stored under forge_data_folder.

---

### 7. `[MANUAL]` Sections at Risk During Migration

**Severity: HIGH**

If migration involves re-creating skills at new paths, the `[MANUAL]` sections in existing SKILL.md files must be carried over. The MANUAL section integrity system (`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/manual-section-integrity.md`) guarantees zero content loss during update-skill operations, but a path migration is not an update-skill operation. If migration is handled by a simple file move/copy, MANUAL sections survive. If migration requires re-running create-skill, MANUAL content is lost unless the migration explicitly reads the old SKILL.md and transplants MANUAL blocks.

---

### 8. Version Format Edge Cases in Directory Names

**Severity: HIGH**

Semver versions can contain characters that are problematic in directory names. The brief-skill schema (`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/brief-skill/data/skill-brief-schema.md` line 48) explicitly says:

> Pre-release handling: If the detected version contains a pre-release tag (e.g., `1.0.0-beta.0`, `2.0.0-rc.1`), preserve it as-is.

Versions like `1.0.0-beta.1+build.123` contain `+` characters. On many file systems, `+` is legal, but:
- URL-encoding in web contexts may break
- Some tools or shell scripts may interpret `+` specially
- Build metadata after `+` in semver is not supposed to affect version ordering but creates distinct directory names
- The same version could appear as both `1.0.0-beta.1` and `1.0.0-beta.1+build.123` -- are these the same directory?

---

### 9. Quick-Skill Version Default Creates Ambiguity

**Severity: MEDIUM**

Quick-skill defaults version to `1.0.0` when detection fails (quick-skill step-04 line 126: `"version": "{extraction_inventory.version or 1.0.0}"`). Under the new structure:
- First quick-skill run: skill goes to `{skills_output_folder}/my-lib/1.0.0/my-lib/`
- User discovers actual version is 2.3.1, re-runs: skill goes to `{skills_output_folder}/my-lib/2.3.1/my-lib/`
- Now two versions exist, both in the export manifest
- Which version's snippet goes into CLAUDE.md?

The current system has no version-aware conflict resolution in the export manifest. The manifest tracks `last_exported` date but not version.

---

### 10. Skill Name vs Repo Name Divergence

**Severity: MEDIUM**

Quick-skill uses `repo_name` for the output directory (step-06 line 38: `{skills_output_folder}/{repo_name}/`), while create-skill uses the brief's `name` field (step-07 line 68). If `repo_name` and `skill-name` differ (e.g., repo is `express` but skill is named `using-express`), the version-nested path becomes:
```
{skills_output_folder}/express/1.0.0/express/       (quick-skill)
{skills_output_folder}/using-express/4.18.0/using-express/  (create-skill)
```

These are treated as different skills. This is actually the current behavior too, but the version nesting amplifies the confusion because now you could have:
```
{skills_output_folder}/express/1.0.0/express/
{skills_output_folder}/using-express/4.18.0/using-express/
```
...both documenting the same library at different versions with different skill names.

---

### 11. Multiple Versions of Same Skill: CLAUDE.md Conflict

**Severity: MEDIUM**

The managed section regeneration logic in export-skill step-04 section 4c scans `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md` and filters against the export manifest. If multiple versions exist:
- `{skills_output_folder}/my-lib/1.0.0/my-lib/context-snippet.md`
- `{skills_output_folder}/my-lib/2.0.0/my-lib/context-snippet.md`

The current glob will not find either (wrong depth). If the glob is updated to handle the new depth, both snippets would be found. The export manifest uses skill name as the key, so only one entry per skill name. But the snippet scanning would find multiple `context-snippet.md` files under different versions.

Need a policy: does the managed section include only the latest exported version? All versions? The current system has no version-aware selection logic.

---

### 12. Stack Skill References Include Integration File Paths

**Severity: MEDIUM**

Stack skills store reference files at paths like:
```
{skills_output_folder}/{project}-stack/references/{library}.md
{skills_output_folder}/{project}-stack/references/integrations/{libA}-{libB}.md
```

In compose-mode (create-stack-skill step-07 line 189), the provenance map stores:

```

Under the new structure, these source skill paths change. The compose-mode provenance would need to reference `skills/{skill_dir}/{version}/{skill_dir}/` instead. Existing stack skills that reference flat-path source skills would have broken provenance references.

### 13. `npx skills publish` / `npx skills add` Compatibility

**Severity: MEDIUM**

The export-skill summary (step-06 lines 85-90) recommends:
```
npx skills publish {skill-name}
npx skills add {skill-name}
```

The `npx skills` tool (agentskills.io ecosystem tooling) expects the standard flat structure `{skill-name}/SKILL.md`. If the version-nested structure is adopted locally but not by the ecosystem tooling, then:
- `npx skills add` would install to the flat structure, contradicting the local version-nested convention
- `npx skills publish` would need to find the skill at the new path depth
- There is no evidence in the codebase that `npx skills` supports version-nested directories

## RECOMMENDATIONS

### Mandatory Before Implementation

1. **Migration strategy:** Implement a dual-path resolution layer. When resolving a skill name, check the new versioned path first, then fall back to the old flat path. This allows existing skills to keep working while new skills use the versioned structure.

2. **Version sanitization:** Define a version-to-directory-name transformation that strips or replaces problematic characters (`+` becomes `-plus-` or is stripped). Document the mapping so it is reversible.

3. **Single-version policy for export:** Define that the managed section always references the single latest-exported version. The export manifest should store version alongside platform list.

4. **Path abstraction:** Rather than updating 50+ hardcoded path patterns, introduce a path resolution function (conceptual, since these are markdown instructions for agents) -- perhaps a config-level `skill_path_template` variable like `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/` that all steps reference. This way the path pattern is defined once in config.yaml.

5. **Migration command/workflow:** Create a dedicated migration workflow or a section in setup-forge that detects flat-structure skills and offers to migrate them to the versioned structure, preserving all MANUAL sections, provenance maps, evidence reports, and export manifests.

1	# agentskills.io Specification
2	
3	## Principle
4	
5	Skills produced by Skill Forge comply with the agentskills.io specification — a format designed for agent consumption through progressive disclosure. The format prioritizes discoverability at low token cost, deterministic procedural instructions, and just-in-time resource loading.
6	
7	## Rationale
8	
9	Agent skills are not documentation for humans. They are instructions consumed by AI agents that treat every statement as actionable truth. The agentskills.io format was designed around this constraint: minimal metadata for discovery, structured instructions for activation, and on-demand resources for execution.
10	
11	Without format compliance:
12	- Skills are not portable across agent platforms
13	- Discovery requires loading full skill content, wasting context tokens
14	- Agents cannot distinguish skills from general documentation
15	
16	With format compliance:
17	- Skills are discoverable from ~100 tokens of frontmatter metadata
18	- Full activation loads < 500 lines of structured instructions
19	- Resources load on demand, keeping context lean during execution
20	
21	## Format Structure
22	
23	### Required: SKILL.md
24	
25	The minimum viable skill is a single `SKILL.md` file containing:
26	
27	**Frontmatter** (YAML between `---` markers):
28	```yaml
29	---
30	name: library-name
31	description: >
32	  What the skill does and when to use it. Include specific keywords
33	  for agent discovery. Mention what NOT to use it for.
34	---
35	```
36	
37	**Frontmatter constraints:**
38	- `name`: 1-64 characters, lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens, must match parent directory name
39	  - **Naming convention:** Prefer gerund form (verb + -ing) for clarity: `processing-pdfs`, `analyzing-spreadsheets`, `managing-databases`. Noun phrases (`pdf-processing`) and action-oriented forms (`process-pdfs`) are acceptable alternatives. Avoid vague names (`helper`, `utils`, `tools`).
40	- `description`: 1-1024 characters, trigger-optimized for agent matching
41	  - **MUST use third-person voice.** The description is injected into the system prompt; inconsistent point-of-view causes discovery problems. Write "Processes Excel files and generates reports" — never "I can help you process Excel files" or "You can use this to process Excel files."
42	
43	**Body:** Free-form markdown — no structural restrictions, but Skill Forge follows a consistent section order (see skill-sections.md in create-skill/data/).
44	
45	### Optional: Supporting Directories
46	
47	```
48	skill-name/
49	├── SKILL.md              # Instructions and API reference
50	├── scripts/              # Executable automation
51	├── references/           # Detailed reference material
52	└── assets/               # Templates, schemas, configs
53	```
54	
55	All subdirectories are exactly one level deep. Files are loaded on demand when SKILL.md directs — never automatically.
56	
57	Scripts and assets extracted by Skill Forge inherit provenance from their source repository. Each file receives a `[SRC:{source_path}:L1]` citation (T1-low confidence) and a SHA-256 content hash for drift detection. Scripts must follow the quality principles in the Script Quality section below. Assets are static files loaded on demand — agents use them as directed by SKILL.md instructions.

75	
76	| Platform | Target File |

78	| `claude` | CLAUDE.md |
79	| `cursor` | .cursorrules |
80	| `copilot` | AGENTS.md |

82	For each platform in `target_platforms`, resolve target file path: `{project-root}/{target-file}`

84	**If multiple platforms:** Sections 4-9a execute as a loop — one full pass per target platform. Each iteration uses the same skill index but rewrites root paths per platform (section 4d) and writes to the platform-specific target file. Section 9b executes once after all iterations complete.
85

88	### 4. Rebuild Complete Skill Index
89	
90	#### 4a. Read Export Manifest
91	
92	Read `{skills_output_folder}/.export-manifest.json`:
93	
94	**If the file exists:** Parse JSON. The manifest tracks which skills have been explicitly exported:
95	
96	```json
97	{
98	  "exports": {
99	    "skill-name": {
100	      "platforms": ["claude"],
101	      "last_exported": "YYYY-MM-DD"
102	    }
103	  }
104	}
105	```
106	
107	**If the file does not exist** (first export or migration): Treat as empty — only the current export target will appear in the rebuilt index.
108	
109	#### 4b. Build Exported Skill Set
110	
111	Determine the set of skills to include in the rebuilt index:
112	
113	1. Start with all skill names listed in the manifest's `exports` object (if manifest exists)
114	2. Add the current export target skill name (ensures it is always included even before manifest is written)
115	3. This is the **exported skill set**
116	
117	#### 4c. Scan and Filter Snippets
118	
119	Scan `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md` to find snippet files on disk.
120	
121	**For each found context-snippet.md:**
122	1. Extract the skill name from the directory path
123	2. **If the skill name is in the exported skill set:** Read snippet content, add to skill index
124	3. **If the skill name is NOT in the exported skill set:** Skip — this skill has not been through export-skill and must not appear in the managed section (ADR-K)
125	
126	**If no snippets pass the filter:** Generate managed section with zero skills — header only, no skill entries.
127	
128	#### 4d. Rewrite Root Paths for Target Platform
129	
130	The context-snippet.md files on disk contain root paths for the platform they were originally exported to. When assembling the managed section for the current target platform, rewrite root paths if they differ:
131	
132	**Platform root path mapping:**
133	
134	| Platform | Root Path Prefix |
135	|----------|-----------------|
136	| `claude` | `.claude/skills/` |
137	| `cursor` | `.cursor/skills/` |
138	| `copilot` | `.agents/skills/` |
139	| _(legacy)_ | `skills/` |
140	
141	The legacy `skills/` prefix may appear in snippets exported before platform-aware root paths were introduced, or in draft snippets generated by create-skill/quick-skill.
142	
143	For each snippet being included in the managed section:
144	
145	1. Read the `root:` value from the snippet's first line
146	2. Detect the current root prefix by matching against the known prefixes above (check platform-specific prefixes first, then fall back to legacy `skills/`)
147	3. If the detected prefix does not match the current target platform's prefix, rewrite it
148	4. Example: if snippet has `root: .claude/skills/my-lib/` but target is `cursor`, rewrite to `root: .cursor/skills/my-lib/`
149	5. Example: if snippet has legacy `root: skills/my-lib/` and target is `copilot`, rewrite to `root: .agents/skills/my-lib/`
150	
151	This ensures each platform's managed section points to the correct platform-specific skill directory, including during migration from pre-platform-aware exports.
152	
153	**Sort skills alphabetically by name.**
154	
155	Count totals:
156	- Total skills (single type)
157	- Total stack skills
158

160	
161	Assemble the complete managed section:
162	
163	```markdown
164	<!-- SKF:BEGIN updated:{current-date} -->
165	[SKF Skills]|{n} skills|{m} stack
166	|IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data.
167	|When using a listed library, read its SKILL.md before writing code.
168	|
169	|{skill-snippet-1}
