{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/.claude/plans/reflective-skipping-wolf.md",
  "content": "# Plan: Version-Aware Skill Directory Structure\n\n## Context\n\nWhen Skill Forge generates a skill for `cognee v0.5.0` and later detects drift in `v0.5.1` or `v0.6.0`, the update-skill workflow overwrites the existing skill in-place. Users pinned to `cognee v0.5.0` lose their skill. The proposed change adds version-aware nesting so multiple versions coexist and users (or package managers like `skill.sh` / `npx skills`) can install a specific version.\n\n**Multi-perspective analysis** was conducted via three parallel Plan agents covering: structural design, workflow impact analysis (41+ files), and breaking change/regression review (17 issues identified, 3 CRITICAL). Key findings are synthesized below.\n\n---\n\n## Proposed Directory Structure\n\n### skills_output_folder (forge workspace)\n\n```\n{skills_output_folder}/\n  .export-manifest.json              <- v2 schema (version-aware)\n  {skill-name}/\n    active -> {version}              <- symlink (convenience)\n    {version}/\n      {skill-name}/                  <- skill package root (agentskills.io-compliant)\n        SKILL.md\n        metadata.json\n        context-snippet.md\n        references/\n        scripts/\n        assets/\n    {older-version}/\n      {skill-name}/\n        ...\n```\n\n**Why inner `{skill-name}/` repetition:** agentskills.io requires `name` to match parent directory name. The inner directory IS the distributable skill package. The version directory is an organizational wrapper. This is compatible with `skill.sh` / `npx skills add` which expects `{skill-name}/SKILL.md`.\n\n### forge_data_folder (workspace artifacts)\n\n```\n{forge_data_folder}/\n  {skill-name}/\n    skill-brief.yaml                 <- version-independent (workflow input)\n    {version}/\n      provenance-map.json\n      evidence-report.md\n      extraction-rules.yaml\n      test-report-{skill-name}.md\n```\n\nNo inner name repetition — forge data isn't a distributable package.\n\n### Platform install paths (UNCHANGED — flat)\n\n```\n.claude/skills/{skill-name}/         <- flat, no version nesting\n  SKILL.md\n  references/\n  ...\n```\n\n**Critical design decision:** Platform root paths stay flat. Version nesting is internal to the forge workspace. Snippet `root:` in CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md/.cursorrules remains `.claude/skills/{skill-name}/`. The version is already visible in the snippet display name `[cognee v0.6.0]`. This avoids token bloat and doesn't require consuming agents to understand versioning.\n\n### Export manifest v2 schema\n\n```json\n{\n  \"schema_version\": \"2\",\n  \"exports\": {\n    \"cognee\": {\n      \"active_version\": \"0.6.0\",\n      \"versions\": {\n        \"0.5.0\": {\n          \"platforms\": [\"claude\"],\n          \"last_exported\": \"2026-03-15\",\n          \"status\": \"archived\"\n        },\n        \"0.6.0\": {\n          \"platforms\": [\"claude\", \"copilot\"],\n          \"last_exported\": \"2026-04-04\",\n          \"status\": \"active\"\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Path Template Abstraction\n\nTo avoid updating 50+ hardcoded path patterns individually, introduce a **path resolution knowledge file** that all workflows reference:\n\n**New file: `src/knowledge/version-paths.md`**\n\nDefines canonical path templates:\n- `{skill_package}` = `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`\n- `{skill_group}` = `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/`\n- `{forge_version}` = `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/`\n- `{forge_group}` = `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/`\n- `{active_skill}` = `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/active/{skill-name}/` (via symlink)\n\nAll workflow steps reference these templates instead of constructing paths directly.\n\n---\n\n## Impact Analysis Summary\n\n| Category | Files Affected | Risk |\n|----------|---------------|------|\n| Write-path workflows (create-skill, quick-skill, create-stack-skill, update-skill) | ~12 step files | CRITICAL |\n| Read-path workflows (export-skill, audit-skill, test-skill, verify-stack, refine-arch) | ~11 step files | HIGH |\n| Context snippet templates (cross-cutting) | ~10 files | HIGH |\n| Compose-mode read paths (create-stack-skill) | ~5 files | HIGH |\n| Data/template files | ~6 files | MEDIUM |\n| Knowledge/spec files | ~3 files | LOW |\n| **TOTAL** | **~47 files** | |\n\n---\n\n## Critical Breaking Changes & Mitigations\n\n### CRITICAL-1: Existing flat-structure skills become unreachable\n**Mitigation:** Dual-path resolution. All reading workflows check versioned path first, fall back to flat path. On first access of a flat-structure skill, offer auto-migration (move files to versioned layout, preserving [MANUAL] sections).\n\n### CRITICAL-2: 50+ hardcoded path patterns\n**Mitigation:** Path template abstraction via `src/knowledge/version-paths.md`. Workflows reference templates, not raw paths.\n\n### CRITICAL-3: Export manifest v1 → v2 incompatibility\n**Mitigation:** If `schema_version` is absent in manifest, treat as v1 and migrate in-place during export-skill step-04.\n\n### HIGH-1: Snippet scanning glob `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md` breaks\n**Mitigation:** Replace glob-based scanning with manifest-driven resolution. Read manifest, resolve each skill's active_version, read snippet from resolved versioned path.\n\n### HIGH-2: Provenance maps at old paths become unfindable\n**Mitigation:** Same dual-path resolution as CRITICAL-1, applied to forge_data_folder paths.\n\n### HIGH-3: Version format edge cases (`1.0.0-beta.1+build.123`)\n**Mitigation:** Strip `+{build}` metadata from directory names (semver spec says build metadata doesn't affect ordering). Pre-release tags (`-beta.1`) are preserved. Document the sanitization rule.\n\n### MEDIUM-1: Quick-skill defaults version to `1.0.0` — creates orphan version directories\n**Mitigation:** Quick-skill already auto-detects version where possible. For undetected versions, `1.0.0` is acceptable — update-skill will create a new version directory when real version is discovered.\n\n### MEDIUM-2: Multiple versions — which goes in CLAUDE.md?\n**Mitigation:** Only the `active_version` from the manifest. One active version per skill at a time.\n\n---\n\n## Implementation Phases\n\n### Phase 1: Foundation — New knowledge file + manifest v2 schema\n\n**New file:**\n- `src/knowledge/version-paths.md` — Path templates, resolution rules, migration logic, version sanitization\n\n**Modify:**\n- `src/knowledge/agentskills-spec.md` — Add note clarifying version wrapper vs package boundary (lines 47-55)\n- `src/knowledge/skill-lifecycle.md` — Update artifact flow references\n- `src/knowledge/provenance-tracking.md` — Update path examples\n\n### Phase 2: Write-path workflows (create new versioned output)\n\n**Modify (all path references → use templates from version-paths.md):**\n- `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-artifacts.md` — Directory creation (lines 57-66), all file write paths (lines 72-97), forge data paths (lines 99-111), verification display (lines 127+)\n- `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-08-report.md` — Output file listing paths\n- `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` — All output paths (lines 38-124)\n- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md` — All output paths\n- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-08-validate.md` — Deliverables paths\n- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-09-report.md` — File listing path\n- `src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` — All file write paths\n\n### Phase 3: Read-path workflows (resolve versioned locations)\n\n**Modify:**\n- `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-skill.md` — Skill discovery: manifest-driven instead of glob (line 64)\n- `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-02-package.md` — Versioned path validation (line 58)\n- `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-03-generate-snippet.md` — Snippet read/write at versioned path\n- `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-04-update-context.md` — {
  "questions": [
    {
      "question": "How should the 'active version' pointer work? Symlinks are convenient for filesystem navigation but don't work on all Windows setups.",
      "header": "Active ptr",
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Symlink + manifest (Recommended)",
          "description": "Filesystem symlink for convenience + manifest active_version field as authoritative source. Workflows read manifest; symlink is a bonus for humans/scripts."
        },
        {
          "label": "Manifest only",
          "description": "No filesystem symlink. All version resolution goes through .export-manifest.json. Simpler, fully cross-platform."
        },
        {
          "label": "Symlink only",
          "description": "No manifest changes. Symlink is the sole pointer. Simpler filesystem but fragile (broken symlinks, Windows compat)."
        }
      ],
      "multiSelect": false
    },
    {
      "question": "When a workflow encounters an existing flat-structure skill (pre-versioning), how should migration happen?",
      "header": "Migration",
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Auto-migrate on first access (Recommended)",
          "description": "Any reading workflow detects flat layout, reads version from metadata.json, moves files to versioned structure automatically. Zero user intervention."
        },
        {
          "label": "Explicit migration command",
          "description": "Add a migration step to setup-forge workflow. User must run it manually. Safer but requires user action."
        },
        {
          "label": "Dual-path forever",
          "description": "Never migrate. Workflows always check both flat and versioned paths. No data movement, but permanent complexity."
        }
      ],
      "multiSelect": false
    },
    {
      "question": "How should semver build metadata (the +build.123 part) be handled in directory names?",
      "header": "Version fmt",
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Strip +build metadata (Recommended)",
          "description": "Per semver spec, build metadata doesn't affect version precedence. Directory name uses only X.Y.Z-prerelease. Example: 1.0.0-beta.1"
        },
        {
          "label": "Replace + with double dash",
          "description": "1.0.0-beta.1+build.123 becomes directory 1.0.0-beta.1--build.123. Preserves info but adds non-standard convention."

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Version-Aware Skill Directory Structure

## Context

When Skill Forge generates a skill for `cognee v0.5.0` and later detects drift in `v0.5.1` or `v0.6.0`, the update-skill workflow overwrites the existing skill in-place. Users pinned to `cognee v0.5.0` lose their skill. The proposed change adds version-aware nesting so multiple versions coexist and users (or package managers like `skill.sh` / `npx skills`) can install a specific version.

**Multi-perspective analysis** was conducted via three parallel Plan agents covering: structural design, workflow impact analysis (41+ files), and breaking change/regression review (17 issues identified, 3 CRITICAL). Key findings are synthesized below.

---

## Proposed Directory Structure

### skills_output_folder (forge workspace)

```
{skills_output_folder}/
  .export-manifest.json              <- v2 schema (version-aware)
  {skill-name}/
    active -> {version}              <- symlink (convenience)
    {version}/
      {skill-name}/                  <- skill package root (agentskills.io-compliant)
        SKILL.md
        metadata.json
        context-snippet.md
        references/
        scripts/
        assets/
    {older-version}/
      {skill-name}/
        ...
```

**Why inner `{skill-name}/` repetition:** agentskills.io requires `name` to match parent directory name. The inner directory IS the distributable skill package. The version directory is an organizational wrapper. This is compatible with `skill.sh` / `npx skills add` which expects `{skill-name}/SKILL.md`.

### forge_data_folder (workspace artifacts)

```
{forge_data_folder}/
  {skill-name}/
    skill-brief.yaml                 <- version-independent (workflow input)
    {version}/
      provenance-map.json
      evidence-report.md
      extraction-rules.yaml
      test-report-{skill-name}.md
```

No inner name repetition — forge data isn't a distributable package.

### Platform install paths (UNCHANGED — flat)

```
.claude/skills/{skill-name}/         <- flat, no version nesting
  SKILL.md
  references/
  ...
```

**Critical design decision:** Platform root paths stay flat. Version nesting is internal to the forge workspace. Snippet `root:` in CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md/.cursorrules remains `.claude/skills/{skill-name}/`. The version is already visible in the snippet display name `[cognee v0.6.0]`. This avoids token bloat and doesn't require consuming agents to understand versioning.

### Export manifest v2 schema

```json
{
  "schema_version": "2",
  "exports": {
    "cognee": {
      "active_version": "0.6.0",
      "versions": {
        "0.5.0": {
          "platforms": ["claude"],
          "last_exported": "2026-03-15",
          "status": "archived"
        },
        "0.6.0": {
          "platforms": ["claude", "copilot"],
          "last_exported": "2026-04-04",
          "status": "active"
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
```

---

## Path Template Abstraction

To avoid updating 50+ hardcoded path patterns individually, introduce a **path resolution knowledge file** that all workflows reference:

**New file: `src/knowledge/version-paths.md`**

Defines canonical path templates:
- `{skill_package}` = `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`
- `{skill_group}` = `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/`
- `{forge_version}` = `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/`
- `{forge_group}` = `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/`
- `{active_skill}` = `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/active/{skill-name}/` (via symlink)

All workflow steps reference these templates instead of constructing paths directly.

---

## Impact Analysis Summary

| Category | Files Affected | Risk |
|----------|---------------|------|
| Write-path workflows (create-skill, quick-skill, create-stack-skill, update-skill) | ~12 step files | CRITICAL |
| Read-path workflows (export-skill, audit-skill, test-skill, verify-stack, refine-arch) | ~11 step files | HIGH |
| Context snippet templates (cross-cutting) | ~10 files | HIGH |
| Compose-mode read paths (create-stack-skill) | ~5 files | HIGH |
| Data/template files | ~6 files | MEDIUM |
| Knowledge/spec files | ~3 files | LOW |
| **TOTAL** | **~47 files** | |

---

## Critical Breaking Changes & Mitigations

### CRITICAL-1: Existing flat-structure skills become unreachable
**Mitigation:** Dual-path resolution. All reading workflows check versioned path first, fall back to flat path. On first access of a flat-structure skill, offer auto-migration (move files to versioned layout, preserving [MANUAL] sections).

### CRITICAL-2: 50+ hardcoded path patterns
**Mitigation:** Path template abstraction via `src/knowledge/version-paths.md`. Workflows reference templates, not raw paths.

### CRITICAL-3: Export manifest v1 → v2 incompatibility
**Mitigation:** If `schema_version` is absent in manifest, treat as v1 and migrate in-place during export-skill step-04.

### HIGH-1: Snippet scanning glob `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md` breaks
**Mitigation:** Replace glob-based scanning with manifest-driven resolution. Read manifest, resolve each skill's active_version, read snippet from resolved versioned path.

### HIGH-2: Provenance maps at old paths become unfindable
**Mitigation:** Same dual-path resolution as CRITICAL-1, applied to forge_data_folder paths.

### HIGH-3: Version format edge cases (`1.0.0-beta.1+build.123`)
**Mitigation:** Strip `+{build}` metadata from directory names (semver spec says build metadata doesn't affect ordering). Pre-release tags (`-beta.1`) are preserved. Document the sanitization rule.

### MEDIUM-1: Quick-skill defaults version to `1.0.0` — creates orphan version directories
**Mitigation:** Quick-skill already auto-detects version where possible. For undetected versions, `1.0.0` is acceptable — update-skill will create a new version directory when real version is discovered.

### MEDIUM-2: Multiple versions — which goes in CLAUDE.md?
**Mitigation:** Only the `active_version` from the manifest. One active version per skill at a time.

---

## Implementation Phases

### Phase 1: Foundation — New knowledge file + manifest v2 schema

**New file:**
- `src/knowledge/version-paths.md` — Path templates, resolution rules, migration logic, version sanitization

**Modify:**
- `src/knowledge/agentskills-spec.md` — Add note clarifying version wrapper vs package boundary (lines 47-55)
- `src/knowledge/skill-lifecycle.md` — Update artifact flow references
- `src/knowledge/provenance-tracking.md` — Update path examples

### Phase 2: Write-path workflows (create new versioned output)

**Modify (all path references → use templates from version-paths.md):**
- `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-artifacts.md` — Directory creation (lines 57-66), all file write paths (lines 72-97), forge data paths (lines 99-111), verification display (lines 127+)
- `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-08-report.md` — Output file listing paths
- `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` — All output paths (lines 38-124)
- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md` — All output paths
- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-08-validate.md` — Deliverables paths
- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-09-report.md` — File listing path
- `src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` — All file write paths

### Phase 3: Read-path workflows (resolve versioned locations)

**Modify:**
- `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-skill.md` — Skill discovery: manifest-driven instead of glob (line 64)
- `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-02-package.md` — Versioned path validation (line 58)
- `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-03-generate-snippet.md` — Snippet read/write at versioned path
- `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-04-update-context.md` — Manifest v2 logic, snippet scanning from manifest (lines 92-124), v1→v2 migration
- `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-06-summary.md` — Location path display
- `src/workflows/export-skill/data/managed-section-format.md` — Update glob patterns to manifest-driven
- `src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` — Version-aware skill resolution (line 63), provenance loading
- `src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-07-report.md` — File listing paths
- `src/workflows/audit-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` — Version-aware skill resolution
- `src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` — Version-aware paths
- `src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-02-detect-mode.md` — Output file path
- `src/workflows/verify-stack/steps-c/step-01-init.md` — Skills directory scan
- `src/workflows/refine-architecture/steps-c/step-01-init.md` — Skills directory scan

### Phase 4: Compose-mode + stack skill reads

**Modify:**
- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` — Compose-mode skill scan
- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-02-detect-manifests.md` — Skill scanning
- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-04-parallel-extract.md` — SKILL.md loading path
- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-05-detect-integrations.md` — SKILL.md reload path
- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/data/compose-mode-rules.md` — Scan pattern

### Phase 5: Context snippet templates (cross-cutting)

**Modify (draft `root:` path to use versioned template):**
- `src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` (lines 129, 131, 166)
- `src/workflows/create-skill/data/compile-assembly-rules.md` (line 191)
- `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-05-compile.md` (line 82)
- `src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md` (line 55)
- `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md` (line 106)
- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/data/stack-skill-template.md` (line 57)
- `src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md` (line 105)
- `src/workflows/export-skill/data/snippet-format.md` (lines 28, 46)
- `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-03-generate-snippet.md` (lines 106, 122)

### Phase 6: Documentation + module-level updates

**Modify:**
- `src/knowledge/overview.md` — If it references skill output structure
- `src/workflows/README.md` — If it references output paths

---

## Verification

1. **Create a test skill:** Run create-skill for a sample lib → verify files land at `{skills_output_folder}/{name}/{version}/{name}/` with `active` symlink
2. **Export the skill:** Run export-skill → verify manifest v2 is written, snippet root is flat platform path, CLAUDE.md managed section renders correctly
3. **Create a second version:** Run create-skill with a different version → verify coexistence under `{skill-name}/`
4. **Update active version:** Run export-skill for the new version → verify manifest `active_version` updates, old version's files are untouched
5. **Update-skill drift detection:** Run update-skill against the active version → verify provenance map loads from versioned forge_data path
6. **Flat migration:** Create a skill at the old flat path → run any reading workflow → verify it offers migration and succeeds
7. **Stack skill:** Run create-stack-skill → verify versioned output structure for stack skills
8. **Audit-skill:** Run audit-skill → verify it resolves the versioned path correctly

---

## Design Decisions (Confirmed)

1. **Active version pointer:** Symlink + manifest. `active` symlink for filesystem convenience, `active_version` in `.export-manifest.json` as authoritative source. Workflows read manifest; symlink is a bonus for humans/scripts.
2. **Migration:** Auto-migrate on first access. Any reading workflow detects flat layout, reads version from metadata.json, moves files to versioned structure automatically. Zero user intervention.
3. **Version sanitization:** Strip `+build` metadata from directory names per semver spec. Directory names use only `X.Y.Z-prerelease`. Example: `1.0.0-beta.1+build.123` becomes directory `1.0.0-beta.1`.

1	# agentskills.io Specification
2	
3	## Principle
4	
5	Skills produced by Skill Forge comply with the agentskills.io specification — a format designed for agent consumption through progressive disclosure. The format prioritizes discoverability at low token cost, deterministic procedural instructions, and just-in-time resource loading.
6	
7	## Rationale
8	
9	Agent skills are not documentation for humans. They are instructions consumed by AI agents that treat every statement as actionable truth. The agentskills.io format was designed around this constraint: minimal metadata for discovery, structured instructions for activation, and on-demand resources for execution.
10	
11	Without format compliance:
12	- Skills are not portable across agent platforms
13	- Discovery requires loading full skill content, wasting context tokens
14	- Agents cannot distinguish skills from general documentation
15	
16	With format compliance:
17	- Skills are discoverable from ~100 tokens of frontmatter metadata
18	- Full activation loads < 500 lines of structured instructions
19	- Resources load on demand, keeping context lean during execution
20	
21	## Format Structure
22	
23	### Required: SKILL.md
24	
25	The minimum viable skill is a single `SKILL.md` file containing:
26	
27	**Frontmatter** (YAML between `---` markers):
28	```yaml
29	---
30	name: library-name
31	description: >
32	  What the skill does and when to use it. Include specific keywords
33	  for agent discovery. Mention what NOT to use it for.
34	---
35	```
36	
37	**Frontmatter constraints:**
38	- `name`: 1-64 characters, lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens, must match parent directory name
39	  - **Naming convention:** Prefer gerund form (verb + -ing) for clarity: `processing-pdfs`, `analyzing-spreadsheets`, `managing-databases`. Noun phrases (`pdf-processing`) and action-oriented forms (`process-pdfs`) are acceptable alternatives. Avoid vague names (`helper`, `utils`, `tools`).
40	- `description`: 1-1024 characters, trigger-optimized for agent matching
41	  - **MUST use third-person voice.** The description is injected into the system prompt; inconsistent point-of-view causes discovery problems. Write "Processes Excel files and generates reports" — never "I can help you process Excel files" or "You can use this to process Excel files."
42	
43	**Body:** Free-form markdown — no structural restrictions, but Skill Forge follows a consistent section order (see skill-sections.md in create-skill/data/).
44	
45	### Optional: Supporting Directories
46	
47	```
48	skill-name/
49	├── SKILL.md              # Instructions and API reference
50	├── scripts/              # Executable automation
51	├── references/           # Detailed reference material
52	└── assets/               # Templates, schemas, configs
53	```
54	
55	All subdirectories are exactly one level deep. Files are loaded on demand when SKILL.md directs — never automatically.
56	
57	Scripts and assets extracted by Skill Forge inherit provenance from their source repository. Each file receives a `[SRC:{source_path}:L1]` citation (T1-low confidence) and a SHA-256 content hash for drift detection. Scripts must follow the quality principles in the Script Quality section below. Assets are static files loaded on demand — agents use them as directed by SKILL.md instructions.
58	
59	## Progressive Disclosure Model
60	
61	The format implements a three-phase loading model:
62	
63	| Phase | What Loads | Token Cost | When |
64	| --- | --- | --- | --- |
65	| Discovery | `name` + `description` from frontmatter | ~50-100 tokens | Agent startup, all skills |
66	| Activation | Full `SKILL.md` body | < 5000 tokens (~500 lines guideline) | Task matches skill description |
67	| Execution | Files from `scripts/`, `references/`, `assets/` | Variable | SKILL.md directs agent to load |
68	
69	## Pattern Examples
70	
71	### Example 1: Trigger-Optimized Description
72	
73	**Context:** Writing a skill description that agents can match to user tasks.
74	
75	**Implementation:**
76	```yaml
77	description: >
78	  Extract and transform data from PostgreSQL databases using pg client.
79	  Use for database queries, schema inspection, connection management,
80	  and migration execution. NOT for: MongoDB, Redis, or other non-SQL stores.
81	  NOT for: ORM-level abstractions (use typeorm-skill or prisma-skill instead).
82	```
83	
84	**Key Points:**
85	- Leads with what the skill does (positive triggers)
86	- Includes negative triggers to prevent false matches
87	- References alternative skills for excluded use cases
88	- Specific keywords: "PostgreSQL", "pg client", "schema inspection"
89	
90	### Example 2: Just-in-Time Resource Loading
91	
92	**Context:** A skill needs to reference a complex schema during execution.
93	
94	**Implementation:**
95	```markdown
96	## Configuration
97	
98	The library accepts a configuration object matching the schema in
99	`references/config-schema.md`. Load that file now to validate
100	the user's configuration against the expected structure.
101	```
102	
103	**Key Points:**
104	- The directive is explicit: "Load that file now"
105	- Agents do not pre-load reference files — they follow the instruction when they reach it
106	- Relative paths use forward slashes, one level deep only
107	
108	### Example 3: Procedural Instructions for Agents
109	
110	**Context:** Writing skill instructions that agents execute deterministically.
111	
112	**Implementation:**
113	```markdown
114	## Setup
115	
116	1. Check if `pg` is installed: run `npm list pg` in the project directory.
117	2. If not installed, run `npm install pg`.
118	3. Locate the database configuration:
119	   - Check `src/config/database.ts` first
120	   - If not found, check environment variables: `DATABASE_URL`, `PG_HOST`
121	   - If neither exists, ask the user for connection details
122	4. Validate the connection by running the health check in `scripts/health-check.sql`.
123	```
124	
125	**Key Points:**
126	- Step-by-step numbering with decision trees
127	- Third-person imperative voice ("Check if..." not "You should check...")
128	- Concrete file paths and commands, not vague guidance
129	- Fallback paths explicitly mapped
130	
131	### Example 4: Skill Forge Compliance Checks
132	
133	**Context:** The validate step in create-skill checks agentskills.io compliance.
134	
135	**Implementation:** Validation covers:
136	1. Frontmatter present with required `name` and `description` fields
137	2. `name` matches parent directory name and formatting rules
138	3. `description` length within 1-1024 characters
139	4. SKILL.md body under 500 lines (warning, not failure)
140	5. All relative paths resolve to existing files
141	6. No deeply nested subdirectories (max one level)
142	
143	**Key Points:**
144	- Compliance is checked during create-skill, not as a post-hoc audit
145	- Line count is a guideline — exceeding 500 lines produces a warning
146	- Path validation prevents broken references in the published skill
147	
148	## MCP Tool References
149	
150	If a skill references MCP (Model Context Protocol) tools, always use fully qualified names to avoid "tool not found" errors: `ServerName:tool_name` (e.g., `BigQuery:bigquery_schema`, `GitHub:create_issue`). Without the server prefix, agents may fail to locate the tool when multiple MCP servers are available.
151	
152	## Script Quality
153	
154	Skills that include executable scripts in `scripts/` must follow these principles:
155	
156	- **Solve, don't punt.** Scripts handle errors explicitly rather than failing and leaving the agent to figure it out. Provide fallback behavior, descriptive error messages, and recovery paths.
157	- **No voodoo constants.** Every magic number or configuration value must be justified with a comment explaining why that value was chosen. If you don't know the right value, the agent won't either.
158	- **Descriptive error output.** Write error messages to stdout/stderr that enable agents to self-correct without user intervention (e.g., "Field 'signature_date' not found. Available fields: customer_name, order_total").
159	
160	## Development Methodology
161	
162	- **Evaluation-driven development.** Define 2-3 concrete use cases and realistic test prompts before building the skill. Build evaluations before writing extensive documentation — this ensures the skill solves real problems rather than documenting imagined ones.
163	- **Realistic test prompts.** Test with prompts the way real users actually talk — with typos, casual abbreviations, and incomplete context. A skill tested only with clean prompts will break in unexpected ways in production.
164	- **Iterative refinement.** Observe how agents navigate and use the skill. Watch for unexpected exploration paths, missed references, overreliance on certain sections, and ignored content. Iterate based on observed behavior, not assumptions.
165	
166	## Anti-Patterns
167	
168	- Writing skills as documentation (README-style) instead of procedural agent instructions
169	- Deeply nested directory structures — one level maximum from skill root
170	- Vague descriptions ("helps with databases") — descriptions must be specific and trigger-optimized
171	- Including redundant content that duplicates what the agent already knows (standard language features, basic CLI commands)
172	- Bundling large library code inside skills — skills reference existing tools, not replace them
173	- Time-sensitive instructions ("If before August 2025, use the old API") — use versioned sections instead
174	- Offering too many tool/library options without a clear default — provide one recommended approach with escape hatches for edge cases
175	- Inconsistent terminology — mixing synonyms ("API endpoint", "URL", "route") confuses agent execution
176	
177	## Related Fragments
178	
179	- [skill-lifecycle.md](skill-lifecycle.md) — where agentskills.io compliance fits in the pipeline
180	- [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md) — how citations appear within the formatted output
181	- [zero-hallucination.md](zero-hallucination.md) — the integrity principle that shapes skill content
182	
183	_Source: synthesized from agentskills.io specification, what-are-skills.mdx, integrate-skills.mdx, and Best Practices for Creating Agent Skills_
184	
```

1	# Skill Lifecycle
2	
3	## Principle
4	
5	The 12 SKF workflows form an end-to-end pipeline from source discovery through verified export, with a pre-code architecture verification path. Each workflow produces artifacts consumed by downstream workflows. Understanding the lifecycle enables Ferris to recommend the right workflow for a user's situation and to maintain artifact continuity across the pipeline.
6	
7	## Rationale
8	
9	Skills are not created in a single step — they move through discovery, design, compilation, verification, and distribution phases. Each phase has dedicated workflows with specific inputs and outputs. The lifecycle model prevents users from skipping critical steps and ensures artifacts flow cleanly between workflows.
10	
11	Without lifecycle awareness:
12	- Users jump to create-skill without proper analysis or briefing
13	- Generated skills lack provenance because upstream artifacts were skipped
14	- Export packages reference stale or untested skills
15	
16	With lifecycle awareness:
17	- Ferris recommends the appropriate entry point based on the user's context
18	- Artifact dependencies are explicit — each workflow declares its inputs
19	- The full pipeline produces verified, export-ready skills with complete provenance
20	
21	## Pipeline Phases
22	
23	| Phase | Workflows | Purpose | Artifacts Produced |
24	| --- | --- | --- | --- |
25	| Setup | SF (Setup Forge) | Detect environment and set capability tier | `forge-tier.yaml` |
26	| Discovery | AN (Analyze Source) | Scan project, identify skillable units | Analysis report, skill briefs |
27	| Design | BS (Brief Skill) | Interactive scope definition for one skill | `skill-brief.yaml` |
28	| Compilation | CS, QS, SS (Create/Quick/Stack) | Extract source and compile skill | `SKILL.md`, metadata, provenance, `scripts/`, `assets/` (when source contains them) |
29	| Architecture Verification | VS, RA (Verify Stack, Refine Architecture) | Pre-code feasibility check and architecture refinement | Feasibility report, refined architecture doc |
30	| Maintenance | US, AS (Update/Audit) | Detect drift and refresh skills | Updated `SKILL.md`, drift report |
31	| Verification | TS (Test Skill) | Quality gate — completeness scoring | Test report, pass/fail decision |
32	| Distribution | EX (Export Skill) | Package and inject into agent context | agentskills.io bundle, snippets |
33	
34	## Typical Flows
35	
36	### First-Time Setup (Brownfield Project)
37	
38	```
39	SF → AN → CS (--batch) → TS → EX
40	```
41	
42	1. **SF** detects tools, writes forge tier
43	2. **AN** scans the project, identifies skillable units, generates skill briefs
44	3. **CS** compiles skills from briefs (batch mode processes all briefs)
45	4. **TS** runs completeness verification on each skill
46	5. **EX** packages passing skills for distribution
47	
48	### Single Skill (Full Quality)
49	
50	```
51	SF → BS → CS → TS → EX
52	```
53	
54	1. **SF** ensures environment is configured
55	2. **BS** guides the user through scope definition, produces a validated brief
56	3. **CS** compiles the skill with full provenance
57	4. **TS** verifies completeness
58	5. **EX** packages for distribution
59	
60	### Single Skill (Fast)
61	
62	```
63	SF → QS → EX
64	```
65	
66	1. **SF** ensures environment is configured
67	2. **QS** resolves a package name or URL to source and compiles directly — no brief needed
68	3. **EX** packages for distribution (optional TS between QS and EX for quality assurance)
69	
70	### Pre-Code Stack Verification
71	
72	```
73	SF → CS×N (per library) → VS → RA → SS (compose) → TS → EX
74	```
75	
76	1. **SF** ensures environment is configured
77	2. **CS** compiles individual skills for each library in the stack
78	3. **VS** cross-references skills against architecture and PRD — produces a feasibility report with verdicts
79	4. **RA** refines the architecture document using skill API evidence and VS findings
80	5. **SS** (compose-mode) synthesizes the stack skill from individual skills + refined architecture
81	6. **TS** verifies the composed stack skill
82	7. **EX** packages for distribution
83	
84	### Maintenance
85	
86	```
87	AS → US → TS → EX
88	```
89	
90	1. **AS** detects drift between skill and current source
91	2. **US** re-extracts changed exports and merges, preserving \[MANUAL\] sections
92	3. **TS** re-verifies the updated skill
93	4. **EX** re-exports the updated package
94	
95	## Pattern Examples
96	
97	### Example 1: Workflow Selection Decision
98	
99	**Context:** A user asks Ferris to create a skill for a specific library.
100	
101	**Implementation:** Ferris evaluates the situation:
102	
103	| Condition | Recommended Flow |
104	| --- | --- |
105	| User knows exactly what to skill, has a brief | CS directly |
106	| User knows the library but needs scope guidance | BS → CS |
107	| User has a package name, wants fast results | QS |
108	| User wants to skill their entire project | AN → CS (batch) |
109	| User has an existing skill that may be outdated | AS → US |
110	| User wants to verify tech stack before building | CS×N → VS → RA → SS (compose) |
111	
112	**Key Points:**
113	- SF is always prerequisite (but only needs to run once per project)
114	- Quick skill (QS) skips briefing — appropriate for well-known packages
115	- Analyze source (AN) is the brownfield entry point for large projects
116	
117	### Example 2: Artifact Flow Between Workflows
118	
119	**Context:** Tracking how artifacts flow through the pipeline.
120	
121	**Implementation:**
122	```
123	SF → forge-tier.yaml
124	       ↓ (read by all subsequent workflows)
125	AN → analysis-report.md + skill-brief.yaml[]
126	       ↓ (briefs consumed by CS)
127	CS → SKILL.md + metadata.json + provenance-map.json + scripts/ + assets/ (when present) + evidence-report.md
128	       ↓ (skill consumed by TS)
129	TS → test-report.md (pass/fail gate)
130	       ↓ (passing skill consumed by EX)
131	EX → agentskills.io bundle + context snippets
132	
133	VS → feasibility-report-{project_name}.md (verdict + integration verdicts)
134	       ↓ (report consumed by RA)
135	RA → refined-architecture-{project_name}.md (gaps filled, issues flagged, improvements suggested)
136	       ↓ (refined doc consumed by SS compose-mode)
137	SS (compose) → SKILL.md (stack skill synthesized from individual skills + architecture)
138	```
139	
140	**Key Points:**
141	- `forge-tier.yaml` is the universal dependency — all workflows read it
142	- Skill briefs are the handoff between discovery/design and compilation
143	- TS acts as a gate — failing skills do not proceed to EX
144	- VS/RA form a pre-code verification loop — rerun after architecture changes
145	
146	### Example 3: Stack Skill vs. Individual Skills
147	
148	**Context:** A user wants to document how multiple libraries work together in their project.
149	
150	**Implementation:** Two approaches serve different needs:
151	- **Individual skills** (via CS or QS): One skill per library, each with its own provenance and lifecycle. Best for reusable skills that apply across projects.
152	- **Stack skill** (via SS): A single consolidated skill documenting how libraries connect. Detects co-import patterns and integration surfaces. Best for project-specific context.
153	
154	**Key Points:**
155	- Stack skills and individual skills are complementary, not competing
156	- SS detects integration patterns (e.g., "express + passport always used together for auth routes")
157	- Individual skills track per-library provenance; stack skills track inter-library relationships
158	
159	## Integration Points
160	
161	- **Setup Forge** must run before any other workflow — it establishes the tier
162	- **Analyze Source** and **Brief Skill** are alternative entry points to compilation
163	- **Test Skill** is optional but recommended — the quality gate before export
164	- **Audit Skill** and **Update Skill** form the maintenance loop
165	- **Verify Stack** and **Refine Architecture** form the pre-code verification path
166	- **Export Skill** is the terminal workflow — it produces the distributable artifact
167	
168	## Related Fragments
169	
170	- [progressive-capability.md](progressive-capability.md) — how the forge tier affects each pipeline phase
171	- [agentskills-spec.md](agentskills-spec.md) — the output format that export-skill packages
172	- [provenance-tracking.md](provenance-tracking.md) — how provenance flows through the pipeline
173	
174	_Source: synthesized from all 12 workflow.md files (including VS, RA) and module-help.csv_
175	
```

```
1	# Provenance Tracking
2	
3	## Principle
4	
5	Every claim in a generated skill has a recorded provenance chain — from the assertion in the skill back to the source location, extraction method, and confidence tier. Provenance is stored in machine-readable format (`provenance-map.json`) alongside human-readable evidence reports, making claims independently verifiable.
6	
7	## Rationale
8	
9	Skills make hundreds of individual claims: function signatures, parameter types, default values, behavioral descriptions. Without provenance, there is no way to verify whether a claim is current, to detect drift when source changes, or to trace a broken instruction back to its origin.
10	
11	Without provenance tracking:
12	- Audit workflows cannot compare skill claims against current source
13	- Update workflows cannot identify which claims need re-extraction
14	- Users cannot distinguish verified claims from stale or fabricated ones
15	
16	With provenance tracking:
17	- Every claim maps to a specific source location and extraction method
18	- Audit workflows perform precise structural and semantic diffing
19	- Update workflows selectively re-extract only changed claims
20	
21	## Provenance Artifacts
22	
23	| Artifact | Format | Purpose | Generated By |
24	| --- | --- | --- | --- |
25	| `provenance-map.json` | JSON | Machine-readable export-to-source mapping | CS, QS, SS |
26	| `evidence-report.md` | Markdown | Human-readable extraction summary | CS, QS, SS |
27	| Inline citations | `[TAG:path:Lnn]` | Per-claim source reference in SKILL.md | CS, QS, SS, US |
28	| `drift-report.md` | Markdown | Findings from source comparison | AS |
29	
30	## Pattern Examples
31	
32	### Example 1: Provenance Map Structure
33	
34	**Context:** The create-skill workflow generates a `provenance-map.json` alongside each skill.
35	
36	**Implementation:**
37	```json
38	{
39	  "skill_name": "express-skill",
40	  "source_repo": "https://github.com/expressjs/express",
41	  "source_commit": "abc1234",
42	  "generated_at": "2025-01-15T10:30:00Z",
43	  "entries": [
44	    {
45	      "export_name": "createServer",
46	      "export_type": "function",
47	      "params": ["options: ServerOptions"],
48	      "return_type": "Server",
49	      "source_file": "src/server.ts",
50	      "source_line": 23,
51	      "confidence": "T1",
52	      "extraction_method": "ast-grep",
53	      "ast_node_type": "export_statement > function_declaration"
54	    },
55	    {
56	      "export_name": "parseConfig",
57	      "export_type": "function",
58	      "params": ["input"],
59	      "return_type": null,
60	      "source_file": "src/config.ts",
61	      "source_line": 45,
62	      "confidence": "T1-low",
63	      "extraction_method": "source-read",
64	      "ast_node_type": null
65	    }
66	  ]
67	}
68	```
69	
70	**Key Points:**
71	- Each entry uses structured fields (`export_name`, `params[]`, `return_type`) for incremental updates
72	- `ast_node_type` is null for non-AST extractions — never fabricated
73	- `extraction_method` distinguishes how the entry was obtained
74	- Confidence tier is recorded per-entry, not per-file
75	- `params` is an array of typed strings (e.g., `"userId: string"`) — enables param-by-param diffing
76	
77	### Example 2: Evidence Report
78	
79	**Context:** The evidence report provides a human-readable summary of the extraction.
80	
81	**Implementation:**
82	```markdown
83	# Evidence Report: express-skill
84	
85	## Extraction Summary
86	- **Tier:** Forge
87	- **Tool versions:** ast-grep 0.25.0
88	- **Source files scanned:** 12
89	- **Exports extracted:** 47
90	- **Confidence distribution:** T1: 42, T1-low: 5, T2: 0, T3: 0
91	
92	## Validation Results
93	- Signature accuracy: all T1 claims verified against AST
94	- Coverage: 47/52 public exports documented (5 internal re-exports excluded)
95	- Gaps: 3 exports with incomplete type information (marked T1-low)
96	
97	## Extraction Log
98	| Export | File | Line | Confidence | Notes |
99	| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
100	| createServer | src/server.ts | 23 | T1 | Full signature with generics |
101	| parseConfig | src/config.ts | 45 | T1-low | Type annotation missing |
102	| ... | ... | ... | ... | ... |
103	```
104	
105	**Key Points:**
106	- Tool versions recorded for reproducibility
107	- Coverage calculated against actual public exports, not estimated
108	- Gaps explicitly listed — they feed into test-skill scoring
109	
110	### Example 3: Audit Drift Detection Using Provenance
111	
112	**Context:** The audit-skill workflow uses the provenance map to detect drift.
113	
114	**Implementation:** The audit process:
115	1. Load the existing `provenance-map.json`
116	2. Re-index current source to build a fresh reference (same extraction method)
117	3. For each entry in the provenance map, compare against fresh reference:
118	   - **Signature changed** → CRITICAL or HIGH severity
119	   - **Export removed** → CRITICAL severity
120	   - **New export undocumented** → HIGH (if > 3) or MEDIUM (if 1-3)
121	   - **Implementation changed, API stable** → MEDIUM severity
122	   - **Style/whitespace only** → LOW severity
123	4. Produce a drift report with severity classifications
124	
125	**Key Points:**
126	- Provenance enables precise diffing — compare specific claims, not entire files
127	- Severity classification uses the rules from audit-skill/data/severity-rules.md
128	- The drift report feeds into the update-skill workflow as re-extraction targets
129	
130	### Example 4: Provenance Preservation During Updates
131	
132	**Context:** The update-skill workflow merges fresh extractions with existing provenance.
133	
134	**Implementation:**
135	1. Load existing provenance map
136	2. Identify claims that need re-extraction (from audit drift report or source change detection)
137	3. Re-extract only affected claims using current tier tools
138	4. Merge fresh provenance entries into the existing map:
139	   - Changed entries: replace with new provenance
140	   - Unchanged entries: preserve existing provenance (timestamp unchanged)
141	   - Removed exports: remove from provenance map
142	   - New exports: add with fresh provenance
143	5. Regenerate evidence report reflecting the update
144	
145	**Key Points:**
146	- Selective re-extraction — only changed entries are re-processed
147	- Provenance timestamps show when each entry was last verified
148	- The merge preserves \[MANUAL\] section provenance (author: "manual", no source location)
149	
150	### File-Level Provenance (file_entries)
151	
152	Scripts and assets use a separate `file_entries` array in `provenance-map.json`, distinct from the export-level `entries` array:
153	
154	| Field | Description |
155	|-------|-------------|
156	| `file_name` | Path relative to skill root (e.g., `scripts/validate.sh`) |
157	| `file_type` | `script` or `asset` |
158	| `source_file` | Path relative to source root |
159	| `confidence` | Always `T1-low` (file-copy, not AST-verified) |
160	| `extraction_method` | Always `file-copy` |
161	| `content_hash` | SHA-256 of file content (for drift detection) |
162	
163	## Inline Citation Rules
164	
165	Citations appear directly in SKILL.md content:
166	- Place citations at the end of the relevant line or block
167	- Use the citation format matching the confidence tier (see [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md))
168	- Multiple citations on the same claim are ordered by confidence: T1 > T1-low > T2 > T3
169	- Citations in \[MANUAL\] sections are author-provided and not managed by the forge
170	
171	## Anti-Patterns
172	
173	- Generating provenance-map.json with fabricated line numbers — line numbers must come from actual extraction
174	- Copying provenance from a previous extraction without re-verifying — timestamps must reflect actual verification time
175	- Omitting the evidence report — it is required for every compilation, not optional
176	- Using provenance from one source file version against a different version — provenance is version-bound
177	
178	## Related Fragments
179	
180	- [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md) — defines the citation formats used in provenance
181	- [zero-hallucination.md](zero-hallucination.md) — the principle that provenance enforces
182	- [manual-section-integrity.md](manual-section-integrity.md) — how \[MANUAL\] sections interact with provenance
183	
184	_Source: consolidated from create-skill steps 03/05/07, audit-skill steps 02/03, and skill-sections.md_
185	
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/version-paths.md",
  "content": "# Version-Aware Paths\n\n## Principle\n\nSkills are stored in a version-nested directory structure that allows multiple versions to coexist. Each version directory contains a self-contained agentskills.io-compliant skill package. All workflows resolve skill paths through canonical templates defined here, ensuring consistency across the pipeline and compatibility with `skill.sh` / `npx skills` distribution tooling.\n\n## Rationale\n\nWithout version-aware paths:\n- Updating a skill for cognee v0.6.0 overwrites the v0.5.0 skill — users pinned to v0.5.0 lose their instructions\n- Audit and provenance comparisons cannot span version boundaries\n- Skills cannot be distributed to registries that serve multiple versions\n\nWith version-aware paths:\n- Multiple versions coexist under `{skill-name}/` — no data loss on update\n- Provenance, evidence, and test reports are preserved per-version\n- The inner `{skill-name}/` directory is a standalone agentskills.io package, directly installable via `npx skills add`\n\n## Path Templates\n\nAll workflows MUST use these templates when constructing paths. Never hardcode paths to skill artifacts.\n\n### skills_output_folder (Deliverables)\n\n| Template | Resolves To | Usage |\n|----------|------------|-------|\n| `{skill_package}` | `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/` | Skill package root — where SKILL.md, metadata.json, context-snippet.md, references/, scripts/, assets/ live |\n| `{skill_group}` | `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/` | Parent directory for all versions of a skill |\n| `{active_skill}` | `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/active/{skill-name}/` | Resolved via `active` symlink — stable path to the current version |\n\n### forge_data_folder (Workspace Artifacts)\n\n| Template | Resolves To | Usage |\n|----------|------------|-------|\n| `{forge_version}` | `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/{version}/` | Version-specific workspace artifacts — provenance-map.json, evidence-report.md, extraction-rules.yaml, test-report |\n| `{forge_group}` | `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/` | Parent directory — contains skill-brief.yaml (version-independent) and version subdirectories |\n\n### Platform Paths (Unchanged — Flat)\n\n| Platform | Root Path |\n|----------|-----------|\n| `claude` | `.claude/skills/{skill-name}/` |\n| `cursor` | `.cursor/skills/{skill-name}/` |\n| `copilot` | `.agents/skills/{skill-name}/` |\n| _(legacy)_ | `skills/{skill-name}/` |\n\nPlatform paths are **not versioned**. The export workflow resolves the active version from the manifest and references its `{skill_package}` when building the managed section. The snippet `root:` always uses the flat platform path.\n\n## Directory Structure\n\n### skills_output_folder\n\n```\n{skills_output_folder}/\n  .export-manifest.json\n  {skill-name}/\n    active -> {version}\n    {version}/\n      {skill-name}/\n        SKILL.md\n        metadata.json\n        context-snippet.md\n        references/\n        scripts/\n        assets/\n    {older-version}/\n      {skill-name}/\n        ...\n  {project}-stack/\n    active -> {version}\n    {version}/\n      {project}-stack/\n        SKILL.md\n        metadata.json\n        context-snippet.md\n        references/\n          integrations/\n```\n\nThe inner `{skill-name}/` directory IS the agentskills.io-compliant skill package. The version directory is an organizational wrapper. The `name` field in SKILL.md frontmatter matches the inner directory name — spec compliance is preserved.\n\n### forge_data_folder\n\n```\n{forge_data_folder}/\n  {skill-name}/\n    skill-brief.yaml\n    {version}/\n      provenance-map.json\n      evidence-report.md\n      extraction-rules.yaml\n      test-report-{skill-name}.md\n```\n\n`skill-brief.yaml` stays at `{forge_group}` level — the brief is a workflow input that defines extraction scope, not a versioned output.\n\n## Version Resolution\n\n### Writing Workflows (CS, QS, SS, US)\n\nWhen writing artifacts, resolve `{version}` from the skill brief's `version` field (CS, SS), the extraction inventory (QS), or the updated metadata (US). Then:\n\n1. Create `{skill_group}` if it does not exist\n2. Create `{skill_package}` (including all parent directories)\n3. Write all deliverables to `{skill_package}`\n4. Create `{forge_version}` if it does not exist\n5. Write all workspace artifacts to `{forge_version}`\n6. Create or update the `active` symlink at `{skill_group}/active` pointing to `{version}`\n\n### Reading Workflows (EX, AS, TS, VS, RA)\n\nWhen reading artifacts, resolve the skill path using the export manifest:\n\n1. Read `{skills_output_folder}/.export-manifest.json`\n2. Look up the skill name in `exports`\n3. Read `active_version` to get the target version\n4. Resolve to `{skill_package}` using the active version\n5. If manifest does not contain the skill: check for `active` symlink at `{skill_group}/active`\n6. If neither manifest nor symlink: fall back to flat-path resolution (migration — see below)\n\n### Manifest-Driven Snippet Scanning (EX Step-04)\n\nReplace the glob-based snippet scan (`{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md`) with manifest-driven resolution:\n\n1. Read export manifest\n2. For each skill in the exported skill set: resolve `active_version` to get `{skill_package}`\n3. Read `{skill_package}/context-snippet.md`\n4. Filter and assemble as before\n\n## Export Manifest v2\n\nThe export manifest gains version awareness:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"schema_version\": \"2\",\n  \"exports\": {\n    \"skill-name\": {\n      \"active_version\": \"0.6.0\",\n      \"versions\": {\n        \"0.5.0\": {\n          \"platforms\": [\"claude\"],\n          \"last_exported\": \"2026-03-15\",\n          \"status\": \"archived\"\n        },\n        \"0.6.0\": {\n          \"platforms\": [\"claude\", \"copilot\"],\n          \"last_exported\": \"2026-04-04\",\n          \"status\": \"active\"\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**Fields:**\n- `schema_version`: `\"2\"` — enables v1-to-v2 migration detection\n- `active_version`: The version whose `{skill_package}` supplies the context snippet for the managed section. Must match exactly one version with `status: \"active\"`\n- `versions.{v}.status`: `\"active\"` (currently exported), `\"archived\"` (previously exported, retained on disk), `\"draft\"` (created but never exported)\n- `versions.{v}.platforms`: Array of platforms this version was last exported to\n- `versions.{v}.last_exported`: ISO date of the last export\n\n**Only one version per skill can have `status: \"active\"` at any time.**\n\n## Version Sanitization\n\nDirectory names use the semver version with `+{build}` metadata stripped:\n\n| Source Version | Directory Name | Rule |\n|---------------|---------------|------|\n| `1.0.0` | `1.0.0` | Clean — no transformation |\n| `0.5.0-beta.1` | `0.5.0-beta.1` | Pre-release preserved |\n| `1.0.0-rc.2+build.456` | `1.0.0-rc.2` | Build metadata stripped per semver spec |\n| `2.0.0+20260404` | `2.0.0` | Build metadata stripped |\n\nBuild metadata does not affect version precedence per the semver specification and is stripped to avoid filesystem issues with the `+` character.\n\n## Migration: Flat to Versioned\n\nWhen a reading workflow encounters a skill at the flat path (`{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/SKILL.md` exists directly — no version subdirectory), it auto-migrates:\n\n1. Read `metadata.json` from the flat path to get the `version` field\n2. Create the versioned directory: `{skill_group}/{version}/{skill-name}/`\n3. Move all package files (SKILL.md, metadata.json, context-snippet.md, references/, scripts/, assets/) into the versioned location\n4. Create the `active` symlink: `{skill_group}/active -> {version}`\n5. If `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/` contains provenance artifacts at the flat level (not in a version subdirectory):\n   - Create `{forge_version}`\n   - Move provenance-map.json, evidence-report.md, extraction-rules.yaml, test-report into `{forge_version}`\n   - Leave skill-brief.yaml at `{forge_group}` (it is already version-independent)\n6. If `.export-manifest.json` exists and lacks `schema_version`:\n   - Migrate to v2 schema: wrap existing entries with `active_version` and `versions` structure\n   - Set `schema_version: \"2\"`\n7. Report migration to user: \"Migrated {skill-name} from flat to versioned layout ({version})\"\n\n**Migration preserves all content** — [MANUAL] sections, provenance, evidence reports, and scripts/assets are moved, not re-generated.\n\n## Anti-Patterns\n\n- Hardcoding `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/SKILL.md` without version resolution — always use `{skill_package}` template\n- Storing `skill-brief.yaml` inside a version directory — the brief is version-independent\n- Versioning platform root paths — platform paths stay flat, version lives in the forge workspace\n- Using glob patterns to discover snippets across all versions — use the export manifest to resolve the active version\n- Creating version directories with `+` in the name — strip build metadata\n\n## Related Fragments\n\n- [agentskills-spec.md](agentskills-spec.md) — the format that `{skill_package}` contents must comply with\n- [skill-lifecycle.md](skill-lifecycle.md) — how versioned artifacts flow through the pipeline\n- [provenance-tracking.md](provenance-tracking.md) — provenance is version-bound and stored per-version in `{forge_version}`\n"
}
```

1	# SKF Knowledge Overview
2	
3	## Principle
4	
5	Agent-level knowledge files capture cross-cutting principles and patterns that Ferris applies across multiple workflows. They are distinct from workflow `data/` files, which contain step-specific operational artifacts. Knowledge is loaded just-in-time when a workflow step directs — never preloaded into context.
6	
7	## Rationale
8	
9	The SKF module has 12 workflows containing numerous operational `data/` files embedded in step directories. These data files define schemas, templates, scoring formulas, and extraction rules tightly coupled to individual steps. But several principles — zero hallucination, confidence tiers, provenance — cut across multiple workflows and need a single authoritative source rather than fragmented repetition.
10	
11	Without a knowledge folder:
12	- Cross-cutting principles get duplicated across workflow steps
13	- Updates to foundational rules require changes in multiple locations
14	- Ferris has no centralized reference for judgment calls that span workflows
15	
16	With knowledge files:
17	- Each principle has one canonical definition
18	- Workflow steps reference knowledge files instead of re-stating rules
19	- Ferris loads only the knowledge relevant to the current task
20	
21	## Knowledge Map
22	
23	| File                                                       | Purpose                                                           | Key Workflows                      |
24	|------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------|
25	| [zero-hallucination.md](zero-hallucination.md)             | Every claim traces to source; uncitable content excluded          | AN, BS, CS, QS, SS, US, AS, TS, RA |
26	| [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md)                 | T1/T1-low/T2/T3 trust model and citation formats                  | AN, BS, CS, QS, SS, US, AS, TS, EX, RA, VS |
27	| [progressive-capability.md](progressive-capability.md)     | Quick/Forge/Forge+/Deep tier philosophy and behavior adaptation          | SF, AN, BS, CS, QS, SS, US, AS, TS, EX, VS, RA |
28	| [agentskills-spec.md](agentskills-spec.md)                 | agentskills.io output format principles and compliance            | CS, QS, SS, US, TS, EX             |
29	| [skill-lifecycle.md](skill-lifecycle.md)                   | End-to-end pipeline, artifact flow, workflow selection            | All 12                             |
30	| [provenance-tracking.md](provenance-tracking.md)           | Provenance-map.json entries and file_entries, evidence reports, claim traceability | CS, QS, SS, US, AS, TS             |
31	| [manual-section-integrity.md](manual-section-integrity.md) | \[MANUAL\] marker preservation and merge algorithm                | CS, US, AS, TS, EX                 |
32	| [qmd-registry.md](qmd-registry.md)                         | Progressive QMD collection registry and collection gate principle | SF, BS, CS, US, AS                 |
33	| [ccc-bridge.md](ccc-bridge.md)                             | ccc_bridge interface, indexing lifecycle, Forge+ discovery-before-extraction | SF, AN, CS, SS, US, AS             |
34	| [tool-resolution.md](tool-resolution.md)                   | Bridge-to-tool and subprocess-to-tool mapping per IDE environment | CS, QS, SS, US, AS, AN, BS, RA     |
35	| [architecture-verification.md](architecture-verification.md) | Pre-code verification pipeline, integration verdicts, iteration loop | VS, RA, SS                        |
36	| [doc-fetcher.md](doc-fetcher.md)                           | T3 external documentation fetching, quarantine rules, tool-agnostic | CS, QS                             |
37	| [split-body-strategy.md](split-body-strategy.md)           | Selective vs full split-body, detection pattern, agent accuracy   | CS, QS, US, TS, EX                |
38	| [overview.md](overview.md)                                 | Knowledge map and JiT loading protocol (this file)               | All (entry point)                  |
39	
40	## JiT Loading Protocol
41	
42	1. Ferris receives a task or workflow activation
43	2. Consult `skf-knowledge-index.csv` to identify relevant knowledge fragments by tags or description. The `tier` column classifies fragments as `core` (always relevant when the domain applies) or `extended` (load only when the specific scenario is active — e.g., `doc-fetcher.md` only when `doc_urls` are present in the brief)
44	3. Load only the files needed for the current task — never bulk-load all knowledge
45	4. Apply knowledge principles during workflow execution alongside step-specific `data/` files
46	
47	## Knowledge vs. Data
48	
49	| Aspect    | Knowledge Files (`knowledge/`)       | Data Files (`data/`)                    |
50	|-----------|--------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------|
51	| Scope     | Cross-cutting, multi-workflow        | Single workflow, single step            |
52	| Content   | Principles, patterns, judgment rules | Schemas, templates, scoring formulas    |
53	| Loading   | JiT via index lookup                 | JiT via step instruction                |
54	| Examples  | Zero hallucination, confidence tiers | skill-brief-schema.md, scoring-rules.md |
55	| Stability | Rarely changes                       | Changes with workflow evolution         |
56	
57	## Related Fragments
58	
59	- [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md) — the trust model that underpins extraction and scoring
60	- [skill-lifecycle.md](skill-lifecycle.md) — the end-to-end pipeline connecting all workflows
61	- [progressive-capability.md](progressive-capability.md) — tier philosophy that affects every workflow
62	
63	_Source: synthesized from module.yaml, README.md, and workflow README_
64

```
1	# SKF Workflows
2	
3	Twelve workflows covering the full skill lifecycle — from source discovery through verified export, with pre-code architecture verification.
4	
5	All workflows are triggered via Ferris (`@Ferris <code>`) and execute as step-by-step sequences with just-in-time step loading.
6	
7	## Workflow Index
8	
9	| Code | Workflow | Steps | Mode | Purpose |
10	| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
11	| SF | [setup-forge](setup-forge/workflow.md) | 5 | Architect | Initialize environment, detect tools, set capability tier |
12	| AN | [analyze-source](analyze-source/workflow.md) | 7 | Architect | Discover skillable units in a repo, produce recommended briefs |
13	| BS | [brief-skill](brief-skill/workflow.md) | 5 | Architect | Design a skill scope through guided discovery |
14	| CS | [create-skill](create-skill/workflow.md) | 8 | Architect | Compile a skill from brief with provenance (supports --batch) |
15	| QS | [quick-skill](quick-skill/workflow.md) | 6 | Architect | Fast skill from package name or GitHub URL — no brief needed |
16	| SS | [create-stack-skill](create-stack-skill/workflow.md) | 9 | Architect | Consolidated project stack skill with integration patterns (code-mode + compose-mode) |
17	| VS | [verify-stack](verify-stack/workflow.md) | 6 | Audit | Validate tech stack feasibility against architecture and PRD |
18	| RA | [refine-architecture](refine-architecture/workflow.md) | 6 | Architect | Improve architecture doc using verified skill data |
19	| US | [update-skill](update-skill/workflow.md) | 7 | Surgeon | Regenerate after source changes, preserving \[MANUAL\] sections |
20	| AS | [audit-skill](audit-skill/workflow.md) | 6 | Audit | Drift detection between skill and current source code |
21	| TS | [test-skill](test-skill/workflow.md) | 7 | Audit | Cognitive completeness verification — quality gate before export |
22	| EX | [export-skill](export-skill/workflow.md) | 6 | Delivery | Package for distribution, inject into CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md |
23	
24	## Typical Flows
25	
26	**First-time setup:**
27	
28	```
29	SF → AN → CS (batch) → TS → EX
30	```
31	
32	**Single skill (full quality):**
33	
34	```
35	SF → BS → CS → TS → EX
36	```
37	
38	**Single skill (fast):**
39	
40	```
41	SF → QS → EX
42	```
43	
44	**Pre-code stack verification:**
45	
46	```
47	SF → CS×N (per library) → VS → RA → SS (compose) → TS → EX
48	```
49	
50	**Maintenance:**
51	
52	```
53	AS → US → TS → EX
54	```
55	
56	## Knowledge vs. Data
57	
58	The module uses two kinds of reference material, loaded just-in-time during workflow execution:
59	
60	| Aspect | Knowledge (`knowledge/`) | Data (`{workflow}/data/`) |
61	| --- | --- | --- |
62	| Scope | Cross-cutting, multi-workflow | Single workflow, single step |
63	| Content | Principles, patterns, judgment rules | Schemas, templates, scoring formulas |
64	| Index | `skf-knowledge-index.csv` | Referenced directly by step files |
65	| Examples | zero-hallucination, confidence-tiers | skill-brief-schema.md, scoring-rules.md |
66	
67	**Knowledge files** live in `src/knowledge/` and capture principles Ferris applies across multiple workflows — zero hallucination, confidence tiers, provenance tracking, \[MANUAL\] preservation. Workflow steps reference these when they need cross-cutting judgment, not step-specific rules.
68	
69	**Data files** live in each workflow's `data/` directory and contain operational artifacts tightly coupled to specific steps — schemas, templates, scoring formulas, extraction patterns.
70	
71	Neither duplicates the other. Knowledge files reference (not repeat) data files where the detailed operational rules live.
72	
73	## Directory Structure
74	
75	Each workflow follows a consistent structure:
76	
77	```
78	{workflow}/
79	├── workflow.md              # Entry point — goals, mode routing, step sequence
80	├── steps-c/                 # Create-mode steps (sequential execution)
81	│   ├── step-01-*.md
82	│   ├── step-02-*.md
83	│   └── ...
84	├── data/                    # Reference data — schemas, rules, patterns
85	│   └── *.md
86	└── templates/               # Output skeletons (only analyze-source, test-skill)
87	    └── *-template.md
88	```
89	
90	### Step Loading
91	
92	Steps execute sequentially via just-in-time loading. Only the current step is in context. Each step explicitly names the next step file. Data and template files are loaded on demand by the steps that need them.
93	
94	## Workflow Details
95	
96	### setup-forge (SF)
97	
98	**5 steps** | **Data:** tier-rules.md
99	
100	Detects available tools (ast-grep, gh, QMD), determines the capability tier (Quick/Forge/Forge+/Deep), writes `forge-tier.yaml` to the sidecar, and optionally indexes the project. Run this first — all other workflows adapt behavior to the detected tier.
101	
102	### analyze-source (AN)
103	
104	**7 steps** | **Data:** skill-brief-schema.md, unit-detection-heuristics.md | **Templates:** analysis-report-template.md
105	
106	Brownfield onboarding entry point. Scans project structure, identifies skillable units using confidence-tiered heuristic signals, maps exports and integration points, presents recommendations for user confirmation, then generates `skill-brief.yaml` files ready for `create-skill --batch`.
107	
108	### brief-skill (BS)
109	
110	**5 steps** | **Data:** skill-brief-schema.md, scope-templates.md
111	
112	Interactive guided discovery. Gathers user intent (target repo, language, what to skill), analyzes the target to understand boundaries, walks through scope definition with the user, confirms the brief, then writes a validated `skill-brief.yaml`.
113	
114	### create-skill (CS)
115	
116	**8 primary steps (11 step files including sub-steps)** | **Data:** extraction-patterns.md, extraction-patterns-tracing.md, skill-sections.md, tier-degradation-rules.md, compile-assembly-rules.md, source-resolution-protocols.md
117	
118	The core compilation engine. Loads a brief, checks ecosystem context, extracts source with AST-backed provenance (T1 confidence when ast-grep available), enriches with usage patterns, compiles into agentskills.io-compliant sections, validates structural integrity, generates the `SKILL.md` artifact, and produces an evidence report. Supports `--batch` for processing multiple briefs.
119	
120	### quick-skill (QS)
121	
122	**6 steps** | **Data:** registry-resolution.md, skill-template.md
123	
124	Fastest path to a skill. Accepts a GitHub URL or package name, resolves to source via registry lookup, performs ecosystem check, extracts public API surface without requiring a brief, compiles best-effort `SKILL.md`, validates, and writes output.
125	
126	### create-stack-skill (SS)
127	
128	**9 steps** | **Data:** integration-patterns.md, manifest-patterns.md, stack-skill-template.md, compose-mode-rules.md
129	
130	Two modes: **Code-mode** analyzes dependency manifests and co-import patterns from actual source code. **Compose-mode** synthesizes a stack skill from pre-generated individual skills + architecture document (no codebase required). Both produce the same output format — consolidated stack skill with integration patterns.
131	
132	### verify-stack (VS)
133	
134	**6 steps** | **Data:** integration-verification-rules.md, coverage-patterns.md, feasibility-report-template.md
135	
136	Pre-code verification engine. Cross-references generated skills against architecture and PRD documents. Three passes: coverage verification (does a skill exist for each tech?), integration verification (do the APIs actually connect?), and requirements verification (does the stack cover the PRD?). Produces a feasibility report with evidence-backed verdicts and prescriptive recommendations. Re-runnable — swap libraries, regenerate skills, re-verify.
137	
138	### refine-architecture (RA)
139	
140	**6 steps** | **Data:** refinement-rules.md
141	
142	Architecture improvement engine. Takes the original architecture document and refines it using verified skill data and VS feasibility findings. Fills gaps, flags issues, suggests improvements — all backed by specific API evidence from the generated skills. Produces a refined architecture document.
143	
144	### update-skill (US)
145	
146	**7 steps** | **Data:** manual-section-rules.md, merge-conflict-rules.md, remote-source-resolution.md (tier-degradation-rules shared from create-skill)
147	
148	Surgical update engine. Loads the existing skill, detects what changed in source since last compilation, re-extracts only affected exports with fresh provenance, merges new extractions while preserving all `[MANUAL]` developer-authored content, validates the merged result, writes the updated skill, and produces a change report.
149	
150	### audit-skill (AS)
151	
152	**6 steps** | **Data:** drift-report-template.md, severity-rules.md
153	
154	Drift detection engine. Loads the skill under audit, re-indexes current source to build a fresh reference, performs structural diff (signature changes, missing exports), performs semantic diff (behavioral divergence), classifies each finding by severity using defined rules, and produces a graded drift report.
155	
156	### test-skill (TS)
157	
158	**7 steps** | **Data:** output-section-formats.md, scoring-rules.md, source-access-protocol.md | **Templates:** test-report-template.md
159	
160	Quality gate before export. Loads the skill, detects test mode (naive for Quick tier, contextual for Forge/Deep), runs coverage checks against source, runs coherence checks for internal consistency, scores using weighted rubric, and produces a completeness report with pass/fail gate decision.
161	
162	### export-skill (EX)
163	
164	**6 steps** | **Data:** managed-section-format.md, snippet-format.md
165	
166	Distribution packaging. Loads the completed skill, packages as agentskills.io-compliant bundle, generates context snippets optimized for token efficiency, updates managed sections in `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md` with platform-aware injection, produces a token budget report, and summarizes the export.
167	
```

50	- Path resolution: See `knowledge/version-paths.md` for canonical path templates (`{skill_package}`, `{skill_group}`, `{forge_version}`, `{forge_group}`)
51	
52	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
53	
54	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
55	
56	### 1. Create Directory Structure
57	
58	Resolve `{version}` from the skill brief's `version` field. Create the following directories:
59	
60	```
61	{skill_group}                          # {skills_output_folder}/{name}/
62	{skill_package}                        # {skills_output_folder}/{name}/{version}/{name}/
63	{skill_package}/references/
64	{forge_version}                        # {forge_data_folder}/{name}/{version}/
65	```
66	
67	If `scripts_inventory` is non-empty, also create: `{skill_package}/scripts/`
68	If `assets_inventory` is non-empty, also create: `{skill_package}/assets/`
69	
70	Where `{name}` is the skill name from the brief (kebab-case) and `{version}` is the semver version from the brief (with build metadata stripped per `knowledge/version-paths.md`).
71	
72	If directories already exist, do not error — proceed with file writing (overwrites existing files).
73	
74	### 2. Write Deliverables to {skill_package}
75	
76	Write these 4 files from the compiled content:
77	
78	**File 1:** `{skill_package}/SKILL.md`
79	- The complete compiled skill document
80	- agentskills.io-compliant format with all sections
81	- [MANUAL] markers seeded
82	
83	**File 2:** `{skill_package}/context-snippet.md`
84	- Compressed 2-line format for CLAUDE.md integration
85	
86	**File 3:** `{skill_package}/metadata.json`
87	- Machine-readable birth certificate with stats and provenance
88	
89	**File 4:** `{skill_package}/references/*.md`
90	- One file per function group or type
91	- Progressive disclosure detail files
92	
93	**Files 4b (conditional):** `{skill_package}/scripts/*`
94	- One file per detected script, copied from source with content preserved
95	- Only created when `scripts_inventory` is non-empty
96	
97	**Files 4c (conditional):** `{skill_package}/assets/*`
98	- One file per detected asset, copied from source with content preserved
99	- Only created when `assets_inventory` is non-empty
100	
101	### 3. Write Workspace Artifacts to {forge_version}
102	
103	Write these 3 files from the compiled content:
104	
105	**File 5:** `{forge_version}/provenance-map.json`
106	- Per-claim source map with AST bindings and confidence tiers
107	
108	**File 6:** `{forge_version}/evidence-report.md`
109	- Build artifact with extraction summary, validation results, warnings
110	
111	**File 7:** `{forge_version}/extraction-rules.yaml`
112	- Language and ast-grep schema used for this extraction (for reproducibility)
113	
114	### 4. Create Active Symlink
115	
116	Create or update the `active` symlink at `{skill_group}/active` pointing to `{version}`:
117	
118	```
119	{skill_group}/active -> {version}
120	```
121	
122	If the symlink already exists, remove it first and recreate. This ensures `{skill_group}/active/{name}/` resolves to the just-written skill package.
123	
124	### 5. Verify Write Completion
125	
126	After all files are written, verify:
127	- All 4 deliverable artifact types exist (SKILL.md, context-snippet.md, metadata.json, at least one file in references/), all 3 workspace artifacts exist (provenance-map.json, evidence-report.md, extraction-rules.yaml), plus scripts/ and assets/ files when inventories are non-empty
128	- The `active` symlink at `{skill_group}/active` resolves to `{version}`
129	- Store `ref_count` = count of files written to `references/` for use in step-08 report
