85	
86	**Processing order:** Process platforms in the order listed in `target_platforms`.
87	
88	### 4. Rebuild Complete Skill Index
89	
90	#### 4a. Read Export Manifest (v2 — version-aware)
91	
92	Read `{skills_output_folder}/.export-manifest.json` — see [knowledge/version-paths.md](../../../knowledge/version-paths.md) for the full v2 schema:
93	
94	**If the file exists:** Parse JSON. Check for `schema_version` field:
95	
96	**v2 manifest** (`schema_version: "2"`):
97	```json
98	{
99	  "schema_version": "2",
100	  "exports": {
101	    "skill-name": {
102	      "active_version": "0.6.0",
103	      "versions": {
104	        "0.5.0": {
105	          "platforms": ["claude"],
106	          "last_exported": "2026-03-15",
107	          "status": "archived"
108	        },
109	        "0.6.0": {
110	          "platforms": ["claude", "copilot"],
111	          "last_exported": "2026-04-04",
112	          "status": "active"
113	        }
114	      }
115	    }
116	  }
117	}
118	```
119	
120	**v1 manifest** (no `schema_version` field — migrate in-place to v2):
121	1. For each entry in `exports`, read its `platforms` and `last_exported`
122	2. Resolve the skill's current version from `{resolved_skill_package}/metadata.json`
123	3. Wrap in v2 structure: set `active_version` to the resolved version, create a single entry in `versions` with `status: "active"`, the original `platforms`, and `last_exported`
124	4. Set `schema_version: "2"` at root
125	5. Hold the migrated structure in context (it will be written in section 9b)
126	
127	**If the file does not exist** (first export or migration): Treat as empty — only the current export target will appear in the rebuilt index.
128	
129	#### 4b. Build Exported Skill Set (version-aware)
130	
131	Determine the set of skills to include in the rebuilt index:
132	
133	1. Start with all skill names listed in the manifest's `exports` object (if manifest exists)
134	2. For each skill, record its `active_version` from the manifest (v2 schema)
135	3. Add the current export target skill name (ensures it is always included even before manifest is written) — use the version from `{resolved_skill_package}/metadata.json` as its `active_version`
136	4. This is the **exported skill set** — each entry has a skill name and its resolved `active_version`
137	
138	#### 4c. Resolve and Filter Snippets (manifest-driven — replaces glob scan)
139	
140	Instead of globbing `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md`, resolve snippets from the exported skill set built in 4b:
141	
142	**For each skill in the exported skill set:**
143	1. Resolve `{skill_package}` using the skill's `active_version`: `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{active_version}/{skill-name}/`
144	2. Read `{skill_package}/context-snippet.md`
145	3. **If snippet exists:** Add to skill index
146	4. **If snippet does not exist at the versioned path:** Check for `active` symlink at `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/active/{skill-name}/context-snippet.md`. If still not found, skip with warning: "Snippet missing for {skill-name} v{active_version} — skipping from managed section"
147	
148	**Skills NOT in the exported skill set are never scanned** — they have not been through export-skill and must not appear in the managed section (ADR-K).
149	
150	**If no snippets pass the filter:** Generate managed section with zero skills — header only, no skill entries.
151

153	
154	The context-snippet.md files on disk contain root paths for the platform they were originally exported to. When assembling the managed section for the current target platform, rewrite root paths if they differ:

297-     "versions": {

300-         "last_exported": "{current-date}",
301-         "status": "active"
302-       }
303-     }
304-   }
305-   ```
306-   - `{version}` is the version from `{resolved_skill_package}/metadata.json`
307-   - Set `platforms` to only the platforms that were successfully written and verified in section 9
308-   - If the skill already has a manifest entry:

288	
289	## Output Architecture
290	
291	### Per-Skill Output
292	
293	Every generated skill produces a self-contained directory:
294	
295	```
296	skills/{name}/
297	├── SKILL.md              # Active skill (loaded on trigger)
298	├── context-snippet.md    # Passive context (compressed, always-on)
299	├── metadata.json         # Machine-readable provenance
300	├── references/           # Progressive disclosure
301	│   ├── {function-a}.md
302	│   └── {function-b}.md
303	├── scripts/              # Executable automation (when detected in source)
304	│   └── {script-name}.sh
305	└── assets/               # Templates, schemas, configs (when detected in source)
306	    └── {asset-name}.json
307	```
308	
309	The `scripts/` and `assets/` directories are optional — only created when the source repository contains executable scripts or static assets matching detection heuristics. Each file traces to its source via `[SRC:file:L1]` provenance citations with SHA-256 content hashes for drift detection. User-authored files go in `scripts/[MANUAL]/` or `assets/[MANUAL]/` subdirectories and are preserved during updates.
310	
311	### SKILL.md Format
312	
313	Skills follow the [agentskills.io specification](https://agentskills.io/specification) with frontmatter:
314	
315	```yaml
316	---
317	name: cognee
318	description: >
319	  Use when cognee is a Python AI memory engine that transforms documents into
320	  knowledge graphs with vector and graph storage for semantic search and
321	  reasoning. Use this skill when writing code that calls cognee's Python API
322	  (add, cognify, search, memify, config, datasets, prune, session) or
323	  integrating cognee-mcp. Covers the full public API, SearchType modes,
324	  DataPoint custom models, pipeline tasks, and configuration for
325	  LLM/embedding/vector/graph providers. Do NOT use for general knowledge graph
326	  theory or unrelated Python libraries.
327	---
328	```
329	
330	Every instruction in the body traces to source:
331	
332	```python
333	await cognee.search(  # [AST:cognee/api/v1/search/search.py:L26]
334	    query_text="What does Cognee do?"
335	)
336	```
337	
338	### metadata.json — The Birth Certificate
339	
340	Machine-readable provenance for every skill:
341	
342	```json
343	{
344	  "name": "cognee",
345	  "version": "0.5.5",
346	  "skill_type": "single",
347	  "source_authority": "community",
348	  "source_repo": "https://github.com/topoteretes/cognee",
349	  "source_root": "cognee/",
350	  "source_commit": null,
351	  "confidence_tier": "Deep",
352	  "spec_version": "1.3",
353	  "generation_date": "2026-03-20T16:55:00+04:00",
354	  "stats": {
355	    "exports_documented": 22,
356	    "exports_public_api": 22,
357	    "exports_internal": 815,
358	    "exports_total": 837,
359	    "public_api_coverage": 1.0,
360	    "total_coverage": 0.026,
361	    "confidence_t1": 837,
362	    "confidence_t2": 14,
363	    "confidence_t3": 10
364	  }
365	}
366	```
367	
368	`scripts` and `assets` arrays are optional — omitted entirely (not empty) when the source has no scripts or assets.
369	
370	### Stack Skill Output
371	
372	Stack skills map how your dependencies interact — shared types, co-import patterns, integration points:
373	
374	```
375	skills/{project}-stack/
376	├── SKILL.md              # Integration patterns + project conventions
377	├── context-snippet.md    # Compressed stack index
378	├── metadata.json         # Component versions, integration graph
379	└── references/
380	    ├── nextjs.md         # Project-specific subset
381	    ├── better-auth.md    # Project-specific subset
382	    └── integrations/
383	        ├── auth-db.md    # Cross-library pattern
384	        └── pwa-auth.md   # Cross-library pattern
```

389	---
390	
391	## Dual-Output Strategy
392	
393	Based on [Vercel research](https://vercel.com/blog/agents-md-outperforms-skills-in-our-agent-evals): passive context (AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md) achieves 100% pass rate vs 79% for active skills alone.
394	
395	Every skill generates both:
396	
397	1. **SKILL.md** — Active skill loaded on trigger with full instructions
398	2. **context-snippet.md** — Passive context, compressed index; injected into platform context files (CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md/.cursorrules) only when `export-skill` is run. Export reads configured IDEs from `config.yaml` and writes to all target platforms in one pass.
399	
400	### Managed Context Section
401	
402	Export injects a managed section between markers:
403	
404	```markdown
405	<!-- SKF:BEGIN updated:2026-03-20 -->
406	[SKF Skills]|1 skill
407	|IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data.
408	|
409	[cognee v0.5.5]|root: .agents/skills/cognee/
410	|IMPORTANT: cognee v0.5.5 — read SKILL.md before writing cognee code. Do NOT rely on training data.
411	|quick-start:{SKILL.md#quick-start} — add → cognify → search async workflow
412	|api: add(), cognify(), search(), memify(), config, datasets, prune, update(), session, SearchType, run_custom_pipeline(), visualize_graph()
413	|key-types:{SKILL.md#key-types} — SearchType(14 modes: GRAPH_COMPLETION default, RAG_COMPLETION, CHUNKS, CYPHER, TEMPORAL...), Task, DataPoint
414	|gotchas: all core functions are async (must await); delete() deprecated since v0.3.9 — use datasets.delete_data(); memify default pipeline changed to triplet embedding (Mar 2026)
```

450	### Internal Utility
451	
452	**`manifest_reader`** detects and parses dependency files across ecosystems:
453	
454	- **Full support:** `package.json`, `pyproject.toml`, `requirements.txt`, `Cargo.toml`, `go.mod`
455	- **Basic support:** `build.gradle`, `pom.xml`, `Gemfile`, `composer.json`
456	
457	---
458	
459	## Workspace Artifacts
460	
461	Build artifacts are committable — another developer can reproduce the same skill:
462	
463	```
464	forge-data/{skill-name}/
465	├── skill-brief.yaml        # Compilation config
466	├── provenance-map.json     # Source map with AST bindings
467	├── evidence-report.md      # Build audit trail
468	└── extraction-rules.yaml   # Language-specific ast-grep schema
469	```
470	
471	The `provenance-map.json` includes a `file_entries` array for script/asset file-level provenance (SHA-256 hashes, source paths) alongside the export-level `entries` array.
472	
473	`skills/` and `forge-data/` are committed. Agent memory (`_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/`) is gitignored.
474	
475	---
476	
477	## Ownership Model
478	
479	| Context | `source_authority` | Distribution |
480	|---------|-------------------|-------------|
481	| OSS library (maintainer generates) | `official` | `npx skills publish` to agentskills ecosystem |
482	| Internal service (team generates) | `internal` | `skills/` in repo, ships with code |
483	| External dependency (consumer generates) | `community` | Local `skills/`, marked as community |
484	
485	Provenance maps enable verification: an `official` skill's provenance must trace to the actual source repo owned by the author.
486	
487	---
488	
489	## Key Design Decisions

72	## Configuration
73	
74	SKF has two install-time variables (defined in `src/module.yaml`), one Core Config variable inherited from BMad, and one runtime preference:
75	
76	| Variable               | Purpose                                                                                              | Default                     |
77	|------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|
78	| `skills_output_folder` | Where generated skills are saved                                                                     | `{project-root}/skills`     |
79	| `forge_data_folder`    | Where workspace artifacts are stored (VS reports, evidence)                                          | `{project-root}/forge-data` |
80	| `output_folder`        | Where refined architecture documents are saved (used by RA workflow). *Inherited from BMad Core Config.* | Defined by BMad Core Config |
81	| `tier_override`        | Force a specific tier for comparison or testing (in `_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/preferences.yaml`) | `~` (auto-detect)           |
82	
83	Runtime configuration (tool detection, tier, and collection state) is managed by the `setup-forge` workflow and persisted in `forge-tier.yaml`.

24	## The Problem
25	
26	You ask an AI agent to use a library. It invents function names that don't exist. It guesses parameter types. You paste documentation into the context — it still gets details wrong. You write instructions by hand — they go stale the moment the code changes.
27	
28	This isn't an edge case. It's the default experience.
29	
30	## How Skill Forge Fixes This
31	
32	1. **Analyzes your sources** — extracts real function signatures, types, and patterns from code repositories, documentation websites, and developer discourse
33	2. **Compiles verified instruction files** — every instruction links to the exact file and line it came from
34	3. **Follows an open standard** — skills comply with the [agentskills.io](https://agentskills.io) spec and work across Claude, Cursor, Copilot, and other AI agents
35	
36	## Before vs After
37	
38	**Without SKF** — your agent guesses:
39	
40	```python
41	import cognee
42	
43	# Agent hallucinates: sync call, wrong parameter name, missing await
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/docs/how-it-works.md",
  "old_string": "Every generated skill produces a self-contained directory:\n\n```\nskills/{name}/\n├── SKILL.md              # Active skill (loaded on trigger)\n├── context-snippet.md    # Passive context (compressed, always-on)\n├── metadata.json         # Machine-readable provenance\n├── references/           # Progressive disclosure\n│   ├── {function-a}.md\n│   └── {function-b}.md\n├── scripts/              # Executable automation (when detected in source)\n│   └── {script-name}.sh\n└── assets/               # Templates, schemas, configs (when detected in source)\n    └── {asset-name}.json\n```",
  "new_string": "Every generated skill produces a self-contained, version-aware directory:\n\n```\nskills/{name}/\n├── active -> {version}           # Symlink to current version\n├── {version}/\n│   └── {name}/                   # agentskills.io-compliant package\n│       ├── SKILL.md              # Active skill (loaded on trigger)\n│       ├── context-snippet.md    # Passive context (compressed, always-on)\n│       ├── metadata.json         # Machine-readable provenance\n│       ├── references/           # Progressive disclosure\n│       │   ├── {function-a}.md\n│       │   └── {function-b}.md\n│       ├── scripts/              # Executable automation (when detected in source)\n│       │   └── {script-name}.sh\n│       └── assets/               # Templates, schemas, configs (when detected in source)\n│           └── {asset-name}.json\n└── {older-version}/\n    └── {name}/                   # Previous version preserved\n        └── ...\n```\n\nMultiple versions coexist under the same skill name. The `active` symlink points to the current version. Updating a skill for a new library release creates a new version directory — users pinned to older versions keep their skill intact. The inner `{name}/` directory is a standalone [agentskills.io](https://agentskills.io) package, directly installable via `npx skills add`."
}
```

1	---
2	name: party-mode
3	description: 'Orchestrates group discussions between all installed BMAD agents, enabling natural multi-agent conversations. Use when user requests party mode.'
4	---
5	
6	# Party Mode Workflow
7	
8	**Goal:** Orchestrates group discussions between all installed BMAD agents, enabling natural multi-agent conversations
9	
10	**Your Role:** You are a party mode facilitator and multi-agent conversation orchestrator. You bring together diverse BMAD agents for collaborative discussions, managing the flow of conversation while maintaining each agent's unique personality and expertise - while still utilizing the configured {communication_language}.
11	
12	---
13	
14	## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
15	
16	This uses **micro-file architecture** with **sequential conversation orchestration**:
17	
18	- Step 01 loads agent manifest and initializes party mode
19	- Step 02 orchestrates the ongoing multi-agent discussion
20	- Step 03 handles graceful party mode exit
21	- Conversation state tracked in frontmatter
22	- Agent personalities maintained through merged manifest data
23	
24	---
25	
26	## INITIALIZATION
27	
28	### Configuration Loading
29	
30	Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/core/config.yaml` and resolve:
31	
32	- `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`
33	- `communication_language`, `document_output_language`, `user_skill_level`
34	- `date` as a system-generated value
35	- Agent manifest path: `{project-root}/_bmad/_config/agent-manifest.csv`
36	
37	### Paths
38	
39	- `installed_path` = `{project-root}/_bmad/core/workflows/party-mode`
40	- `agent_manifest_path` = `{project-root}/_bmad/_config/agent-manifest.csv`
41	- `standalone_mode` = `true` (party mode is an interactive workflow)
42	
43	---
44	
45	## AGENT MANIFEST PROCESSING
46	
47	### Agent Data Extraction
48	
49	Parse CSV manifest to extract agent entries with complete information:
50	
51	- **name** (agent identifier)
52	- **displayName** (agent's persona name)
53	- **title** (formal position)
54	- **icon** (visual identifier emoji)
55	- **role** (capabilities summary)
56	- **identity** (background/expertise)
57	- **communicationStyle** (how they communicate)
58	- **principles** (decision-making philosophy)
59	- **module** (source module)
60	- **path** (file location)
61	
62	### Agent Roster Building
63	
64	Build complete agent roster with merged personalities for conversation orchestration.
65	
66	---
67	
68	## EXECUTION
69	
70	Execute party mode activation and conversation orchestration:
71	
72	### Party Mode Activation
73	
74	**Your Role:** You are a party mode facilitator creating an engaging multi-agent conversation environment.
75	
76	**Welcome Activation:**
77	
78	"🎉 PARTY MODE ACTIVATED! 🎉
79	
80	Welcome {{user_name}}! All BMAD agents are here and ready for a dynamic group discussion. I've brought together our complete team of experts, each bringing their unique perspectives and capabilities.
81	
82	**Let me introduce our collaborating agents:**
83

Perfect. I now have all the information needed to provide a comprehensive report. Let me compile the findings:

## Summary Report: Version Specification and Documentation URL Handling

### 1. VERSION SPECIFICATION

**Schema Fields:**
- Field name: `version`
- Type: Semantic version string (`X.Y.Z` or `X.Y.Z-prerelease`)
- Constraint: Semver format with pre-release support
- Status: **Required field** (both brief-skill and analyze-source schemas)

**Detection vs. User Override:**

The current system uses **auto-detection only**. Users cannot directly specify a target version in the workflows:

1. **Auto-detection**: Version is auto-detected from language-specific files during step-02 (analyze-target):
   - Python: `pyproject.toml` → `__init__.py` → `_version.py` → `setup.py` → git tags
   - JavaScript/TypeScript: `package.json` version field
   - Rust: `Cargo.toml` (or workspace root) → git tags
   - Go: `go.mod` or git tags

2. **Fallback**: If detection fails or returns non-semver, defaults to `"1.0.0"`

3. **User override**: Only occurs at step-04 (confirm-brief), where the user can "adjust before we continue" — but this is presented as an adjustment to a pre-detected value, not a primary input field.

4. **Pre-release handling**: Pre-release tags (e.g., `1.0.0-beta.0`, `2.0.0-rc.1`) are preserved as-is and treated as valid semver.

**Version reconciliation in create-skill:**

During step-03-extract, if the source version has changed since the brief was created:
- A **warning** is displayed: "Brief version ({brief.version}) differs from source version ({source_version}). Using source version ({source_version})."
- The **source version is used** for extraction
- The mismatch is recorded in context for the evidence report

---

### 2. DOCUMENTATION URLs

**Schema Fields:**
- Field name: `doc_urls`
- Type: Array of objects with structure: `{url, label}`
- Status: **Optional field** (required only when `source_type: "docs-only"`)
- Min entries when required: >= 1

**Where doc_urls are collected:**

1. **step-01 (gather-intent):**
   - For `source_type: "docs-only"` skills: user provides documentation URLs as the primary input
   - For `source_type: "source"` skills: optional supplemental question — "Are there any documentation URLs you'd like to include for supplemental context? (These will be fetched as T3 external references.)"

2. **step-03 (scope-definition):**
   - If `source_type: "docs-only"`: section 2 confirms which doc pages to include
   - If `source_type: "source"` AND supplemental `doc_urls` collected: section 2b allows add/remove/confirm of URLs

**Version-specific URL support:**

The current schema and workflows do **NOT** explicitly support version-specific doc URLs. However:

- Each `doc_urls` entry has a `label` field that could theoretically include version info (e.g., `"API Reference v2.0"`)
- During doc-fetching (step-03c), the fetched content is not version-filtered — a single URL is fetched regardless of the skill's version
- There is **no automated mechanism** to resolve different doc URLs based on the skill version

---

### 3. DOC_URLS DOWNSTREAM FLOW

**step-03c (fetch-docs) in create-skill workflow:**

This is where `doc_urls` from the brief are consumed:

1. **Eligibility check**: Skipped if `doc_urls` is absent/empty (graceful degradation)
2. **Security notice**: User informed which external URLs will be fetched
3. **Fetch implementation**: Tool-agnostic — uses Firecrawl, WebFetch, web-reader, curl, or any available web tool
4. **Subpage discovery**: If a root URL is detected with minimal content (<2000 words and high link density), automatically discovers and fetches up to 10 relevant subpages
5. **Content extraction**: Function/method signatures, parameters, return types, configuration options, usage examples parsed from fetched markdown
6. **Citation**: Every extracted item receives T3 confidence citation: `[EXT:{url}]`

**Merge behavior:**
- **docs-only mode**: Doc-fetch inventory replaces the empty inventory from step-03 source extraction
- **supplemental mode** (source + doc_urls): T3 items merged into existing extraction inventory respecting conflict rules

**Conflict rule**: T3 items NEVER override T1, T1-low, or T2 items for the same export. Precedence: `T1 > T1-low > T2 > T3`

---

### 4. VERSION-DOC COUPLING LOGIC

**Current status: NO active version-doc coupling**

The workflows do not implement explicit version-to-documentation mapping:

1. **Version is separate from doc_urls**: Version is captured in one field, doc_urls in another — no cross-reference
2. **No version-based doc selection**: The doc-fetcher doesn't attempt to fetch version-specific variants of a documentation URL
3. **Doc URLs are static in the brief**: Once captured, `doc_urls` are not adjusted based on version reconciliation in create-skill

**What exists:**
- Version reconciliation (section-01 of source-resolution-protocols.md) detects version changes and uses the source version
- Doc fetching (step-03c) fetches whatever URLs are in `doc_urls`, regardless of version mismatch
- No logic to say "if version changed from X to Y, try fetching docs from URL-for-Y instead of URL-for-X"

**Potential future improvement:** A user could manually include version-differentiated URLs in the label or manage multiple skills per version, but this is a workaround, not a feature.

---

### 5. KEY FILES AND LOCATIONS

**Schema Definitions:**
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/brief-skill/data/skill-brief-schema.md` (lines 1-149)
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md` (identical structure)

**Version Detection:**
- `skill-brief-schema.md` lines 33-48: Version Detection section
- `step-02-analyze-target.md` lines 162-176: 4b. Detect Source Version subsection

**Doc URL Input:**
- `step-01-gather-intent.md` lines 105-123: Both sources of doc_urls (docs-only and supplemental)

**Scope/Docs Confirmation:**
- `step-03-scope-definition.md` lines 74-105: Docs-only mode and supplemental URL confirmation

**Doc Fetching:**
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-fetch-docs.md` (comprehensive doc-fetching implementation)

**Version Reconciliation:**
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/source-resolution-protocols.md` lines 141-165: Version Reconciliation section
```

**PARTY MODE ACTIVATED!**

Welcome Armel! I've assembled the team to discuss whether the brief-skill workflow should support version-specific library/docs targeting. Let me bring in the most relevant experts.

**The question on the table:** *Should users be able to brief a specific version of a library or documentation set — especially when docs are organized by version (e.g., `docs.example.com/v2/` vs `docs.example.com/v3/`)?*

**Current state I've confirmed:**
- Version is **auto-detected only** from package manifests (package.json, pyproject.toml, etc.)
- Users can adjust at the confirm step, but can't specify a *target* version upfront
- `doc_urls` are static — no version-doc coupling
- Doc fetcher fetches whatever URLs are in the brief, regardless of version
- We just shipped version-aware output directories (`{skill-name}/{version}/{skill-name}/`)

Let me bring in the first round of perspectives.

---

🏗️ **Morgan (Module Builder):** This is a natural gap that the version-aware output structure just exposed. We now have version-nested directories — `cognee/0.5.0/cognee/`, `cognee/0.6.0/cognee/` — but the *input* pipeline has no way to target a specific version. The brief captures whatever version the source happens to be at detection time. If a user wants to compile a skill for cognee v0.5.0 while their local checkout is at v0.6.0, they're stuck.

The fix is straightforward from a module architecture standpoint: add an optional `target_version` field to the skill brief schema. When present, it overrides auto-detection. The version reconciliation in create-skill step-03 already handles brief-vs-source mismatches — we just need to make that intentional rather than a warning condition.

For `doc_urls`, I'd recommend a `doc_version_pattern` field — something like `https://docs.cognee.com/{version}/api/` — where `{version}` gets resolved from the brief's version. This keeps URLs DRY and version-aware without forcing users to manually update URLs for each version.

---

🔄 **Wendy (Workflow Builder):** Morgan's right about the gap, but let me flag the workflow implications. The brief-skill workflow has a clean 5-step flow: gather-intent → analyze-target → scope → confirm → write. Adding version targeting touches steps 1 and 2:

**Step 1 (gather-intent):** We'd need a new question — "Are you targeting a specific version? (Leave blank for auto-detect)". This must come *before* target analysis so step 2 can validate.

**Step 2 (analyze-target):** If `target_version` is provided, version detection should *validate* against what's available rather than *discover*. If the user says "v0.5.0" but the source is at v0.6.0, we should warn but proceed — that's the whole point. For docs-only skills (no source repo), `target_version` becomes the primary version source.

**For `doc_urls`**, I'd actually push back on Morgan's `doc_version_pattern` approach. It's clever but fragile — doc URL structures vary wildly between projects. Some use `/v2/`, some use `/2.x/`, some use `/stable/` vs `/latest/`. A pattern-based approach would need too many escape hatches.

My recommendation: keep `doc_urls` as-is, but add a **prompt at step 1** when the user provides a `target_version` AND `doc_urls`: *"You're targeting version {target_version}. Do these doc URLs match that version?"* Simple, explicit, no magic.

---

🔬 **Dr. Quinn (Creative Problem Solver):** AHA! Both Morgan and Wendy are circling the real problem but missing the root cause. Let me apply some systems thinking here.

The *actual* problem isn't "users can't specify a version." It's that **the brief doesn't distinguish between the source version and the skill target version**. These are different things:

1. **Source version** — what's in the repo right now (auto-detected)
2. **Target version** — what version the user wants the skill to describe (user intent)
3. **Doc version** — what version the documentation URLs correspond to (external dependency)

Right now, all three are collapsed into one `version` field. That works when they're the same — and breaks when they're not.

The docs-by-version problem is a symptom. The cure: separate `target_version` (user intent) from `detected_version` (auto-detection). When they match, everything works as today. When they diverge, the workflow can make intelligent decisions — warn about mismatches, prompt for version-correct doc URLs, and tag the resulting skill accurately.

I agree with Wendy's pragmatic approach for doc URLs. Pattern templates are engineering overkill for a human-in-the-loop workflow. A simple confirmation prompt is cleaner and more robust.

One more thing — for **docs-only skills** (no source repo), `target_version` isn't optional — it's essential. There's no package manifest to detect from. Currently these default to `1.0.0`, which is almost always wrong.

---

**Round 1 Summary:**
- **Consensus:** Yes, version-specific briefing is a good idea — it fills a gap exposed by version-aware output
- **Morgan** advocates `target_version` field + `doc_version_pattern`
- **Wendy** agrees on `target_version` but prefers a simple confirmation prompt over URL patterns
- **Dr. Quinn** identifies the root cause: conflating source/target/doc versions — recommends separating them

What are your thoughts? Want to dig deeper into any perspective, or should I bring in different agents?

[E] Exit Party Mode - End the collaborative session

1	# Skill Brief Schema
2	
3	## Required Fields
4	
5	| Field | Type | Constraint | Description |
6	|-------|------|------------|-------------|
7	| name | string | kebab-case `[a-z0-9-]+` | Unique skill identifier |
8	| version | string | Semantic version (`X.Y.Z` or `X.Y.Z-prerelease`) | Auto-detect from source (see Version Detection below), fall back to `1.0.0` |
9	| source_repo | string | GitHub URL or local path | Repository or project root (optional when `source_type: "docs-only"`) |
10	| language | string | Recognized language | Primary programming language |
11	| scope | object | See Scope Object below | Boundary definition |
12	| description | string | 1-3 sentences | What the skill covers |
13	| forge_tier | string | `Quick` / `Forge` / `Forge+` / `Deep` | Inherited from forge-tier.yaml (Title Case) |
14	| created | string | ISO date `YYYY-MM-DD` | Generation date |
15	| created_by | string | user_name from config | Who generated the brief |
16	
17	## Optional Fields
18	
19	| Field | Type | Constraint | Description |
20	|-------|------|------------|-------------|
21	| source_type | string | `source` or `docs-only` | Default `source`. When `docs-only`: `source_repo` optional, `doc_urls` required |
22	| doc_urls | array | `{url, label}` objects | Documentation URLs for T3 content. Required when `source_type: "docs-only"` |
23	| `scripts_intent` | string | `detect` / `none` / free-text | Describes whether scripts should be extracted. Values: `detect` (auto-detect from source — default when absent), `none` (skip scripts), or a free-text description of expected scripts (e.g., "CLI validation tools in bin/"). |
24	| `assets_intent` | string | `detect` / `none` / free-text | Describes whether assets should be extracted. Values: `detect` (auto-detect from source — default when absent), `none` (skip assets), or a free-text description of expected assets (e.g., "JSON schemas in schemas/"). |
25	| `source_authority` | string | `official` / `community` / `internal` | Default `community`. Set to `official` only when the skill creator is the library maintainer. Forced to `community` when `source_type: "docs-only"`. |
26	
27	When `source_type: "docs-only"`:
28	- `source_repo` becomes optional (set to doc site URL for reference)
29	- `doc_urls` must have at least one entry
30	- `source_authority` is forced to `community` (T3 external documentation cannot be `official`)
31	- All extracted content gets `[EXT:{url}]` citations
32	
33	## Version Detection
34	
35	During brief creation, attempt to auto-detect the source version before defaulting to `"1.0.0"`. Check the first matching file in the source:
36	
37	- **Python:** `pyproject.toml` `[project] version` (static) → if `dynamic = ["version"]`, check `__init__.py` for `__version__` → `_version.py` if exists → `setup.py` `version=` → `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`
38	- **JavaScript/TypeScript:** `package.json` (`"version"`)
39	- **Rust:** `Cargo.toml` `[package] version` (static) → if `version = { workspace = true }`, resolve from workspace root `Cargo.toml` → `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`
40	- **Go:** version tag from `go.mod` or `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`
41	
42	If the source is a remote GitHub repo, use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{file}` to read the version file. If the source is local, read the file directly.
43	
44	If detection succeeds, use the detected version. If it fails or returns a non-semver value, fall back to `"1.0.0"`.
45	
46	The create-skill workflow (step-03-extract) also performs version reconciliation at extraction time — if the source version has changed since the brief was created, the extraction step warns and uses the source version.
47	
48	**Pre-release handling:** If the detected version contains a pre-release tag (e.g., `1.0.0-beta.0`, `2.0.0-rc.1`), preserve it as-is. Pre-release tags are valid semver and must not be stripped. When comparing versions during reconciliation, use semver-aware comparison that respects pre-release ordering.
49	
50	## Scope Object Structure
51	
52	```yaml
53	scope:
54	  type: full-library | specific-modules | public-api | component-library | docs-only
55	  include:
56	    - "src/**/*.ts"           # Glob patterns for included files/directories
57	  exclude:
58	    - "**/*.test.*"           # Glob patterns for excluded files
59	    - "**/node_modules/**"
60	  notes: "Optional notes about scope decisions"
61	  # Additional fields when scope.type is "component-library":
62	  # registry_path: "path/to/registry.ts"  # Optional — auto-detected if omitted
63	  # ui_variants:                           # Optional — design system variants
64	  #   - name: "shadcnui"
65	  #     package: "packages/components/react-shadcn"
66	  # demo_patterns:                         # Optional — auto-detected if omitted
67	  #   - "**/demo/**"
68	  #   - "**/*.stories.*"
69	```
70	
71	## YAML Template
72	
73	```yaml
74	---
75	name: "{skill-name}"
76	version: "{detected-version or 1.0.0}"  # Auto-detect from source, fall back to 1.0.0
77	source_type: "source"                    # "source" (default) or "docs-only"
78	source_repo: "{github-url-or-local-path}"
79	language: "{detected-language}"
80	description: "{brief-description}"
81	forge_tier: "{Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep}"
82	created: "{date}"
83	created_by: "{user_name}"
84	scope:
85	  type: "{full-library|specific-modules|public-api|component-library|docs-only}"
86	  include:
87	    - "{pattern}"
88	  exclude:
89	    - "{pattern}"
90	  notes: "{optional-scope-notes}"
91	# Optional: documentation URLs for T3 content (required when source_type: "docs-only")
92	# doc_urls:
93	#   - url: "https://docs.example.com/api"
94	#     label: "API Reference"
95	# scripts_intent: detect         # Optional: detect | none | description
96	# assets_intent: detect          # Optional: detect | none | description
97	# source_authority: community    # Optional: official | community | internal
98	---
99	```
100	
101	## Human-Readable Presentation Format
102	
103	When presenting the brief for confirmation (brief-skill step 04 only — not applicable to analyze-source batch generation), display as:
104	
105	```
106	Skill Brief: {name}
107	====================
108	
109	Target:      {source_repo}
110	Language:    {language}
111	Forge Tier:  {forge_tier}
112	Description: {description}
113	
114	Scope: {scope.type}
115	  Include: {scope.include patterns, one per line}
116	  Exclude: {scope.exclude patterns, one per line}
117	  Notes:   {scope.notes}
118	
119	{If source_type is "docs-only":}
120	Source Type: docs-only
121	Doc URLs:
122	  {doc_urls, one per line with labels}
123	
124	{If source_type is "source" AND supplemental doc_urls collected:}
125	Supplemental Docs:
126	  {doc_urls, one per line with labels}
127	
128	{If scripts_intent or assets_intent was explicitly set (not default "detect"):}
129	Scripts:    {scripts_intent}
130	Assets:     {assets_intent}
131	
132	Source Authority: {source_authority}
133	
134	Version:    {version}
135	Created:    {created}
136	Created by: {created_by}
137	```
138	
139	## Validation Rules
140	
141	1. `name` must be unique within {forge_data_folder}
142	2. `source_repo` must be accessible (gh api for GitHub, path exists for local)
143	3. `language` must be a recognized programming language
144	4. `scope.type` must be one of the five defined types
145	5. `scope.include` must have at least one pattern (exception: `docs-only` scope, where include patterns are optional since no source code is available)
146	6. `forge_tier` must be one of: Quick, Forge, Forge+, Deep (Title Case, must match the tier from forge-tier.yaml, or default to Quick)
147	7. When `source_type: "docs-only"`: `doc_urls` must have >= 1 entry, `source_repo` becomes optional
148	8. Each `doc_urls` entry must have a valid `url` field
149

```
135	- **Quick tier (remote, no clone):** `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/{branch} --jq '.sha'`
136	
137	Store the result as `source_commit` in context. If capture fails (not a git repo, API unavailable), set `source_commit: null` — this is not an error.
138	
139	---
140	
141	## Version Reconciliation (all tiers, source mode only)
142	
143	**If `source_type: "docs-only"`:** skip this section — no source files exist to reconcile.
144	
145	After the source path is accessible (local path from step-01, or ephemeral clone from above), check whether the source contains a version identifier and reconcile it with `brief.version`. Look for the first matching version file in the resolved source path:
146	
147	- Python: `pyproject.toml` (`[project] version`), `setup.py` (`version=`), `__version__` in `__init__.py`
148	- JavaScript/TypeScript: `package.json` (`"version"`). **Monorepo resolution:** When multiple `package.json` files exist (workspace root + packages), resolve version using this priority:
149	  1. Package whose `name` field matches `brief.name` (e.g., the skill's target library name)
150	  2. Package with a `bin` field (CLI entry point — represents the published version)
151	  3. Root workspace `package.json` version (if present)
152	  4. Fall back to `brief.version` if no version found. For monorepos using workspace protocols (pnpm, yarn, npm workspaces), the root `package.json` often has no `version` field — this is expected, not an error.
153	- Rust: `Cargo.toml` (`[package] version`)
154	- Go: `go.mod` (module version if tagged)
155	
156	**If a source version is found AND it differs from `brief.version`:**
157	
158	⚠️ Warn the user: "Brief version ({brief.version}) differs from source version ({source_version}). Using source version ({source_version})."
159	
160	Update the working version in context to the source version. Record the mismatch in context for the evidence report (step-08).
161	
162	**If no version file is found or version cannot be extracted:** keep `brief.version` as-is. No warning needed.
163	
164	**If source is remote and accessed via Quick tier (gh_bridge, no local files):** attempt to read the version file via `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, "{version_file}")` — resolved as `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{version_file}` or direct file read if local (see [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md)) — for the primary version file of the detected language. If the read fails, keep `brief.version`.
165	
```

11	
12	# Step 4: Confirm Brief
13	
14	## STEP GOAL:
15	
16	To present the complete skill brief in human-readable format, highlighting all fields that will be written to skill-brief.yaml, and obtain explicit user approval before writing.
17	
18	## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
19	
20	### Universal Rules:
21	
22	- 🛑 NEVER generate content without user input
23	- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
24	- 🔄 CRITICAL: When loading next step with 'C', ensure entire file is read
25	- 📋 YOU ARE A FACILITATOR, not a content generator
26	- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT In your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
27	- ⚙️ TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK: If any instruction references a subprocess, subagent, or tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread
28	
29	### Role Reinforcement:
30	
31	- ✅ You are a quality reviewer in Ferris Architect mode
32	- ✅ If you already have been given a name, communication_style and identity, continue to use those while playing this new role
33	- ✅ Present information precisely and clearly — this is a confirmation gate
34	- ✅ Highlight anything with low confidence or defaults that the user should verify
35	
36	### Step-Specific Rules:
37	
38	- 🎯 Focus only on presenting and confirming — do not write files yet (that's step 05)
39	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to proceed without explicit user approval
40	- 💬 Approach: Prescriptive — exact presentation format showing all YAML fields
41	- 📋 This is a P2 confirmation gate — the user MUST approve before continuing
42	
43	## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
44	
45	- 🎯 Follow the MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
46	- 💾 Load the schema to ensure all required fields are presented
47	- 📖 This is a mandatory checkpoint — no auto-proceed
48	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to write skill-brief.yaml in this step
49	
50	## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
51	
52	- Available context: All gathered data from steps 01-03 (target, intent, name, tier, language, scope)
53	- Focus: Presenting the complete brief for final review
54	- Limits: Do not write files — only present for approval
55	- Dependencies: Confirmed scope boundaries from step 03
56	
57	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
58	
59	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
60	
61	### 1. Load Schema
62	
63	Load `{briefSchemaFile}` to reference required fields and the human-readable presentation format.
64	
65	### 2. Assemble Complete Brief
66	
67	Compile all gathered data from steps 01-03 into the complete brief:
68	
69	- **name:** {skill name from step 01}
70	- **version:** {auto-detected from source, or "1.0.0" if not found — see schema for detection rules}
71	- **source_repo:** {target repo from step 01}
72	- **language:** {detected/confirmed language from steps 02-03}
73	- **description:** {derived from user intent in step 01}
74	- **forge_tier:** {tier from step 01}
75	- **created:** {current date}
76	- **created_by:** {user_name from config}
77	- **scope.type:** {scope type from step 03}
78	- **scope.include:** {include patterns from step 03}
79	- **scope.exclude:** {exclude patterns from step 03}
80	- **scope.notes:** {any scope notes from step 03}
81	- **source_type:** {source or docs-only, from step 01}
82	- **doc_urls:** {collected documentation URLs with labels, from steps 01/03 — include if source_type is "docs-only" or supplemental URLs were collected}
83	- **scripts_intent:** {detect/none/description from step 03, or "detect" if not explicitly set}
84	- **assets_intent:** {detect/none/description from step 03, or "detect" if not explicitly set}
85	- **source_authority:** {official/community/internal from step 01 — default "community"}
86	
87	### 3. Present Brief for Review
88	
89	Using the presentation format from the schema:
90	
91	"**Please review the complete skill brief before I write it.**
92	
93	---
94	
95	```
96	Skill Brief: {name}
97	====================
98	
99	Target:      {source_repo}
100	Language:    {language}
101	Forge Tier:  {forge_tier}
102	Description: {description}
103	
104	Scope: {scope.type}
105	  Include: {scope.include patterns, one per line}
106	  Exclude: {scope.exclude patterns, one per line}
107	  Notes:   {scope.notes}
108	
109	{If source_type is "docs-only":}
110	Source Type: docs-only
111	Doc URLs:
112	  {doc_urls, one per line with labels}
113	
114	{If source_type is "source" AND supplemental doc_urls collected:}
115	Supplemental Docs:
116	  {doc_urls, one per line with labels}
117	
118	{If scripts_intent or assets_intent was explicitly set (not default "detect"):}
119	Scripts:    {scripts_intent}
120	Assets:     {assets_intent}
121	
122	Source Authority: {source_authority}
123	
124	Version:    {version}
125	Created:    {created}
126	Created by: {created_by}
127	```
128	
129	---"
130	
131	### 4. Highlight Items Needing Attention
132	
133	Flag any fields that may need review:
134	
135	{If language was overridden or low confidence:}
136	"**Note:** Language was {auto-detected / manually overridden}."
137	
138	{If description was derived (not stated by user):}
139	"**Note:** Description was derived from your stated intent. Adjust if needed."
140	
141	{If forge tier was defaulted:}
142	"**Note:** Forge tier defaulted to Quick (no forge-tier.yaml found)."
143	
144	{If any scope patterns seem broad or narrow:}
145	"**Note:** {specific observation about scope breadth}."
146	
147	"**This is your last chance to make changes before writing the file.**
148	
149	You can:
150	- Adjust any field by telling me what to change
151	- Revise scope boundaries by selecting [R]
152	- Proceed to write by selecting [C]"
153	
154	### 5. Handle Inline Adjustments
155	
156	If the user requests changes to specific fields (name, description, version, etc.):
157	- Make the adjustment
158	- Re-present the updated brief
159	- Return to the menu
160	
161	### 6. Present MENU OPTIONS
162	
163	Display: **Select an Option:** [R] Revise Scope [A] Advanced Elicitation [P] Party Mode [C] Approve and Write
164	
165	#### Menu Handling Logic:
166	
167	- IF R: Load, read entire file, then execute {reviseStepFile} to re-enter scope definition
168	- IF A: Invoke {advancedElicitationSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
169	- IF P: Invoke {partyModeSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
170	- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
171	- IF Any other comments or queries: help user respond, apply any field adjustments, re-present brief if changed, then [Redisplay Menu Options](#6-present-menu-options)
172	
173	#### EXECUTION RULES:
174	
175	- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
176	- ONLY proceed to write step when user selects 'C'
177	- After other menu items execution, return to this menu
178	- User can chat, request field changes, or ask questions — always respond and then redisplay menu
179	
180	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
181	
182	ONLY WHEN C is selected and the user has explicitly approved the brief will you load and read fully `./step-05-write-brief.md` to write the skill-brief.yaml file.
183	
184	---
185	
186	## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
187	
188	### ✅ SUCCESS:
189	
190	- Schema loaded for field reference
191	- Complete brief assembled from all gathered data
192	- Brief presented in human-readable format
193	- Low-confidence or defaulted fields highlighted
194	- User explicitly approved the brief
195	- Menu presented with revise option
196	
197	### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
198	
199	- Writing skill-brief.yaml in this step (too early)
200	- Proceeding without explicit user approval
201	- Not presenting all required fields
202	- Not highlighting defaulted or low-confidence values
203	- Not offering the [R] Revise option
204	
205	**Master Rule:** Skipping steps, optimizing sequences, or not following exact instructions is FORBIDDEN and constitutes SYSTEM FAILURE.
206

{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/.claude/plans/reflective-skipping-wolf.md",
  "content": "# Plan: Version-Specific Briefing for Libraries and Documentation\n\n## Context\n\nWith version-aware skill directories now shipped, the *output* pipeline supports multiple versions. But the *input* pipeline has no way to target a specific version. Users cannot tell the brief-skill workflow \"I want a skill for cognee v0.5.0\" — it always auto-detects whatever version the source happens to be at. Worse, for docs-only skills (no source repo), the version defaults to `1.0.0`, which is almost always wrong.\n\nMany documentation sites are organized by version (e.g., `docs.example.com/v2/` vs `docs.example.com/v3/`). Users need to specify the correct doc URL for their target version, and the system should validate this coupling.\n\n**Party mode consensus:** Add a `target_version` field that separates user intent from auto-detection. For doc URLs, use a simple confirmation prompt rather than pattern-based URL templates.\n\n---\n\n## Design\n\n### New field: `target_version`\n\nAn optional field in the skill brief schema. When present:\n- It becomes the authoritative version for the skill (overrides auto-detection)\n- Version reconciliation in create-skill respects it (no warning, intentional override)\n- For docs-only skills, it serves as the primary version source (no more defaulting to `1.0.0`)\n\n### Doc URL version confirmation\n\nWhen `target_version` is set AND `doc_urls` are provided, the workflow prompts:\n> \"You're targeting version {target_version}. Do these doc URLs match that version? [Y/N]\"\n\nNo pattern magic — explicit human confirmation. Simple and robust across all doc URL formats.\n\n---\n\n## Files to Modify\n\n### 1. Schema (both copies)\n- `src/workflows/brief-skill/data/skill-brief-schema.md`\n- `src/workflows/analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md`\n\n**Changes:**\n- Add `target_version` to Optional Fields table: `target_version | string | Semantic version | User-specified target version. Overrides auto-detection. Required for docs-only skills when version is known.`\n- Update Version Detection section: add paragraph explaining that when `target_version` is present, auto-detection still runs but the `target_version` takes precedence. The detected version is recorded for reference but does not override.\n- Update YAML Template: add commented `# target_version: \"X.Y.Z\"` line\n- Update Presentation Format: show `Target Version: {target_version}` when set, and `Detected Version: {version}` separately\n\n### 2. brief-skill step-01 (gather-intent)\n- `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`\n\n**Changes in section 3 (Gather Target Repository):**\n- After collecting the target repo/docs, add a new question:\n  > \"Are you targeting a specific version of this library? (Leave blank to auto-detect from source)\"\n- If user provides a version: store as `target_version`, set `version` to this value\n- If blank: proceed as today (auto-detect in step-02)\n- For docs-only skills: emphasize this question — \"Since this is a docs-only skill, specifying the version is recommended (otherwise defaults to 1.0.0)\"\n\n**Changes in section 3 (doc_urls collection):**\n- When `target_version` is set AND `doc_urls` are being collected (either docs-only or supplemental):\n  > \"You're targeting version {target_version}. Do these documentation URLs correspond to that version?\"\n- If user says no: prompt them to provide version-correct URLs\n- If yes: proceed\n\n**Changes in section 7 (Summarize):**\n- Show `Target Version: {target_version}` when set\n\n### 3. brief-skill step-02 (analyze-target)\n- `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md`\n\n**Changes in section 4b (Detect Source Version):**\n- If `target_version` was provided in step-01:\n  - Still run auto-detection for informational purposes\n  - Display: \"**Target version:** {target_version} (user-specified)\"\n  - If auto-detected version differs: \"**Detected version:** {detected_version} — differs from target, using target version\"\n  - Store `target_version` as the brief's `version` field\n- If no `target_version`: behavior unchanged (auto-detect, default to 1.0.0)\n\n### 4. brief-skill step-04 (confirm-brief)\n- `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-04-confirm-brief.md`\n\n**Changes in section 2 (Assemble):**\n- Add `target_version` to the assembly list\n\n**Changes in section 3 (Present):**\n- When `target_version` is set, show both:\n  ```\n  Target Version: {target_version} (user-specified)\n  Detected Version: {detected_version or \"N/A\"}\n  ```\n- When not set, show as today: `Version: {version}`\n\n**Changes in section 4 (Highlight):**\n- If `target_version` differs from detected version, flag it:\n  > \"Note: Target version ({target_version}) differs from detected source version ({detected_version}). The target version will be used.\"\n\n### 5. brief-skill step-05 (write-brief)\n- `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md`\n\n**Changes:**\n- Include `target_version` field in the YAML output when it was set\n\n### 6. create-skill source-resolution-protocols\n- `src/workflows/create-skill/data/source-resolution-protocols.md`\n\n**Changes in Version Reconciliation section:**\n- Add a check at the top: \"If `brief.target_version` is present, use it as the authoritative version. Do NOT warn about brief-vs-source mismatch — the user intentionally specified this version.\"\n- Existing reconciliation logic (warn when brief.version differs from source) still applies when `target_version` is absent\n\n### 7. analyze-source step-06 (generate-briefs)\n- `src/workflows/analyze-source/steps-c/step-06-generate-briefs.md`\n\n**Changes:**\n- No changes needed. Analyze-source generates briefs with auto-detected versions — it discovers what's in the repo, not what the user wants. `target_version` is a user-intent field relevant to brief-skill's interactive flow. Batch-generated briefs from analyze-source use detected versions.\n\n### 8. quick-skill step-01 (resolve-target)\n- `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-01-resolve-target.md`\n\n**Changes:**\n- Quick-skill accepts a package name or URL. If the user appends `@version` (e.g., `QS cognee@0.5.0`), parse it as the target version\n- Store as `target_version` in the extraction context\n- This follows npm/pip convention and feels natural\n\n---\n\n## Files NOT Modified\n\n- `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-fetch-docs.md` — doc fetcher fetches whatever URLs are in the brief, which the user has already confirmed match the target version. No changes needed.\n- `src/knowledge/doc-fetcher.md` — no changes needed\n- `src/knowledge/version-paths.md` — already handles versioned output; input versioning is orthogonal\n\n---\n\n## Verification\n\n1. **brief-skill with target_version:** Run BS for a library, specify v0.5.0 as target version. Verify the brief YAML contains `target_version: \"0.5.0\"` and `version: \"0.5.0\"`.\n2. **brief-skill without target_version:** Run BS without specifying version. Verify behavior is unchanged (auto-detect).\n3. **docs-only with target_version:** Run BS with docs-only source type, specify version. Verify version is not `1.0.0` but the user-specified value.\n4. **doc URL version confirmation:** Provide target_version AND doc_urls. Verify the workflow asks whether URLs match the target version.\n5. **create-skill respects target_version:** Run CS with a brief that has `target_version`. Verify no warning about version mismatch, and the skill is compiled at the target version.\n6. **quick-skill @version:** Run `QS cognee@0.5.0`. Verify the version is parsed and used.\n"
}

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Version-Specific Briefing for Libraries and Documentation

## Context

With version-aware skill directories now shipped, the *output* pipeline supports multiple versions. But the *input* pipeline has no way to target a specific version. Users cannot tell the brief-skill workflow "I want a skill for cognee v0.5.0" — it always auto-detects whatever version the source happens to be at. Worse, for docs-only skills (no source repo), the version defaults to `1.0.0`, which is almost always wrong.

Many documentation sites are organized by version (e.g., `docs.example.com/v2/` vs `docs.example.com/v3/`). Users need to specify the correct doc URL for their target version, and the system should validate this coupling.

**Party mode consensus:** Add a `target_version` field that separates user intent from auto-detection. For doc URLs, use a simple confirmation prompt rather than pattern-based URL templates.

---

## Design

### New field: `target_version`

An optional field in the skill brief schema. When present:
- It becomes the authoritative version for the skill (overrides auto-detection)
- Version reconciliation in create-skill respects it (no warning, intentional override)
- For docs-only skills, it serves as the primary version source (no more defaulting to `1.0.0`)

### Doc URL version confirmation

When `target_version` is set AND `doc_urls` are provided, the workflow prompts:
> "You're targeting version {target_version}. Do these doc URLs match that version? [Y/N]"

No pattern magic — explicit human confirmation. Simple and robust across all doc URL formats.

---

## Files to Modify

### 1. Schema (both copies)
- `src/workflows/brief-skill/data/skill-brief-schema.md`
- `src/workflows/analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md`

**Changes:**
- Add `target_version` to Optional Fields table: `target_version | string | Semantic version | User-specified target version. Overrides auto-detection. Required for docs-only skills when version is known.`
- Update Version Detection section: add paragraph explaining that when `target_version` is present, auto-detection still runs but the `target_version` takes precedence. The detected version is recorded for reference but does not override.
- Update YAML Template: add commented `# target_version: "X.Y.Z"` line
- Update Presentation Format: show `Target Version: {target_version}` when set, and `Detected Version: {version}` separately

### 2. brief-skill step-01 (gather-intent)
- `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`

**Changes in section 3 (Gather Target Repository):**
- After collecting the target repo/docs, add a new question:
  > "Are you targeting a specific version of this library? (Leave blank to auto-detect from source)"
- If user provides a version: store as `target_version`, set `version` to this value
- If blank: proceed as today (auto-detect in step-02)
- For docs-only skills: emphasize this question — "Since this is a docs-only skill, specifying the version is recommended (otherwise defaults to 1.0.0)"

**Changes in section 3 (doc_urls collection):**
- When `target_version` is set AND `doc_urls` are being collected (either docs-only or supplemental):
  > "You're targeting version {target_version}. Do these documentation URLs correspond to that version?"
- If user says no: prompt them to provide version-correct URLs
- If yes: proceed

**Changes in section 7 (Summarize):**
- Show `Target Version: {target_version}` when set

### 3. brief-skill step-02 (analyze-target)
- `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md`

**Changes in section 4b (Detect Source Version):**
- If `target_version` was provided in step-01:
  - Still run auto-detection for informational purposes
  - Display: "**Target version:** {target_version} (user-specified)"
  - If auto-detected version differs: "**Detected version:** {detected_version} — differs from target, using target version"
  - Store `target_version` as the brief's `version` field
- If no `target_version`: behavior unchanged (auto-detect, default to 1.0.0)

### 4. brief-skill step-04 (confirm-brief)
- `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-04-confirm-brief.md`

**Changes in section 2 (Assemble):**
- Add `target_version` to the assembly list

**Changes in section 3 (Present):**
- When `target_version` is set, show both:
  ```
  Target Version: {target_version} (user-specified)
  Detected Version: {detected_version or "N/A"}
  ```
- When not set, show as today: `Version: {version}`

**Changes in section 4 (Highlight):**
- If `target_version` differs from detected version, flag it:
  > "Note: Target version ({target_version}) differs from detected source version ({detected_version}). The target version will be used."

### 5. brief-skill step-05 (write-brief)
- `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md`

**Changes:**
- Include `target_version` field in the YAML output when it was set

### 6. create-skill source-resolution-protocols
- `src/workflows/create-skill/data/source-resolution-protocols.md`

**Changes in Version Reconciliation section:**
- Add a check at the top: "If `brief.target_version` is present, use it as the authoritative version. Do NOT warn about brief-vs-source mismatch — the user intentionally specified this version."
- Existing reconciliation logic (warn when brief.version differs from source) still applies when `target_version` is absent

### 7. analyze-source step-06 (generate-briefs)
- `src/workflows/analyze-source/steps-c/step-06-generate-briefs.md`

**Changes:**
- No changes needed. Analyze-source generates briefs with auto-detected versions — it discovers what's in the repo, not what the user wants. `target_version` is a user-intent field relevant to brief-skill's interactive flow. Batch-generated briefs from analyze-source use detected versions.

### 8. quick-skill step-01 (resolve-target)
- `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-01-resolve-target.md`

**Changes:**
- Quick-skill accepts a package name or URL. If the user appends `@version` (e.g., `QS cognee@0.5.0`), parse it as the target version
- Store as `target_version` in the extraction context
- This follows npm/pip convention and feels natural

---

## Files NOT Modified

- `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-fetch-docs.md` — doc fetcher fetches whatever URLs are in the brief, which the user has already confirmed match the target version. No changes needed.
- `src/knowledge/doc-fetcher.md` — no changes needed
- `src/knowledge/version-paths.md` — already handles versioned output; input versioning is orthogonal

---

## Verification

1. **brief-skill with target_version:** Run BS for a library, specify v0.5.0 as target version. Verify the brief YAML contains `target_version: "0.5.0"` and `version: "0.5.0"`.
2. **brief-skill without target_version:** Run BS without specifying version. Verify behavior is unchanged (auto-detect).
3. **docs-only with target_version:** Run BS with docs-only source type, specify version. Verify version is not `1.0.0` but the user-specified value.
4. **doc URL version confirmation:** Provide target_version AND doc_urls. Verify the workflow asks whether URLs match the target version.
5. **create-skill respects target_version:** Run CS with a brief that has `target_version`. Verify no warning about version mismatch, and the skill is compiled at the target version.
6. **quick-skill @version:** Run `QS cognee@0.5.0`. Verify the version is parsed and used.

```
1	# Skill Brief Schema
2	
3	## Purpose
4	
5	Defines the output contract for skill-brief.yaml files generated by analyze-source. Each generated brief must conform to this schema to be consumable by brief-skill and create-skill downstream workflows.
6	
7	## Required Fields
8	
9	| Field | Type | Constraint | Description |
10	|-------|------|------------|-------------|
11	| name | string | kebab-case `[a-z0-9-]+` | Unique skill identifier |
12	| version | string | Semantic version (`X.Y.Z` or `X.Y.Z-prerelease`) | Auto-detect from source (see Version Detection below), fall back to `1.0.0` |
13	| source_repo | string | GitHub URL or local path | Repository or project root (optional when `source_type: "docs-only"`) |
14	| language | string | Recognized language | Primary programming language |
15	| scope | object | See Scope Object below | Boundary definition |
16	| description | string | 1-3 sentences | What the skill covers |
17	| forge_tier | string | `Quick` / `Forge` / `Forge+` / `Deep` | Inherited from forge-tier.yaml (Title Case) |
18	| created | string | ISO date `YYYY-MM-DD` | Generation date |
19	| created_by | string | user_name from config | Who generated the brief |
20	
21	## Optional Fields
22	
23	| Field | Type | Constraint | Description |
24	|-------|------|------------|-------------|
25	| source_type | string | `source` or `docs-only` | Default `source`. When `docs-only`: `source_repo` optional, `doc_urls` required |
26	| doc_urls | array | `{url, label}` objects | Documentation URLs for T3 content. Required when `source_type: "docs-only"` |
27	| `scripts_intent` | string | `detect` / `none` / free-text | Describes whether scripts should be extracted. Values: `detect` (auto-detect from source — default when absent), `none` (skip scripts), or a free-text description of expected scripts (e.g., "CLI validation tools in bin/"). |
28	| `assets_intent` | string | `detect` / `none` / free-text | Describes whether assets should be extracted. Values: `detect` (auto-detect from source — default when absent), `none` (skip assets), or a free-text description of expected assets (e.g., "JSON schemas in schemas/"). |
29	| `source_authority` | string | `official` / `community` / `internal` | Default `community`. Set to `official` only when the skill creator is the library maintainer. Forced to `community` when `source_type: "docs-only"`. |
30	
31	When `source_type: "docs-only"`:
32	- `source_repo` becomes optional (set to doc site URL for reference)
33	- `doc_urls` must have at least one entry
34	- `source_authority` is forced to `community` (T3 external documentation cannot be `official`)
35	- All extracted content gets `[EXT:{url}]` citations
36	
37	## Version Detection
38	
39	During brief generation, attempt to auto-detect the source version before defaulting to `"1.0.0"`. Check the first matching file in the source:
40	
41	- **Python:** `pyproject.toml` `[project] version` (static) → if `dynamic = ["version"]`, check `__init__.py` for `__version__` → `_version.py` if exists → `setup.py` `version=` → `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`
42	- **JavaScript/TypeScript:** `package.json` (`"version"`)
43	- **Rust:** `Cargo.toml` `[package] version` (static) → if `version = { workspace = true }`, resolve from workspace root `Cargo.toml` → `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`
44	- **Go:** version tag from `go.mod` or `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`
45	
46	If the source is a remote GitHub repo, use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{file}` to read the version file. If the source is local, read the file directly.
47	
48	If detection succeeds, use the detected version. If it fails or returns a non-semver value, fall back to `"1.0.0"`.
49	
50	The create-skill workflow (step-03-extract) also performs version reconciliation at extraction time — if the source version has changed since the brief was created, the extraction step warns and uses the source version.
51	
52	**Pre-release handling:** If the detected version contains a pre-release tag (e.g., `1.0.0-beta.0`, `2.0.0-rc.1`), preserve it as-is. Pre-release tags are valid semver and must not be stripped. When comparing versions during reconciliation, use semver-aware comparison that respects pre-release ordering.
53	
54	## Scope Object
55	
56	```yaml
57	scope:
58	  type: full-library | specific-modules | public-api | component-library | docs-only
59	  include:
60	    - 'src/**/*.ts'          # At least one required
61	  exclude:
62	    - 'src/**/*.test.ts'     # Optional
63	  notes: 'Optional rationale for scope decision'
64	  # Additional fields when scope.type is "component-library":
65	  # registry_path: "path/to/registry.ts"  # Optional — auto-detected if omitted
66	  # ui_variants:                           # Optional — design system variants
67	  #   - name: "shadcnui"
68	  #     package: "packages/components/react-shadcn"
69	  # demo_patterns:                         # Optional — auto-detected if omitted
70	  #   - "**/demo/**"
71	  #   - "**/*.stories.*"
72	```
73	
74	### Scope Types
75	
76	| Type | Use When |
77	|------|----------|
78	| full-library | Entire codebase of a unit |
79	| specific-modules | Selected components or packages |
80	| public-api | Only exported interfaces |
81	| component-library | UI component libraries with registries, props-based APIs, and design system variants |
82	| docs-only | When source_type is docs-only — no source code available, all content from doc_urls |
83	
84	## YAML Template
85	
86	```yaml
87	name: '{unit-name}'
88	version: '{detected-version or 1.0.0}'
89	source_type: 'source'
90	source_repo: '{project_path}'
91	language: '{detected_language}'
92	scope:
93	  type: '{detected_scope_type}'
94	  include:
95	    - '{include_pattern}'
96	  exclude:
97	    - '{exclude_pattern}'
98	  notes: '{scope_rationale}'
99	description: '{1-3 sentence description}'
100	forge_tier: '{Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep}'
101	created: '{current_date}'
102	created_by: '{user_name}'
103	```
104	
105	## Validation Rules
106	
107	1. **Name uniqueness**: No duplicate names within forge_data_folder
108	2. **Source accessibility**: source_repo path must exist or be reachable
109	3. **Language recognized**: Must be a known programming language
110	4. **Scope type valid**: Must match one of the five defined types (full-library, specific-modules, public-api, component-library, docs-only)
111	5. **Include patterns**: At least one include glob pattern required (exception: `docs-only` scope, where include patterns are optional since no source code is available)
112	6. **Forge tier match**: Must match value in forge-tier.yaml
113	7. **Docs-only mode**: When `source_type: "docs-only"`, `doc_urls` required (>= 1), `source_repo` optional
114	8. Each `doc_urls` entry must have a valid `url` field
115	
116	## Output Location
117	
118	Each skill-brief.yaml is written to:
119	```
120	{forge_data_folder}/{unit-name}/skill-brief.yaml
121	```
122	
```

docs/examples.md:68:Skills are stored per-version — updating cognee to v0.6.0 creates a new version directory without overwriting v0.5.5. The `active` symlink always points to the current version. The `scripts/` and `assets/` directories appear only when the source repository contains them. Each file traces back to its source with provenance citations and SHA-256 hashes.
docs/how-it-works.md:93:SKF takes a different approach: it mechanically extracts function signatures, type definitions, and usage patterns from source code via AST parsing, enriches them with documentation and developer discourse, then compiles everything into version-pinned skills that comply with the [agentskills.io specification](https://agentskills.io/specification). Every instruction traces to its source — nothing is generated from training data.
docs/how-it-works.md:141:# 4. Reset to tier_override: ~ (auto-detect)
docs/how-it-works.md:144:Set `tier_override` to `Quick`, `Forge`, `Forge+`, or `Deep`. Set to `~` (null) to return to auto-detection. The override is respected by all tier-aware workflows (CS, SS, US, AS, TS).
docs/how-it-works.md:154:| **T1** | AST extraction | `ast_bridge` | Current code, structurally verified. Immutable for that version. |
docs/how-it-works.md:293:Every generated skill produces a self-contained, version-aware directory:
docs/how-it-works.md:297:├── active -> {version}           # Symlink to current version
docs/how-it-works.md:298:├── {version}/
docs/how-it-works.md:310:└── {older-version}/
docs/how-it-works.md:311:    └── {name}/                   # Previous version preserved
docs/how-it-works.md:315:Multiple versions coexist under the same skill name. The `active` symlink points to the current version. Updating a skill for a new library release creates a new version directory — users pinned to older versions keep their skill intact. The inner `{name}/` directory is a standalone [agentskills.io](https://agentskills.io) package, directly installable via `npx skills add`.

84	---
85	
86	## Version Pinning
87	
88	Every skill records the exact version (or commit) of the source code it was built from. This means you always know which version of the library the instructions apply to.
89	
90	When the source updates, you can re-run `@Ferris US` (update-skill) to regenerate the skill for the new version while preserving any manual additions you've made.
91	
92	---
93	
94	## BMAD Module
95	
96	SKF is a plugin (called a "module") for the [BMad Method](https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD), a framework for running structured AI workflows. BMad provides the workflow engine — step-by-step execution, shared knowledge bases, and consistent outputs. SKF plugs into that engine and focuses specifically on skill compilation.
97	
98	You don't need to know BMad to use SKF. The standalone installer sets everything up.
```

This means you always know which version of the library the instructions apply to.\n\nBy default, the version is auto-detected from the source (package.json, pyproject.toml, etc.). You can also target a specific version — either by specifying it during `@Ferris BS` (brief-skill) or by appending `@version` to a quick skill command (`@Ferris QS cognee@0.5.0`). This is especially useful for docs-only skills where no source code is available for auto-detection.\n\nWhen the source updates, you can re-run `@Ferris US` (update-skill) to regenerate the skill for the new version while preserving any manual additions you've made."
}
