id: toolu_017jQhitXNnZDE4VgZcnAp5C
```
1	# Source Resolution Protocols
2	
3	## Remote Source Resolution (Forge/Deep only)
4	
5	If `source_repo` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
6	
7	If `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:
8	
9	1. **Check `git` availability:** Verify `git` is functional (`git --version`). If `git` is not available, skip to the fallback warning below.
10	
11	2. **Ephemeral shallow clone:** Clone the repository to a system temp path for AST access:
12	
13	   ```
14	   temp_path = {system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/
15	   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
16	   ```
17	
18	   **If `include_patterns` are NOT specified:**
19	
20	   ```
21	   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
22	   ```
23	
24	   **If `include_patterns` ARE specified**, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope:
25	
26	   ```
27	   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo} {temp_path}
28	   ```
29	
30	   **Mode selection:** Choose sparse-checkout mode based on whether `exclude_patterns` exist:
31	
32	   - **No `exclude_patterns`:** Use default **cone mode** (faster). Convert `include_patterns` to directory roots.
33	   - **`exclude_patterns` present:** Use **`--no-cone` mode** which supports gitignore-style negation patterns (`!` prefix). This applies both include and exclude at the git level, avoiding unnecessary blob downloads.
34	
35	   **Cone mode (no exclude patterns):**
36	
37	   **IMPORTANT:** `git sparse-checkout set` expects **directories**, not glob patterns. Convert `include_patterns` before passing them:
38	
39	   **Classification rule:** A pattern is an **individual file** if it contains no glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) AND does not end with `/`. Everything else is a glob — strip it to its directory root (the path prefix before the first glob character or wildcard segment).
40	
41	   - Strip glob suffixes to directory roots (e.g., `src/core/**/*.py` → `src/core`, `src/api/*.ts` → `src/api`)
42	   - Deduplicate the resulting directory list
43	   - Individual files (e.g., `pyproject.toml`, `src/utils/helpers.py`) are kept as-is
44	
45	   **If only directory roots (no individual files):**
46	
47	   ```
48	   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {converted_directory_roots}
49	   ```
50	
51	   **If any individual files are present (or mixed):**
52	
53	   ```
54	   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {converted_directory_roots} {individual_files}
55	   ```
56	
57	   Example transformation:
58	   ```
59	   Brief include_patterns:        sparse-checkout args:
60	   src/core/**/*.py          →    src/core          (directory root)
61	   src/api/*.ts              →    src/api           (directory root)
62	   examples/**/*.py          →    examples          (directory root)
63	   pyproject.toml            →    pyproject.toml    (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
64	   src/utils/helpers.py      →    src/utils/helpers.py (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
65	   ```
66	
67	   **No-cone mode (exclude patterns present):**
68	
69	   When `exclude_patterns` exist, use `--no-cone` mode to pass both include and exclude patterns directly as gitignore-style rules:
70	
71	   1. Convert `include_patterns` to gitignore-style patterns. For patterns without glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) that do not end with `/`, apply the **file-detection heuristic**: if the last path segment contains a `.` (file extension), it is an **individual file** — prepend `/` and keep as-is (e.g., `packages/registry/registry.json` → `/packages/registry/registry.json`). If the last path segment has no extension, it is a **bare directory name** — append `/**` (e.g., `cognee` → `cognee/**`). Patterns that already contain glob characters are kept as-is (e.g., `cognee/**` → kept as-is).
72	   2. Convert `exclude_patterns` to negation patterns by prepending `!`. Apply the same file-detection heuristic: individual files (last segment has `.` extension) get `!/` prefix only (e.g., `src/internal/config.json` → `!/src/internal/config.json`); bare directory names get `/**` appended (e.g., `cognee/tests` → `!cognee/tests/**`). Patterns with globs are kept as-is with `!` prefix (e.g., `cognee/tests/**` → `!cognee/tests/**`; `**/test_*` → `!**/test_*`).
73	   3. **CRITICAL:** List all include patterns BEFORE negated exclude patterns — git processes patterns in order and a negation can only suppress a prior inclusion.
74	   4. Pass to sparse-checkout — include patterns first, then negated exclude patterns:
75	
76	   ```
77	   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --no-cone {include_gitignore_patterns} {negated_exclude_patterns}
78	   ```
79	
80	   Example transformation:
81	   ```
82	   Brief include_patterns:          Brief exclude_patterns:
83	   cognee/**                        cognee/tests/**
84	   packages/registry/registry.json  cognee/alembic/**
85	                                    **/test_*
86	
87	   sparse-checkout args (--no-cone):
88	   'cognee/**' '/packages/registry/registry.json' '!cognee/tests/**' '!cognee/alembic/**' '!**/test_*'
89	   ```
90	
91	   Note: `registry.json` is an individual file (has `.json` extension), so it gets `/` prefix instead of `/**` suffix.
92	
93	   **Note:** `--no-cone` mode is slower than cone mode for very large repositories but eliminates downloading excluded blobs entirely.
94	
95	   **Always-included root files:**
96	
97	   Regardless of `include_patterns`, always add these root-level version/manifest files to the sparse-checkout pattern list. These are needed for version reconciliation and must not require a fallback to `gh api`:
98	
99	   `pyproject.toml`, `package.json`, `Cargo.toml`, `go.mod`, `setup.py`, `setup.cfg`, `VERSION`
100	
101	   In cone mode, always use the `--skip-checks` command form when adding these files — even if `include_patterns` resolved to only directory roots (which would normally use the form without `--skip-checks`). The command becomes: `git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {directory_roots} pyproject.toml package.json Cargo.toml go.mod setup.py setup.cfg VERSION`. In no-cone mode, list them as explicit include patterns before any negation patterns. Do not flag them as extraneous inclusions during post-checkout filtering.
102	
103	   **Post-checkout filtering:**
104	
105	   After checkout, apply the original glob `include_patterns` as file-level filters when building the extraction file list — sparse-checkout gets the right directories, glob filtering narrows to the exact files. When `--no-cone` mode was used, most exclude filtering is already done at the git level, but apply `exclude_patterns` as a final pass to catch any edge cases where gitignore pattern matching diverges from the brief's glob semantics. Always-included root files (see above) are exempt from post-checkout filtering.
106	
107	3. **If clone succeeds:** Update the working source path to `{temp_path}` for all subsequent AST operations in this step. Capture the source commit: `git -C {temp_path} rev-parse HEAD` — store as `source_commit` in context. Proceed with the **Forge/Deep Tier** extraction strategy below. Mark `ephemeral_clone_active = true` for cleanup.
108	
109	4. **If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):**
110	
111	   ⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:**
112	
113	   "Ephemeral clone of `{source_repo}` failed: {error}. Degrading to source reading (T1-low) for this run. For T1 (AST-verified) confidence, clone the repository locally and update `source_repo` in your brief to the local path."
114	
115	   Proceed with Quick tier extraction strategy below. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
116	
117	**Ephemeral clone cleanup:** After extraction is complete for all files in scope (whether successful or partially failed), before presenting the Gate 2 summary (Section 6), if `ephemeral_clone_active`:
118	
119	1. **Reset working directory first:** Before deleting the clone, ensure the shell working directory is not inside the temp path. Run `cd {project-root}` using the **absolute path** captured at workflow start (not a relative path). This prevents `getcwd` errors when the temp directory is deleted while it is the shell's cwd — which happens if `cd {temp_path}` was used during CCC init or extraction operations.
120	2. **Delete the clone:** `rm -rf {temp_path}`
121	3. **Log:** "Ephemeral source clone cleaned up."
122	
123	This ensures cleanup runs even if some extractions failed, as long as the step itself is still executing. **If any error halts the extraction step before Gate 2 is reached**, cleanup must still occur: reset cwd to `{project-root}` and attempt to delete `{temp_path}` before halting. Log the cleanup attempt regardless of success.
124	
125	---
126	
127	## Source Commit Capture (all tiers, source mode only)
128	
129	**If `source_type: "docs-only"`:** skip — set `source_commit: null`.
130	
131	After the source path is accessible, capture the current commit hash for provenance tracking:
132	
133	- **Local path:** `git -C {source_root} rev-parse HEAD` — if the path is a git repo
134	- **Ephemeral clone (Forge/Deep):** already captured during clone (step 3 above)
135	- **Quick tier (remote, no clone):** `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/{branch} --jq '.sha'`
136	
137	Store the result as `source_commit` in context. If capture fails (not a git repo, API unavailable), set `source_commit: null` — this is not an error.
138	
139	---
140	
141	## Version Reconciliation (all tiers, source mode only)
142	
143	**Target version override:** If `brief.target_version` is present, use it as the authoritative version for the skill. Do NOT warn about a brief-vs-source version mismatch — the user intentionally specified this version. Set the working version to `brief.target_version` and skip the rest of this reconciliation section. The `target_version` field indicates deliberate user intent (e.g., targeting an older version, or providing the version for a docs-only skill).
144	
145	**If `source_type: "docs-only"`:** skip this section — no source files exist to reconcile.
146	
147	After the source path is accessible (local path from step-01, or ephemeral clone from above), check whether the source contains a version identifier and reconcile it with `brief.version`. Look for the first matching version file in the resolved source path:
148	
149	- Python: `pyproject.toml` (`[project] version`), `setup.py` (`version=`), `__version__` in `__init__.py`
150	- JavaScript/TypeScript: `package.json` (`"version"`). **Monorepo resolution:** When multiple `package.json` files exist (workspace root + packages), resolve version using this priority:
151	  1. Package whose `name` field matches `brief.name` (e.g., the skill's target library name)
152	  2. Package with a `bin` field (CLI entry point — represents the published version)
153	  3. Root workspace `package.json` version (if present)
154	  4. Fall back to `brief.version` if no version found. For monorepos using workspace protocols (pnpm, yarn, npm workspaces), the root `package.json` often has no `version` field — this is expected, not an error.
155	- Rust: `Cargo.toml` (`[package] version`)
156	- Go: `go.mod` (module version if tagged)
157	
158	**If a source version is found AND it differs from `brief.version`:**
159	
160	⚠️ Warn the user: "Brief version ({brief.version}) differs from source version ({source_version}). Using source version ({source_version})."
161	
162	Update the working version in context to the source version. Record the mismatch in context for the evidence report (step-08).
163	
164	**If no version file is found or version cannot be extracted:** keep `brief.version` as-is. No warning needed.
165	
166	**If source is remote and accessed via Quick tier (gh_bridge, no local files):** attempt to read the version file via `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, "{version_file}")` — resolved as `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{version_file}` or direct file read if local (see [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md)) — for the primary version file of the detected language. Here's the honest answer:

**SKF does NOT checkout a specific git tag or branch when you specify a target version.** Here's what actually happens:

1. **Ephemeral clone** (Forge/Deep tier): `git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch}` — this clones the **default branch HEAD**, not a specific tag. The `{branch}` comes from the repo's default branch, not from the version.

2. **Quick tier** (remote): Reads files via `gh api` from the default branch.

3. **Local path**: Uses whatever's on disk.

4. **Version reconciliation**: Reads `package.json`/`pyproject.toml`/etc. to detect the version, then compares with the brief's version. If `target_version` is set, it skips the warning — but **the extracted code is still from HEAD, not from the tagged version**.

**So `target_version` as currently implemented is just a label.** If you say "target v0.5.0" but the repo is at v0.6.0, the SKILL.md will say "v0.5.0" but the extracted signatures come from v0.6.0's code. That's misleading.

To actually extract from a specific version, the clone command needs to use the git tag:

```
git clone --depth 1 --branch v0.5.0 --single-branch ...
```

This is a real gap. Want me to fix the source resolution protocol to clone from the target version's git tag when `target_version` is specified?

Based on my thorough exploration of the Skill Forge source resolution system, here's the complete end-to-end understanding:

## **Source Resolution: End-to-End Analysis**

### **1. WHERE `{branch}` IS DEFINED & RESOLVED**

#### **Brief Schema** (`skill-brief-schema.md`)
- **Field:** `source_branch` (optional, not mandatory)
- **Default:** If not specified, "detect default branch" (per step-01-load-brief.md line 118)
- **Current Status:** The schema **documents** `source_branch` as an optional field but **does NOT detail the resolution mechanism**

#### **Create-Skill Workflow - Step 01 (Load Brief)**
- **Line 99:** Lists optional field: `source_branch — branch to use (default: main/master)`
- **Line 118:** States: "If branch not specified, detect default branch" — **but provides NO implementation detail**
- **Line 117:** References resolving via `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` using `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{branch}?recursive=1`
- **Resolution gap:** The step file says "detect default branch" but never shows the actual command to fetch it

#### **Source Resolution Protocols** (`source-resolution-protocols.md`)
Lines 15, 21, 27 show the clone command template:

git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
```
- **`{branch}` placeholder is used but NOT defined**
- No logic specifies what `{branch}` should be if `source_branch` is not in the brief

#### **Update-Skill Workflow - Remote Source Resolution**
Line 13-14 (remote-source-resolution.md):
```
No `--branch` flag is used — the clone targets the remote's default branch, which must match the branch used during the original [CS] Create Skill run.
```
- **Critical finding:** Update-skill **deliberately omits `--branch`**, forcing it to use the repository's default branch
- **Requirement:** This assumes the original create-skill also used the default branch, but there's no validation mechanism to ensure this

---

### **2. DOES THE BRIEF SCHEMA HAVE A `branch` FIELD?**

- ✅ `source_branch` is documented as **optional** (skill-brief-schema.md, line 99)

- ❌ **NOT** in the "Required Fields" table (lines 3-15)
- ❌ **NOT** in the "Optional Fields" table (lines 18-27) — this is the gap
- ❌ **No validation rules** for `source_branch` in the schema validation section (lines 147-158)

**Schema gap identified:** The field is mentioned in step-01-load-brief.md as optional but is missing from the official schema definition in skill-brief-schema.md.

### **3. HOW IS `{branch}` ACTUALLY RESOLVED?**

**Current implementation gap:** The documentation does NOT show a concrete implementation. Based on what IS documented:

2. **If `source_branch` is NOT in brief:** Instructions say "detect default branch" but provide NO mechanism:
   - Likely intended: `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` → parse `default_branch` field

- Step 01 (step-01-load-brief.md, section 4: "Resolve Source Code Location")
- Currently only mentions: "If branch not specified, detect default branch" (line 118)

### **4. QUICK TIER (NO CLONE) — GH API PATH**

From **step-03-extract.md** (Quick Tier section, lines 148-149):
```
Tool resolution for gh_bridge: Use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{branch}?recursive=1` for list_tree, 
`gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}` for read_file. If source is local, use direct file listing/reading instead.
```

**For version targeting at Quick tier:**
- Source-resolution-protocols.md, line 135:

  ```
- **No tag support shown** — only branch-based commit fetching

### **5. VERSION DETECTION & GIT TAGS**

**Where tags could be integrated:**

1. **Brief-skill Step 02** (step-02-analyze-target.md, lines 162-186):

   - Can accept `@version` suffix in user input (step-01-resolve-target.md, lines 72-80)

2. **Create-skill Step 03** (step-03-extract.md):
   - Performs "Version Reconciliation" after source access
   - Could be extended to **tag-aware branch selection**

3. **update-skill workflow:**
   - Currently **ignores branch entirely** (forces default)
   - Could be enhanced to **re-clone from a specific tag** if version changed

### **6. UPDATE-SKILL CLONING BEHAVIOR**

From **remote-source-resolution.md** (lines 12-19):

- ✅ **Does clone** for remote sources at Forge/Deep tier
- ✅ Uses sparse-checkout (only changed files)
- ❌ **Does NOT accept `--branch`** — always uses remote's default branch

## **KEY FINDINGS & GAPS**

| Aspect | Current State | Issue |
|--------|---------------|-------|
| **Branch specification** | `source_branch` optional field | Not in formal schema validation; resolution logic missing |
| **Default branch detection** | "Detect default branch" stated | NO IMPLEMENTATION SHOWN (gh api call needed) |
| **{branch} placeholder** | Used in git clone commands | NOT RESOLVED — values are assumed but not injected |
| **Brief schema completeness** | Partial | `source_branch` in docs but not in official tables |
| **Tag support** | Exists for version detection | NOT exposed as a branching/targeting mechanism |
| **Quick tier (no clone)** | Uses `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{branch}` | Can target tags via `@tag` syntax theoretically |
| **Update-skill versioning** | Forces default branch | Cannot target specific versions via tags — **breaking change risk** |
| **Version + branch alignment** | Assumed | No validation that they match |

---

## **WHERE TAG SUPPORT COULD BE ADDED**

1. **Brief schema:** Add optional `source_tag` field (parallel to `source_branch`)
2. **Step 01 resolution logic:** Check `source_tag` → use `git clone --depth 1 --branch {source_tag}` (works for annotated tags)
3. **Quick tier:** For `gh api` calls, accept `@tag` in path resolution (e.g., `repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}@{tag}`)
4. **Update-skill:** Store `original_tag` in provenance map; re-clone from same tag (not default branch)
5. **Validation:** Ensure `source_tag` OR `source_branch` (or neither for default) — not both

---

**Bottom line:** The current system **assumes repository default branches are stable** and uses branch-based resolution, but provides **no concrete implementation for default branch detection** and **no tag-based version targeting**. Git tags exist in version detection but aren't used for source cloning.

---

**PARTY MODE — Round 1: The Tag Resolution Problem**

The question: When `target_version` is set, we need to actually clone from that version's git tag. What are the risks, edge cases, and breaking changes?

---

🔄 **Wendy (Workflow Builder):** Let me map the execution paths. There are **four source access modes** that all need version-aware resolution:

1. **Forge/Deep + remote** → `git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch}` — this already accepts tags, so `git clone --branch v0.5.0` works natively
2. **Forge/Deep + local** → reads from disk — if the user has v0.6.0 checked out but wants v0.5.0, we'd need `git checkout v0.5.0` in a temp worktree, which is invasive
3. **Quick tier + remote** → `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}` — defaults to default branch, but accepts `?ref=v0.5.0` query parameter
4. **Quick tier + local** → reads files directly — same problem as local Forge

The safe approach: **only resolve tags for remote sources**. For local paths, warn the user: "You specified target_version {v} but your local source may be at a different version. Consider pointing to the remote URL instead, or checking out the correct version locally." Don't silently operate on mismatched code.

For update-skill, `remote-source-resolution.md` deliberately omits `--branch`. If the original skill was compiled from a tag, the provenance should record that tag so update-skill can re-clone from it.

---

🔬 **Dr. Quinn (Creative Problem Solver):** AHA — the deepest problem is **tag naming conventions are not standardized**. Let me enumerate the variants I've seen:

- `v0.5.0` (most common — Go, many JS/Python projects)
- `0.5.0` (no prefix — some Python, Rust)
- `release/0.5.0` (release branches used as tags)
- `@scope/package@0.5.0` (monorepo per-package tags — npm workspaces, Lerna)
- `python-v0.5.0` or `sdk-v0.5.0` (monorepo per-language tags)

You can't just prepend `v` and hope. The resolution needs a **tag discovery step**:

1. List available tags: `git ls-remote --tags {repo}` or `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/tags`
2. Match `target_version` against tags: try exact match first (`0.5.0`), then `v` prefix (`v0.5.0`), then partial match
3. If multiple matches (monorepo): ask the user to disambiguate
4. If zero matches: warn and fall back to default branch with a clear message

This tag discovery should happen **once** — during the brief creation (step-02 analyze-target) or at extract time (step-03) — not repeatedly.

---

🏗️ **Morgan (Module Builder):** Both valid points. From a module architecture perspective, I want to flag **three impacts beyond create-skill**:

**Impact 1 — Provenance integrity:** If we clone from a tag, the `source_commit` captured by `git rev-parse HEAD` will be the tagged commit, not HEAD. This is correct and desired — but the provenance map and evidence report should record both the tag name AND the commit hash. Currently only the hash is stored.

**Impact 2 — Update-skill version drift:** When update-skill runs later, it clones from default branch (HEAD). If the original skill was from tag `v0.5.0`, the update would detect massive drift because it's comparing v0.5.0 provenance against HEAD code. The update workflow needs to know which tag/ref was used originally so it can re-clone from it — or from a newer tag if the user is upgrading.

**Impact 3 — Quick-skill `@version` syntax:** QS already parses `cognee@0.5.0`. But Quick tier uses `gh api` not `git clone`. The API supports `?ref=tagname` — but we need the same tag discovery logic to resolve `0.5.0` to the actual tag name (which might be `v0.5.0`).

I'd recommend storing `source_ref` (the resolved git ref — tag name or branch) in both the brief and provenance map. This becomes the single source of truth for "what ref was used to build this skill."

---

**Round 1 Summary:**
- **Wendy:** Four access modes need handling; only resolve tags for remote sources; warn on local mismatch
- **Dr. Quinn:** Tag naming varies wildly — need a discovery + matching step, not hardcoded prefixes
- **Morgan:** Store `source_ref` in provenance; update-skill needs to know the original ref; Quick tier needs `?ref=` support

---

Now let me read the update-skill remote resolution and provenance map to verify these impacts, then write the plan.

1	# Remote Source Resolution (Forge/Deep Tier)
2	
3	If `source_root` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
4	
5	If `source_root` (from metadata.json) is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:
6	
7	1. **Check `git` availability:** Verify `git` is functional (`git --version`). If `git` is not available, skip to the fallback warning below.
8	
9	2. **Ephemeral sparse clone:** Clone only the changed files from the change manifest to a system temp path. Note: at this point in the flow, `{source_root}` is known to be a remote URL (the local-path case was already handled above).
10	
11	   ```
12	   temp_path = {system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/
13	   git clone --depth 1 --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_root} {temp_path}
14	   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {changed_files_from_manifest}
15	   ```
16	
17	   **Note:** `--skip-checks` is required because `changed_files_from_manifest` contains individual file paths (e.g., `src/core/parser.py`), not directories. Without this flag, `git sparse-checkout set` rejects non-directory entries.
18	
19	   No `--branch` flag is used — the clone targets the remote's default branch, which must match the branch used during the original [CS] Create Skill run. This scopes the clone to only the files identified in step-02's change manifest, avoiding a full repository download.
20	
21	3. **If clone succeeds:** Update the working source path to `{temp_path}` for all subsequent AST operations in this step. Proceed with the **Forge tier** extraction strategy below. Mark `ephemeral_clone_active = true` for cleanup.
22	
23	4. **If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):**
24	
25	   Warning message: "Ephemeral clone of `{source_root}` failed: {error}. Degrading to source reading (T1-low) for this run. For T1 (AST-verified) confidence, clone the repository locally and re-run [CS] Create Skill with the local path, then re-run this update."
26	
27	   Override the extraction strategy to Quick tier for this run. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.

29	## Ephemeral Clone Cleanup
30	
31	After extraction is complete for all files in scope (whether successful or partially failed), before presenting the extraction summary, if `ephemeral_clone_active`, delete the `{temp_path}` directory. Log: "Ephemeral source clone cleaned up." This ensures cleanup runs even if some extractions failed, as long as the step itself is still executing.
32	
33	## Version Reconciliation
34	
35	After the source path is accessible, check whether the source version has changed since the original skill was created. Look for the version file matching the detected language (e.g., `pyproject.toml`, `package.json`, `Cargo.toml`). If the source version differs from the current `metadata.json` version, record `source_version_detected` in context for step-06 to use when updating `metadata.json`. No warning needed here — step-06 handles the version update.

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Version-Aware Source Resolution (Tag-Based Cloning)

## Context

The `target_version` field was added to the brief schema, but it currently acts as a **label only** — the clone still pulls from the default branch, so extracted API signatures may not match the target version. This makes version-specific skills unreliable.

The fix: when `target_version` is set and the source is remote, resolve it to a git tag and clone from that tag. `git clone --branch` already accepts tag names natively.

**Party mode analysis** identified three critical concerns:
1. **Tag naming varies** (`v0.5.0`, `0.5.0`, `@scope/pkg@0.5.0`) — need discovery + matching, not hardcoded prefixes
2. **Four source access modes** need handling differently (Forge remote, Forge local, Quick remote, Quick local)
3. **Update-skill** needs to know the original ref to re-clone from the same tag

---

## Design

### New field: `source_ref`

A resolved git ref (tag name, branch, or `HEAD`) recorded in:
- **skill-brief.yaml** — `source_ref: "v0.5.0"` (the ref used for cloning)
- **metadata.json** — `source_ref: "v0.5.0"` (persisted for update-skill)
- **provenance-map.json** — already has `source_commit`; `source_ref` adds the human-readable ref

### Tag Resolution Algorithm

When `target_version` is set and source is remote:

1. **List tags:** `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/tags --paginate --jq '.[].name'` (or `git ls-remote --tags {repo}`)
2. **Match** `target_version` against tag names in priority order:
   - Exact match: `0.5.0`
   - With `v` prefix: `v0.5.0`
   - With package scope (for monorepos): `{brief.name}@{target_version}`, `@{scope}/{brief.name}@{target_version}`
3. **If single match:** use it as `{branch}` in `git clone --branch {resolved_tag}`
4. **If multiple matches:** present list to user, ask to disambiguate
5. **If zero matches:** warn: "No git tag found matching version {target_version}. Available tags: {list closest 5}. Falling back to default branch — extracted code may not match target version." Proceed with default branch.

Store the resolved tag (or `HEAD` if fallback) as `source_ref`.

### Per-Access-Mode Behavior

| Mode | target_version set | Behavior |
|------|-------------------|----------|
| **Forge/Deep + remote** | Yes | Resolve tag → `git clone --branch {resolved_tag}` |
| **Forge/Deep + local** | Yes | Warn: "Local source may not match target version {v}. Ensure you've checked out the correct version, or use a remote URL." Proceed with local files. |
| **Quick + remote** | Yes | Resolve tag → append `?ref={resolved_tag}` to all `gh api repos/.../contents/` calls |
| **Quick + local** | Yes | Same warning as Forge local |
| **Any mode** | No | Unchanged behavior (default branch / local files) |

### Update-Skill Integration

`remote-source-resolution.md` currently omits `--branch`. Change:
- Read `source_ref` from existing `metadata.json`
- If `source_ref` is a tag (not `HEAD`): `git clone --branch {source_ref}` — re-clone from same tag
- If user ran update-skill with a new `target_version`: resolve that new tag instead

---

## Files to Modify

### 1. `src/workflows/create-skill/data/source-resolution-protocols.md`

**In the Remote Source Resolution section (top of file):**
- Add a "Tag Resolution" subsection BEFORE the clone command
- When `brief.target_version` is set: run tag resolution algorithm, store result as `source_ref`
- Update clone commands to use `{source_ref}` instead of `{branch}` when resolved
- In "Source Commit Capture" section: also store `source_ref` alongside `source_commit`

**In the Version Reconciliation section:**
- The existing `target_version` override (already added) stays — but now the code being extracted actually matches the target version, so the override is truthful

**For Quick tier remote:** Add `?ref={source_ref}` to `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}` and `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{source_ref}` calls

**For local sources:** Add warning when `target_version` is set but source is local

### 2. `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`

**In the source resolution invocation section:**
- After loading source-resolution-protocols, ensure `source_ref` is available in context for provenance

### 3. `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-artifacts.md`

**In metadata.json generation:**
- Add `source_ref` field to the metadata output

### 4. `src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md`

**In metadata.json template:**
- Add `"source_ref": "{source_ref or null}"` field

### 5. `src/workflows/update-skill/data/remote-source-resolution.md`

**In the clone section (line 13):**
- Read `source_ref` from existing `metadata.json`
- If `source_ref` exists and is not `HEAD`/null: add `--branch {source_ref}` to the clone command
- If user provided a new `target_version`: resolve the new tag and use it instead
- Update the "Note" on line 19 to explain ref-aware cloning

### 6. `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-03-quick-extract.md`

**In the gh api calls:**
- When `target_version` is set: append `?ref={source_ref}` to content API calls
- Add the tag resolution step before extraction begins

### 7. Both `skill-brief-schema.md` files

**In Optional Fields table:**
- Add `source_ref` field: `source_ref | string | Git ref (tag/branch) | Resolved git ref used for source access. Set automatically during tag resolution — do not set manually.`

### 8. `src/knowledge/provenance-tracking.md`

**In provenance-map.json example:**
- Add `"source_ref": "v0.5.0"` alongside `source_commit`

### 9. Docs updates

- `docs/how-it-works.md` — metadata.json example: add `source_ref` field
- `docs/concepts.md` — Version Pinning section: mention tag-based cloning

---

## Files NOT Modified

- `src/workflows/brief-skill/` steps — `target_version` collection already done; tag resolution happens at extract time, not brief time
- `src/workflows/audit-skill/` — reads existing skill, doesn't clone (uses provenance map)
- `src/workflows/export-skill/` — reads from local skill package, no source access

---

## Verification

1. **Remote + target_version:** Run CS with a brief targeting `cognee@0.5.0` against the remote repo. Verify the clone uses the resolved tag (check `source_ref` in metadata.json and `source_commit` matches the tagged commit, not HEAD).
2. **Tag discovery with `v` prefix:** Target version `0.5.0` on a repo that tags as `v0.5.0`. Verify auto-resolution works.
3. **No matching tag:** Target a non-existent version. Verify warning is shown and fallback to default branch occurs.
4. **Quick tier + remote:** Run QS with `@version` targeting. Verify `gh api` calls include `?ref=` parameter.
5. **Local source + target_version:** Verify warning is displayed about potential version mismatch.
6. **Update-skill re-clone:** After creating a skill from tag `v0.5.0`, run US. Verify it re-clones from `v0.5.0` (not default branch).
7. **Metadata persistence:** Verify `source_ref` appears in metadata.json and provenance-map.json.

```

## COMPREHENSIVE SKILL MANAGEMENT OPERATIONS ANALYSIS

Based on my thorough search of `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/`, here's what exists and what's missing for skill management:

### 1. RENAME OPERATIONS

**Status: MECHANISM EXISTS BUT LIMITED TO SOURCE LEVEL**

Found in:
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-02-detect-changes.md` — Detects renamed files in source code (`exports_renamed` count tracked)
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` — Updates provenance map when exports are renamed (line 90: `Update export_name if renamed`)

**CRITICAL CONSTRAINT:**
Per `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/agentskills-spec.md` (line 38):
> `name`: 1-64 characters, lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens, **must match parent directory name**

This means:
- A **skill rename** requires moving `{skills_output_folder}/{old-skill-name}/` → `{new-skill-name}/`
- The inner `{skill-name}/` directory must also be renamed to match the new name
- **All files that reference the skill name must be updated:**
  - `SKILL.md` frontmatter `name` field (line 38 of agentskills-spec)
  - `metadata.json` `name` field
  - `context-snippet.md` references (likely in root paths)
  - `.export-manifest.json` entries (the `skill-name` key)
  - `provenance-map.json` `skill_name` field
  - Platform context files (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, .cursorrules) — managed sections reference old skill directory paths
  - QMD registry entries in forge-tier.yaml (if indexed)

**What's MISSING:**
- NO workflow exists to handle full skill rename (directory move + all metadata updates)
- NO capability in the forger agent menu to trigger a rename operation
- NO automation to rewrite managed sections after skill directory moves

---

### 2. DELETE/DROP OPERATIONS

**Status: NO DIRECT MECHANISM EXISTS — ONLY CLEANUP AFTER MANUAL DELETION**

**Found:**
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-04-update-context.md` (line 148) — Addresses the **consequence** of skill directory deletion:
  - If a skill's directory is deleted but its manifest entry remains, manifest-driven resolution fails
  - Fallback to `active` symlink, then flat-path resolution handles missing entries with warnings
  - **No automatic cleanup of orphaned manifest entries**

- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/setup-forge/steps-c/step-03-auto-index.md` — Detects and cleans **orphaned QMD collections** (Deep tier only):
  - Prompts user to remove orphaned collections not tracked in registry
  - Also removes stale registry entries when collections are missing
  - **This is QMD hygiene, not skill deletion**

- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` (line 99) — Removes provenance entries for **deleted exports** (source-level):
  - Handles deletion of individual functions/classes from source
  - Does NOT handle deletion of entire skills

**What's MISSING:**
- NO workflow to delete a skill and clean its manifest entry atomically
- NO workflow to remove a skill version while preserving other versions
- NO capability to prune archived versions from the manifest
- NO capability to remove a skill from all platform context files (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, .cursorrules)
- NO forger agent menu action for skill deletion

---

### 3. EXPORT MANIFEST MANAGEMENT

**Read `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-04-update-context.md`:**

**What happens when a skill directory is deleted:**

**4a: Export Manifest v2 Schema** (lines 99-127)
- Manifest structure: `exports` → `skill-name` → `active_version` + `versions` array
- Each version tracks: `platforms`, `last_exported`, `status` (`"active"`, `"archived"`, or `"draft"`)
- **If manifest entry exists but skill directory is missing:** Manifest still references it in exported skill set (line 135)
- **No automatic purging of stale entries** — manifest assumes all entries are valid

**4b: Manifest-Driven Snippet Scanning** (lines 139-150)
- Loop through exported skill set from manifest
- For each skill, resolve `active_version` to construct `{skill_package}` path
- **If snippet file missing at versioned path:** Warning issued "Snippet missing for {skill-name} v{active_version} — skipping from managed section"
- Snippet is **silently excluded** from the rebuilt managed section, but **manifest entry is NOT cleaned**

**4c & 4d: Root Path Rewriting** (lines 152-174)
- When assembling managed section for each platform, rewrite root paths if they differ
- **No cleanup of platform-specific paths** — each platform gets its own section

**9b: Manifest Update After Export** (lines 287-316)
- Manifest updated ONLY after successful write to disk
- **Only platforms with successful writes are recorded** in the manifest
- **No operation to remove entries for deleted skills** — manifest is append-only for versions

**Critical Gap:**
> "Can skills be removed from the manifest? **Not explicitly via export-skill.** A skill can be deleted manually, its manifest entry becomes orphaned, and will be skipped during context rebuild (with a warning). The manifest entry persists."

---

### 4. VERSION-AWARE PATHS

**Read `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/version-paths.md`:**

Directory structure hierarchy (lines 54-78):
```
{skills_output_folder}/
  .export-manifest.json
  {skill-name}/
    active -> {version}
    {version}/
      {skill-name}/
        SKILL.md
        metadata.json
        context-snippet.md
        references/
        scripts/
        assets/
```

**A rename operation must update ALL these levels:**
1. Outer `{skill-name}/` directory
2. Inner `{skill-name}/` directory
3. `active` symlink target name
4. SKILL.md frontmatter `name` field
5. `metadata.json` `name` field
6. All manifest entries (key must change)
7. All provenance references
8. All context snippet root paths
9. Platform context file references

---

This constraint is **VERIFIED TWICE** during export (from step-04-update-context.md context). The spec makes rename a **structural requirement** — not optional.

**Read `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/skill-lifecycle.md`:**

  → Architecture Verification (VS/RA) → Maintenance (US/AS) → Verification (TS) → Distribution (EX)
```

## SUMMARY

### ✅ What Exists:
1. **Export renaming** (at source level in update-skill) — tracks renamed exports in provenance
2. **Export deletion** (at source level) — removes provenance entries for deleted functions
3. **Manifest v2 schema** with version tracking and status fields
4. **Orphaned skill handling in export** — warns when snippet is missing, skips from context
5. **QMD collection cleanup** in setup-forge (Deep tier) — prompts to remove orphaned collections
6. **Drift detection** in audit-skill — identifies what changed between skill and source

### ❌ What's Missing:
1. **Skill rename workflow** — no directory move + metadata update automation
2. **Skill delete workflow** — no directory deletion + manifest cleanup + context file update
3. **Version pruning** — no operation to remove archived versions from manifest
4. **Manifest cleanup** — no mechanism to remove orphaned entries when skill directories are deleted
5. **Context file purging** — no automatic removal of skill snippets from CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md/.cursorrules when skills are deleted
6. **Forger menu actions** — no management commands in the agent interface
7. **module-help.csv entries** — no management commands registered
8. **Lifecycle integration** — no management phase in the documented pipeline

### 🎯 Architectural Observations:

1. **Asymmetric Design:** Skills flow **in** (create → export) but cannot flow **out** (no delete/retire path)
2. **Export-as-Gate:** ADR-K establishes export-skill as the sole publishing gate, but there's no corresponding **un-publishing gate** for removals
3. **Manifest as Append-Only:** The manifest records versions but has no mechanism to retire or purge entries
4. **Directory Moves Are Expensive:** Rename requires updating >9 different locations; no atomic operation exists
5. **Orphan Tolerance:** The system tolerates orphaned manifest entries and warns during export, but doesn't automate cleanup
```

**PARTY MODE ACTIVATED!**

Welcome Armel! The question: **Should Ferris support renaming skills (e.g., `cognee` → `cognee-skf-community`) and dropping specific skill versions (e.g., drop `cognee 0.1.0` because deprecated)?**

Current state: SKF has **zero management operations**. Skills flow in (create → export) but cannot flow out. The manifest is append-only. Let's discuss.

---

**Round 1: Is this a good idea? What are the risks?**

---

🏗️ **Morgan (Module Builder):** Absolutely yes — this is a lifecycle gap. We have 12 workflows covering creation through distribution, but **no management phase**. That's like building a package manager with `install` but no `uninstall`. Two distinct operations here:

**Rename (RS - Rename Skill):** This is surgically complex. The agentskills.io spec requires `name` to match parent directory name, so a rename is really a **coordinated move** across 9+ locations: outer directory, inner version directories, SKILL.md frontmatter, metadata.json, provenance-map.json, export manifest key, context snippets, platform files, and QMD collections. I'd model this as a new workflow — not an ad-hoc script. The risk is partial renames leaving inconsistent state.

**Drop (DS - Drop Skill):** Simpler but with a critical decision: drop a **specific version** vs drop the **entire skill**. Dropping v0.1.0 while keeping v0.5.0 means removing one version directory and updating the manifest. Dropping the whole skill means removing the skill_group directory, purging from manifest, and rebuilding managed sections in all platform context files. Both are needed.

I'd propose two new workflows: **RS** (Rename Skill) and **DS** (Drop Skill), bringing the total to 14.

---

🔄 **Wendy (Workflow Builder):** Morgan's right on the scope, but let me stress the **atomicity problem**. A rename that fails halfway leaves a skill in a broken state — SKILL.md says `cognee-skf-community` but the directory is still `cognee/`. Same with drops — if you delete the directory but the manifest update fails, export-skill will warn about a missing snippet on every run.

My recommendation: **implement both as transactional operations with rollback**:

1. **Rename flow:** Copy to new name → verify all references → update manifest → rebuild context files → delete old directory. If any step fails, the old skill is still intact.
2. **Drop flow:** Remove from manifest FIRST → rebuild context files (removes snippet) → then delete files. If file deletion fails, the skill is already unpublished — no orphan in CLAUDE.md.

For the forger menu, I'd suggest piggybacking on existing trigger patterns:
- `RS` — Rename Skill (asks: which skill? new name?)
- `DS` — Drop Skill (asks: which skill? which version, or all?)

Both should require explicit confirmation — these are destructive operations.

---

🔬 **Dr. Quinn (Creative Problem Solver):** Let me play devil's advocate on **rename**. How often does someone actually need to rename a skill? The name is derived from the library name during briefing. The example `cognee` → `cognee-skf-community` suggests a **naming convention** problem, not a rename need. If we had better defaults — like auto-prefixing community skills with `community-` or suffixing with the authority — the rename need disappears.

That said, I see two legitimate rename scenarios:
1. **Quick-skill used `repo_name`, user wants `skill-name`** — QS names from the repo, CS names from the brief. If you start with QS and later want to formalize, you'd need a rename.
2. **Namespace collision** — two libraries with the same name in different ecosystems.

For **drop**, I'm fully convinced. Version accumulation is a real problem. If you've compiled 5 versions of a library, the oldest ones are just disk waste and confusion. A `DS cognee 0.1.0` that surgically removes one version directory and its forge-data, updates the manifest, and doesn't touch the active version — that's clean and useful.

One edge case to flag: **what if the user tries to drop the active version?** The workflow must either refuse ("Cannot drop the active version — switch active first") or auto-promote the next most recent version.

---

⚡ **Victor (Innovation Strategist):** Let me ask the strategic question nobody's raised: **what's the distribution impact?**

If someone publishes a skill to the agentskills.io ecosystem (`npx skills publish cognee`), then renames locally to `cognee-skf-community`, the published skill and the local skill diverge. The ecosystem has `cognee`, the local has `cognee-skf-community`. Update-skill can't reconcile. This isn't a bug — it's a **design decision** about whether names are permanent identifiers or mutable labels.

My recommendation: treat **rename as a local-only operation** with a clear warning that published skills cannot be renamed in the registry. And for **drop**, consider adding a `status: "deprecated"` to the manifest version entry rather than hard-deleting. Deprecated versions stop appearing in context files but their artifacts stay on disk for audit/rollback. Hard delete is a separate flag (`DS cognee 0.1.0 --purge`).

This gives you a **soft drop** (deprecate — reversible) and a **hard drop** (purge — destructive). Most of the time, soft drop is what people actually want.

---

**Round 1 Summary:**

| Agent | Rename | Drop |
|-------|--------|------|
| **Morgan** | Yes — new RS workflow, 9+ locations to update | Yes — new DS workflow, version-level and skill-level |
| **Wendy** | Yes — must be transactional (copy-verify-delete) | Yes — manifest-first removal order for safety |
| **Dr. Quinn** | Maybe — consider better naming defaults first | Yes — version pruning is clearly needed. Watch the active-version edge case |
| **Victor** | Yes but local-only — warn about registry divergence | Soft drop (deprecate) by default, hard delete with --purge flag |

**Consensus:** Drop is unanimously supported. Rename is supported but with caveats about complexity and scope. Victor's soft/hard drop distinction and Dr. Quinn's active-version guard are important design points.

What direction do you want to take this, Armel? Or should I bring in different perspectives?

[E] Exit Party Mode - End the collaborative session

> DEVELOPER

we should take the best decision for a long term, robust and stable support. Let plan the implementation when ready

> AGENT

Strong input from the team. Let me synthesize the best long-term decisions and write the plan.

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Skill Management Operations — Rename (RS) and Drop (DS)

## Context

SKF has 12 workflows covering creation through distribution but **no management phase**. Skills flow in but cannot flow out. The manifest is append-only. As the version-aware directory structure accumulates versions, users need to rename skills (e.g., after graduating from QS to a formal name) and drop deprecated versions (disk waste, confusion in export listings).

**Party mode consensus:**
- **Drop:** Unanimously needed. Two modes: soft drop (deprecate — reversible, keeps files) and hard drop (purge — deletes files). Active-version guard required.
- **Rename:** Needed but complex (9+ locations). Must be transactional (copy-verify-delete). Local-only with registry divergence warning.
- Both operations update the manifest atomically and rebuild platform context files.

---

## Design Decisions

### 1. Soft Drop vs Hard Drop
- **Soft drop (default):** Sets version status to `"deprecated"` in manifest. Removes snippet from platform context files on next export. Files stay on disk for audit/rollback.
- **Hard drop (`--purge`):** Same as soft drop + deletes the version directory from skills_output_folder and forge_data_folder.
- **Rationale:** Most drops are "stop using this version," not "erase all evidence." Soft drop is safe and reversible. Hard drop is available when disk space matters.

### 2. Active Version Guard
- Cannot drop the active version unless it's the only version (dropping the entire skill).
- If dropping a non-active version: proceed normally.
- If dropping the active version AND other versions exist: refuse with "Cannot drop the active version. Switch active to another version first, or drop all versions with `--all`."
- If dropping all versions: removes the entire skill_group directory and manifest entry.

### 3. Rename as Transactional Copy-Verify-Delete
- Copy skill_group to new name → verify all internal references → update manifest → rebuild context files → delete old directory.
- If any step fails, old skill remains intact. No partial renames.
- Rename applies to ALL versions of a skill (the entire skill_group).
- Local-only: warn if `source_authority: "official"` that published registry skill won't be renamed.

### 4. New Manifest Statuses
Current: `"active"`, `"archived"`, `"draft"`
Add: `"deprecated"` — version exists on disk but excluded from all export operations. Export-skill treats deprecated versions like they don't exist.

---

## New Workflows

### RS — Rename Skill (3 steps)

`src/workflows/rename-skill/`

**workflow.md** — Entry point, mode routing, step sequence.

**step-01-select.md** — Load target:
1. Ask user which skill to rename (list available skills from manifest + skill_group scan)
2. Ask for the new name (validate: kebab-case, no collision with existing skill names)
3. Warn if `source_authority: "official"` — "This skill may be published. Renaming locally will diverge from the registry."
4. Confirm: "Rename `{old-name}` → `{new-name}` (all {n} versions)? [Y/N]"

**step-02-execute.md** — Transactional rename:
1. **Copy** `{skill_group_old}` → `{skill_group_new}` (entire directory tree)
2. **Rename inner directories:** For each `{version}/{old-name}/` → `{version}/{new-name}/`
3. **Update file contents** in new location:
   - `SKILL.md` frontmatter `name:` field
   - `metadata.json` `name` field
   - `context-snippet.md` — rewrite root paths and `[{name} v...]` display name
   - `provenance-map.json` `skill_name` field
4. **Update `active` symlink** — recreate pointing to correct version
5. **Update forge_data:** Copy `{forge_group_old}` → `{forge_group_new}`
6. **Update export manifest:** Remove old key, add new key with same version data
7. **Rebuild platform context files:** Run managed section regeneration (same logic as export-skill step-04) to replace old skill references
8. **Verify:** Check that `{skill_package_new}` has correct name in all files
9. **Delete old:** Remove `{skill_group_old}` and `{forge_group_old}`
10. If any step fails: delete new directories, report error, old skill unchanged

**step-03-report.md** — Summary:
- Files moved, references updated, manifest updated
- Context files rebuilt (list platforms)
- QMD collection note: "If QMD collections were registered for the old name, re-run `@Ferris SF` to update."

### DS — Drop Skill (3 steps)

`src/workflows/drop-skill/`

**workflow.md** — Entry point, mode routing, step sequence.

**step-01-select.md** — Load target:
1. Ask which skill to drop (list from manifest + directory scan)
2. Show available versions with their status
3. Ask: specific version(s) or all?
   - If specific version: validate it exists
   - If all: confirm this removes the entire skill
4. Ask: deprecate (keep files) or purge (delete files)?
5. **Active version guard:** If user selected the active version AND other versions exist → refuse. Prompt to switch active first or drop all.
6. Confirm: "Drop `{skill-name}` v{version} ({deprecate|purge})? [Y/N]"

**step-02-execute.md** — Execute drop:

**Version-level drop:**
1. **Update manifest:** Set version status to `"deprecated"` (or remove entry if purge)
2. **If dropped version was active:** Update `active_version` to next most recent non-deprecated version (only reachable if dropping all)
3. **Rebuild platform context files:** Regenerate managed sections — deprecated versions are excluded from snippet scanning
4. **If purge:** Delete `{skill_package}` directory and `{forge_version}` directory
5. **Update `active` symlink** if it pointed to the dropped version

**Skill-level drop (all versions):**
1. **Remove skill from manifest** entirely
2. **Rebuild platform context files:** Regenerate without this skill
3. **If purge:** Delete entire `{skill_group}` and `{forge_group}` directories
4. **If deprecate (no purge):** Set all versions to `"deprecated"`, keep files

**step-03-report.md** — Summary:
- Version(s) dropped, status changes
- Context files rebuilt
- Disk space freed (if purge)
- Remaining versions listed

---

## Verification

1. **Soft drop version:** Run DS on a skill with multiple versions, deprecate one. Run EX — verify deprecated version absent from CLAUDE.md. Verify files still on disk.
2. **Hard drop version:** Run DS with purge. Verify directory deleted, manifest entry removed.
3. **Drop active version guard:** Try to drop the active version when other versions exist — verify refusal.
4. **Drop all versions:** Run DS --all on a skill. Verify manifest entry removed, context files rebuilt.
5. **Rename:** Run RS on a skill. Verify new name in SKILL.md, metadata.json, manifest. Verify old directory gone. Verify context files reference new name.
6. **Rename rollback:** Simulate a failure mid-rename. Verify old skill unchanged.
7. **Test suite:** Verify menu count updated, workflow files exist.

1	---
2	name: audit-skill
3	description: "Drift detection. Forge tier: structural. Deep tier: full."
4	web_bundle: true
5	installed_path: '{project-root}/_bmad/skf/workflows/skillforge/audit-skill'
6	---
7	
8	# Audit Skill
9	
10	**Goal:** Detect drift between an existing skill and its current source code, producing a severity-graded drift report with AST-backed findings and actionable remediation suggestions.
11	
12	**Your Role:** In addition to your name, communication_style, and persona, you are also a skill auditor operating in Ferris Audit mode. This is a deterministic analysis workflow — every finding must trace to actual code with file:line citations. You enforce the zero-hallucination principle: structural truth over semantic guessing. You bring AST analysis expertise and drift detection methodology, while the source code provides the ground truth.
13	
14	---
15	
16	## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
17	
18	This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution:
19	
20	### Core Principles
21	
22	- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is a self-contained instruction file that is a part of an overall workflow that must be followed exactly
23	- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only the current step file is in memory — never load future step files until told to do so
24	- **Sequential Enforcement**: Sequence within the step files must be completed in order, no skipping or optimization allowed
25	- **State Tracking**: Document progress in output file frontmatter using `stepsCompleted` array
26	- **Append-Only Building**: Build the drift report by appending sections as directed to the output file
27	- **Tier-Aware Execution**: Adapt analysis depth based on detected forge tier (Quick/Forge/Forge+/Deep) with graceful degradation
28	
29	### Step Processing Rules
30	
31	1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Always read the entire step file before taking any action
32	2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute all numbered sections in order, never deviate
33	3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: If a menu is presented, halt and wait for user selection
34	4. **CHECK CONTINUATION**: If the step has a menu with Continue as an option, only proceed to next step when user selects 'C' (Continue)
35	5. **SAVE STATE**: Update `stepsCompleted` in frontmatter before loading next step
36	6. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, load, read entire file, then execute the next step file
37	
38	### Critical Rules (NO EXCEPTIONS)
39	
40	- 🛑 **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
41	- 📖 **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
42	- 🚫 **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
43	- 💾 **ALWAYS** update frontmatter of output files when writing the final output for a specific step
44	- 🎯 **ALWAYS** follow the exact instructions in the step file
45	- ⏸️ **ALWAYS** halt at menus and wait for user input
46	- 📋 **NEVER** create mental todo lists from future steps
47	- ⚙️ **TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK**: If any instruction references a subprocess, subagent, or tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread
48	- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
49	
50	---
51	
52	## INITIALIZATION SEQUENCE
53	
54	### 1. Module Configuration Loading
55	
56	Load and read full config from {project-root}/_bmad/skf/config.yaml and resolve:
57	
58	- `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`, `communication_language`, `document_output_language`
59	- `skills_output_folder`, `forge_data_folder`, `sidecar_path`
60	- Generate and store `timestamp` as the current date-time in `YYYYMMDD-HHmmss` format. This value is fixed for the entire workflow run and must not be regenerated in subsequent steps.
61	
62	### 2. First Step Execution
63	
64	Load, read the full file and then execute `./steps-c/step-01-init.md` to begin the workflow.
65

```
120	**v1 manifest** (no `schema_version` field — migrate in-place to v2):
121	1. For each entry in `exports`, read its `platforms` and `last_exported`
122	2. Resolve the skill's current version from `{resolved_skill_package}/metadata.json`
123	3. Wrap in v2 structure: set `active_version` to the resolved version, create a single entry in `versions` with `status: "active"`, the original `platforms`, and `last_exported`
124	4. Set `schema_version: "2"` at root
125	5. Hold the migrated structure in context (it will be written in section 9b)
126	
127	**If the file does not exist** (first export or migration): Treat as empty — only the current export target will appear in the rebuilt index.
128	
129	#### 4b. Build Exported Skill Set (version-aware)
130	
131	Determine the set of skills to include in the rebuilt index:
132	
133	1. Start with all skill names listed in the manifest's `exports` object (if manifest exists)
134	2. For each skill, record its `active_version` from the manifest (v2 schema)
135	3. Add the current export target skill name (ensures it is always included even before manifest is written) — use the version from `{resolved_skill_package}/metadata.json` as its `active_version`
136	4. This is the **exported skill set** — each entry has a skill name and its resolved `active_version`
137	
138	#### 4c. Resolve and Filter Snippets (manifest-driven — replaces glob scan)
139	
140	Instead of globbing `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md`, resolve snippets from the exported skill set built in 4b:
141	
142	**For each skill in the exported skill set:**
143	1. Resolve `{skill_package}` using the skill's `active_version`: `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/{active_version}/{skill-name}/`
144	2. Read `{skill_package}/context-snippet.md`
145	3. **If snippet exists:** Add to skill index
146	4. **If snippet does not exist at the versioned path:** Check for `active` symlink at `{skills_output_folder}/{skill-name}/active/{skill-name}/context-snippet.md`. If still not found, skip with warning: "Snippet missing for {skill-name} v{active_version} — skipping from managed section"
147	
148	**Skills NOT in the exported skill set are never scanned** — they have not been through export-skill and must not appear in the managed section (ADR-K).
149	
150	**If no snippets pass the filter:** Generate managed section with zero skills — header only, no skill entries.
151	
152	#### 4d. Rewrite Root Paths for Target Platform
153	
154	The context-snippet.md files on disk contain root paths for the platform they were originally exported to. When assembling the managed section for the current target platform, rewrite root paths if they differ:
155	
156	**Platform root path mapping:**
157	
158	| Platform | Root Path Prefix |
159	|----------|-----------------|

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/version-paths.md",
  "old_string": "**Only one version per skill can have `status: \"active\"` at any time.**",
  "new_string": "**Only one version per skill can have `status: \"active\"` at any time.**\n\n## Skill Management Operations\n\nTwo workflows operate on the version-aware structure for skill lifecycle management:\n\n### Rename (RS - Rename Skill)\n\nRenames a skill across all versions. Because the agentskills.io spec requires `name` to match parent directory name, a rename is a coordinated move across:\n1. Outer `{skill_group}` directory: `{skills_output_folder}/{old-name}/` → `{skills_output_folder}/{new-name}/`\n2. Inner `{skill-name}/` directories inside each version: `{version}/{old-name}/` → `{version}/{new-name}/`\n3. `SKILL.md` frontmatter `name:` field (in every version)\n4. `metadata.json` `name` field (in every version)\n5. `context-snippet.md` root paths and display name (in every version)\n6. `provenance-map.json` `skill_name` field (in every version under `{forge_group}`)\n7. `{forge_group}` directory: `{forge_data_folder}/{old-name}/` → `{forge_data_folder}/{new-name}/`\n8. Export manifest: remove old key, add new key with same version data\n9. Platform context files (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, .cursorrules): rebuild managed sections\n\nRename is transactional — copy-verify-delete pattern. If any step fails, old skill remains intact. See `workflows/rename-skill/`.\n\n### Drop (DS - Drop Skill)\n\nDrops a specific version or the entire skill with two modes:\n\n**Soft drop (default):** Sets version status to `\"deprecated\"` in the export manifest. Files remain on disk. Export-skill excludes deprecated versions from all platform context files. Reversible by manually editing manifest back to `\"active\"`/`\"archived\"`.\n\n**Hard drop (`--purge`):** Same as soft drop, plus deletes the version directory (`{skill_package}`) and forge data directory (`{forge_version}`). Irreversible.\n\n**Active version guard:** Cannot drop the active version when other versions exist. The user must either switch active to another version first, or drop all versions at once.\n\n**Skill-level drop:** Removes the entire `{skill_group}` from the manifest. If purge, also deletes `{skill_group}` and `{forge_group}` directories.\n\nSee `workflows/drop-skill/`."
}
```

  "old_string": "### Maintenance\n\n```\nAS → US → TS → EX\n```\n\n1. **AS** detects drift between skill and current source\n2. **US** re-extracts changed exports and merges, preserving \\[MANUAL\\] sections\n3. **TS** re-verifies the updated skill\n4. **EX** re-exports the updated package",
  "new_string": "### Maintenance\n\n```\nAS → US → TS → EX\n```\n\n1. **AS** detects drift between skill and current source\n2. **US** re-extracts changed exports and merges, preserving \\[MANUAL\\] sections\n3. **TS** re-verifies the updated skill\n4. **EX** re-exports the updated package\n\n### Management\n\n```\nRS or DS → EX\n```\n\n1. **RS** renames a skill across all versions (transactional copy-verify-delete), OR\n2. **DS** drops a specific version (soft — deprecate, or hard — purge) or the entire skill\n3. **EX** rebuilds platform context files (CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md/.cursorrules) to reflect the management operation — deprecated versions are excluded, renamed skills appear under their new name"
}

Keep files on disk.\n- **Hard drop (`--purge`):** Set status to `\"deprecated\"` + delete `{skill_package}` and `{forge_version}` directories.\n- **Active version guard:** Cannot drop active version when other non-deprecated versions exist. Must drop all or switch active first.\n- **Skill-level drop (all versions):** Remove entire skill entry from manifest. Resolve `skills_output_folder`, `forge_data_folder`, `sidecar_path`, `communication_language`.\n- First step: `./steps-c/step-01-select.md`\n\n#### `steps-c/step-01-select.md`\nFrontmatter:\n```\n---\nname: 'step-01-select'\ndescription: 'Select skill, version(s), and drop mode (deprecate/purge)'\nnextStepFile: './step-02-execute.md'\nversionPathsKnowledge: '../../../knowledge/version-paths.md'\n---\n```\n\nMandatory execution rules (follow audit-skill template style):\n- Read complete step file before action\n- Speak in `{communication_language}`\n- Role: You are Ferris in Management mode — destructive operation specialist. If missing or empty, halt: \"No skills to drop. Manifest is empty or missing.\"\n3. **List available skills:** Display all skill names from manifest with their version summary:\n   ```\n   Available skills:\n   1. cognee\n      - 0.1.0 (deprecated)\n      - 0.5.0 (archived)\n      - 0.6.0 (active) *\n   2. express\n      - 4.18.0 (active) *\n   ```\n   The `*` marks the active version. Also scan `{skills_output_folder}/` for any skill directories not in the manifest and note them as \"(not in manifest)\".\n4. **Ask which skill:** \"Which skill would you like to drop?\" Accept name or number.\n5. **Display version details:** Show the selected skill's versions with status, last_exported, and platforms.\n6. **Ask scope:** \"Drop which version(s)?\"\n   - `[N] Specific version` — ask which one\n   - `[A] All versions (drops entire skill)`\n7. **If specific version selected:**\n   - Validate it exists in the manifest\n   - **Active version guard:** If this version's status is `\"active\"` AND other non-deprecated versions exist → refuse with: \"Cannot drop the active version `{version}`. Either: (a) switch active to another version first by re-running [EX] Export Skill with a different version, or (b) drop all versions with the 'All versions' option.\"\n8. **Ask mode:** \"How should this be dropped?\"\n   - `[D] Deprecate (soft)` — Keep files on disk, mark as deprecated in manifest. Reversible by editing manifest.\n   - `[P] Purge (hard)` — Delete files from disk. All four files for the drop-skill workflow have been created.

## Files Created

- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/drop-skill/workflow.md` — Workflow entry point. Sets name, description, web_bundle, installed_path, goal, Ferris Management-mode role, and the initialization sequence that loads config and enters `./steps-c/step-01-select.md`.

- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/drop-skill/steps-c/step-01-select.md` — Selection + confirmation gate. Loads version-paths knowledge, reads the export manifest (halts if empty), lists all skills with version/status, scans for directories not in the manifest, prompts for skill → scope (specific/all) → mode (deprecate/purge), enforces the active version guard (refuses to drop an active version when other non-deprecated versions exist), computes `affected_directories` from the `{skill_package}` / `{skill_group}` / `{forge_version}` / `{forge_group}` templates, and presents a Y/N confirmation summary before storing decisions and loading step-02.

- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/drop-skill/steps-c/step-02-execute.md` — Execution step. Re-reads version-paths and managed-section-format, mutates the manifest (sets `status: "deprecated"` on version-level drops, removes the whole `exports.{skill}` entry on skill-level drops), rebuilds the managed section in CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md/.cursorrules for every IDE in config using the same logic as export-skill step-04 sections 4b–5 (excluding deprecated versions, per-platform root path rewrite, alphabetical sort), surgically replaces content only between `<!-- SKF:BEGIN -->` and `<!-- SKF:END -->`, and in purge mode deletes each path in `affected_directories` (with an in-scope check against `{skills_output_folder}` / `{forge_data_folder}`), repoints/removes the `active` symlink when needed, computes disk freed, verifies final state, and records per-stage errors with recovery guidance.

- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/drop-skill/steps-c/step-03-report.md` — Final report (no nextStepFile). Re-reads the updated manifest to render remaining versions for the skill (or "(skill fully removed)" for skill-level drops), reports manifest status, platform files rebuilt (and failed), files deleted and disk freed in purge mode, the reversibility note for soft drops, and surfaces any verification warnings.

## Key Design Points Honored

- Soft drop marks `status: "deprecated"` and retains files; hard drop also deletes `{skill_package}` + `{forge_version}` (version-level) or `{skill_group}` + `{forge_group}` (skill-level).
- Active version guard refuses version-level drops of the active version whenever other non-deprecated versions remain, with clear user guidance on the two valid paths forward.
- All paths use the `{skill_package}` / `{skill_group}` / `{forge_version}` / `{forge_group}` templates from `knowledge/version-paths.md` — no hardcoded paths.
- Context rebuild reuses export-skill's managed-section logic (sections 4b–5) and filters deprecated versions so they disappear from all platform files immediately.
- Every destructive action traces to an explicit step-01 confirmation; step-02 never re-prompts or widens scope.
- Frontmatter, rule style (Universal/Role/Step-Specific), execution protocols, context boundaries, and Success/Failure metrics all follow the audit-skill template format.

Created the rename-skill (RS) workflow at `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/rename-skill/`.

## Files created

- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/rename-skill/workflow.md` — workflow entry point (Ferris Management mode, transactional copy-verify-delete safety framing, initialization sequence, first step = `./steps-c/step-01-select.md`)
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/rename-skill/steps-c/step-01-select.md` — selection + validation (knowledge load, manifest read, list skills, old-name selection, new-name validation with 4 gates: kebab-case regex `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9]$`, 1-64 length, identity, collision), `source_authority: "official"` warning, affected-version enumeration from manifest ∪ on-disk, confirmation gate, context handoff)
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/rename-skill/steps-c/step-02-execute.md` — transactional execution in 10 sections (re-read knowledge → copy both groups → rename inner dirs → update SKILL.md/metadata.json/context-snippet.md/provenance-map.json → fix `active` symlink → grep verify → re-key manifest with backup → rebuild platform context files via export-skill step-04 logic → delete old — point of no return → store results). Every section before section 8 has an explicit rollback procedure; section 7 is best-effort per-platform; section 8 failures are recorded for manual cleanup. Error handling summary table included.
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/rename-skill/steps-c/step-03-report.md` — final report renderer surfacing file-level counts, manifest re-key, platform rebuild status, all warning categories (section2/section3/verification), and post-commit deletion errors, plus the next-steps block (re-run EX, re-register QMD collections, note that agentskills.io registry is unchanged)

## Key design notes

- **Transactional safety** is the load-bearing theme in all four files: copy-before-delete is called out in the workflow header, the step-01 confirmation gate, and every section of step-02.
- Kebab-case regex allows the single-character edge case via a secondary `^[a-z0-9]$` rule noted in the user prompt.
- Collision check covers manifest `exports`, `{skills_output_folder}/`, and `{forge_data_folder}/` — a collision in any of the three halts validation.
- Verification in step-02 section 5 distinguishes structural references (hard failure → rollback) from SKILL.md body-text mentions (recorded as `verification_warnings`, non-blocking) so legitimate historical/changelog prose doesn't block a rename.
- Section 7 platform rebuild failures do NOT trigger rollback — the manifest and filesystem are canonical by that point; context files are derived and can be regenerated via `[EX] Export Skill`.
- Frontmatter fields match the drop-skill sibling exactly (`nextStepFile`, `versionPathsKnowledge`, `managedSectionLogic` reuse).
- All paths use the `{skill_package}`, `{skill_group}`, `{forge_version}`, `{forge_group}` templates from `knowledge/version-paths.md` — no hardcoded paths.
- Success/Failure metrics block included at the end of every step file in the same format as drop-skill.

```
1	# SKF Workflows
2	
3	Twelve workflows covering the full skill lifecycle — from source discovery through verified export, with pre-code architecture verification.
4	
5	All workflows are triggered via Ferris (`@Ferris <code>`) and execute as step-by-step sequences with just-in-time step loading.
6	
7	## Workflow Index
8	
9	| Code | Workflow | Steps | Mode | Purpose |
10	| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
11	| SF | [setup-forge](setup-forge/workflow.md) | 5 | Architect | Initialize environment, detect tools, set capability tier |
12	| AN | [analyze-source](analyze-source/workflow.md) | 7 | Architect | Discover skillable units in a repo, produce recommended briefs |
13	| BS | [brief-skill](brief-skill/workflow.md) | 5 | Architect | Design a skill scope through guided discovery |
14	| CS | [create-skill](create-skill/workflow.md) | 8 | Architect | Compile a skill from brief with provenance (supports --batch) |
15	| QS | [quick-skill](quick-skill/workflow.md) | 6 | Architect | Fast skill from package name or GitHub URL — no brief needed |
16	| SS | [create-stack-skill](create-stack-skill/workflow.md) | 9 | Architect | Consolidated project stack skill with integration patterns (code-mode + compose-mode) |
17	| VS | [verify-stack](verify-stack/workflow.md) | 6 | Audit | Validate tech stack feasibility against architecture and PRD |
18	| RA | [refine-architecture](refine-architecture/workflow.md) | 6 | Architect | Improve architecture doc using verified skill data |
19	| US | [update-skill](update-skill/workflow.md) | 7 | Surgeon | Regenerate after source changes, preserving \[MANUAL\] sections |
20	| AS | [audit-skill](audit-skill/workflow.md) | 6 | Audit | Drift detection between skill and current source code |
21	| TS | [test-skill](test-skill/workflow.md) | 7 | Audit | Cognitive completeness verification — quality gate before export |
22	| EX | [export-skill](export-skill/workflow.md) | 6 | Delivery | Package for distribution, inject into CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md |
23	
24	## Typical Flows
25	
26	**First-time setup:**
27	
28	```
29	SF → AN → CS (batch) → TS → EX
30	```
31	
32	**Single skill (full quality):**
33	
34	```
35	SF → BS → CS → TS → EX
36	```
37	
38	**Single skill (fast):**
39	
40	```
41	SF → QS → EX
42	```
43	
44	**Pre-code stack verification:**
45	
46	```
47	SF → CS×N (per library) → VS → RA → SS (compose) → TS → EX
48	```
49	
50	**Maintenance:**
51	
52	```
53	AS → US → TS → EX
54	```
55	
56	## Knowledge vs. Data
57	
58	The module uses two kinds of reference material, loaded just-in-time during workflow execution:
59	
60	| Aspect | Knowledge (`knowledge/`) | Data (`{workflow}/data/`) |
```
